The defensible, durable contribution is narrower and better: a *boundary result* showing that on the contact/force regime where an inverse-dynamics constraint exists, a single-quantity physics reward out-transfers a learned reward on real-hardware contact shift — with the regime boundary (H5) as the headline, not the caveat.

**(2) Five key findings, ranked by reliability.**
1. *The sensor-free inverse-dynamics-constrained GRF estimator is real, strong, and deployable on its own (R²=0.99/0.99, RMSE ~14 N, real-hardware cross-footwear).* Supported by all five; challenged by none. Most reliable — it is a measured hardware result, not a transfer claim.
2. *The strong claim must be scoped to the contact/force regime (H5).* Supported by Academic, Skeptic, Historian, and the card itself; challenged by no one. Highly reliable — it is a concession every lens converges on.
3. *The card's H1 foil (Video-Language Critic) is mis-matched; the real competitor is a dynamics-invariant-by-construction learned reward.* Supported by Academic + Skeptic, with ConTraIRL 2606.03017 / TRIRL as dated evidence; the card implicitly challenges it by pre-registering FLORA as "the sharpest learned-side competitor." Reliable and the most decision-relevant.
4. *"Distribution-free" is overstated — the PINN is distribution-bound with a wider envelope (H4 trust region).* Supported by Skeptic + Academic + Historian; challenged by the card's First-principles framing. Moderately reliable — partly semantic, but a referee-killing framing risk.
5. *Reward-transfer leaves the expensive on-hardware optimization loop uncosted; action-transfer (VIRAL) may win on deployment wall-time.* Supported by Practitioner + Economist; challenged by the card's "reward not policy" premise (Risk 2 partially concedes). Plausible but untested — depends on the real-rollout count, which nobody has measured.

**(3) Hidden cross-perspective connection.** The Economist's "small-lab data-light bet vs compute-rich action-transfer bet" and the Historian's "structured-prior wave vs scaled-learned wave" are the *same* fault line viewed through money vs time. Both predict the identical resolution: the law-grounded reward survives precisely where scaling a learned reward is *uneconomical* — the narrow GRF/contact-wrench regime where 1M-trajectory data engines (Robometer) have no leverage. That convergence is what makes H5 (the regime boundary) not a weakness but the load-bearing thesis: the contribution is durable exactly on the subset the scaling camp can't cheaply buy.

**(4) Actionable insight — DE-RISK (do not commit blind, do not drop).** The anchor and the wedge are real; the framing is fragile. De-risk in three concrete moves before committing: (a) **Re-target H1's baseline** from Video-Language Critic to a dynamics-invariant-by-construction learned reward (re-implement [[2605.11020|TRIRL]] or [[2605.22123|FLORA]]'s flow-abstraction reward on the *same* footwear/terrain task) — this is the card's own FP falsifier, run against the strong foil. (b) **Demote "distribution-free" to "wider-envelope-on-contact-regime"** and make H5's boundary the headline. (c) **Add the missing optimization-safety experiment**: confirm the frozen GRF reward, used as a sole objective, does not collapse to a degenerate low-impact gait (the blind spot). *Concrete next experiment*: one contact task (quiet locomotion), three rewards held to a matched base RL — QuietWalk PINN-force vs a re-implemented dynamics-invariant learned reward (TRIRL-style) vs a VIRAL-style DR action policy — evaluated for cross-footwear/terrain SR retention by shift severity *on real hardware*, with a reward-hacking probe (does the PINN reward induce a degenerate gait?). If the PINN reward beats the *strong* learned baseline under the high-heels contact shift, commit; if it only beats VLC, the contribution is "reward not policy" (already consensus), not "law-grounded," and the bet collapses to a boundary paper.

**(5) Frontier question.** Is there a measurable *physical-constraint-strength* axis — how tightly an inverse-dynamics or conservation law pins the reward to state — that *predicts* a learned reward's OOD drift, so one could compute in advance which tasks fall inside the "physics-grounding wins" regime (H5's boundary) rather than discovering it empirically per task? If that axis exists, A2 becomes a theory of *when* to ground a reward in physics versus learn it, which is the paradigm-level result the current card only gestures at.

---

#### Prompt 4 — Peer Review (independent)

*Reviewer stance: skeptical Stanford robotics faculty. Grounded in a full read of the A2 card (Sim2Real.md lines 186–258) plus vault checks of the cited competitor papers.*

---

##### 1. Confidence scores (1–10, reliability)

| # | Finding | Score | One-line justification |
|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | Sensor-free inverse-dynamics GRF estimator is real, strong, deployable on its own (R²=0.99/0.99, RMSE ~14 N, cross-footwear, real hardware) | **9** | This is a measured hardware result reported in the anchor paper, not a transfer claim. The one deduction: the RMSE ~14 N figure is not in the card body and is unverified against the source here; the R² and −7.17 dBA numbers are card-attested. |
| 2 | The strong claim must be scoped to the contact/force regime (H5) | **9** | The card *already* makes this its H5 and Risk-1; scoping a physics-law reward to tasks where an inverse-dynamics/conservation constraint exists is near-tautological and uncontested. High reliability, but note it is the card's own position, not an external correction. |
| 3 | The H1 foil (Video-Language Critic) is mis-matched; the real competitor is a dynamics-invariant-by-construction learned reward | **5** | The *direction* is right but the briefing materially overstates the gap. The card's Related table already contains TRIRL (2605.11020, "dynamics-disentangled by construction"), FLORA (2605.22123, "invariant by abstraction"), and Robometer (2603.02115, 1M-trajectory scaled), and explicitly pre-registers FLORA as "The FP falsifier homes here." The card has not chosen VLC as its only foil; it has built the strong-foil bench already. ConTraIRL 2606.03017, the briefing's lead dated evidence, is **not in the vault at all** — an uncheckable citation. |
| 4 | "Distribution-free" is overstated — the PINN is distribution-bound with a wider envelope (H4) | **7** | Correct and referee-relevant. A frozen neural GRF estimator with an admitted off-manifold trust envelope (the card's own H4 + Risk-3) is not literally distribution-free. The card already concedes the envelope operationally while keeping the absolute word in the First-principles framing — so this is a genuine framing inconsistency. Partly semantic, which caps it below 8. |
| 5 | Reward-transfer leaves the on-hardware optimization loop uncosted; action-transfer (VIRAL) may win on wall-time | **6** | Valid omission: the card's Risk-2 concedes "a transferable reward still needs an on-hardware optimization loop" but never costs the real-rollout count, and the H1 prediction compares retention, not deployment wall-time. Plausible. Deducted because it is untested *on both sides* — VIRAL's distillation also has a real cost, so "action-transfer wins on wall-time" is asserted, not shown. |

**Mean = 7.2. Min = 5.**

---

##### 2. Weakest link

**Finding 3** is the least-confident claim, and it is the briefing's self-declared "most decision-relevant" one — a dangerous combination. The briefing frames the card as having chosen a *weak strawman* (VLC) and being blind to the 2026 dynamics-invariant literature. The card text contradicts this: TRIRL, FLORA, and Robometer are all present as learned-side competitors, with FLORA named as the falsifier target. The briefing's own Finding-3 line ("card implicitly concedes by pre-registering FLORA") half-admits this, which means the synthesis is internally inconsistent about whether the gap exists.

What would verify it: (a) confirm whether ConTraIRL 2606.03017 is a real arXiv paper and what it claims — it is currently absent from KnowledgeHub and unverifiable in-vault, so it cannot bear weight as "dated evidence"; (b) read FLORA (2605.22123) and TRIRL (2605.11020) and decide whether either has *ever been evaluated under a contact-dynamics shift* (footwear/terrain/payload) versus only appearance/object/gravity/disabled-joint OOD. If neither has touched contact-dynamics shift, the card's whitespace is real and Finding 3 collapses to "re-target the H1 *prediction's* foil from VLC to FLORA," a one-line edit, not a reframe. The recommendation (re-implement TRIRL/FLORA on the footwear task) is sound regardless — but it is the card's *existing* H1 falsifier, not a new insight.

---

##### 3. Bias check

**The Skeptic over-dominated, with the Economist/Historian supplying a flattering convergence.** Findings 3 and 4 (both Skeptic-led) carry the synthesis, and the "hidden cross-perspective connection" is a Skeptic argument (money-vs-time fault line) dressed as independent corroboration — the Economist and Historian are made to "predict the identical resolution," which is suspiciously tidy and is precisely how a single perspective launders itself into apparent consensus. The Academic is invoked mainly to second the Skeptic. The Practitioner (Finding 5) is the only genuinely orthogonal voice and is under-weighted — it raises the one *empirical* gap (real-rollout cost) that the briefing then files as "plausible but untested" rather than promoting to a required experiment. Net: not balanced; tilted toward framing-critique over measurement-critique.

---

##### 4. Missing perspective

**The experimentalist / reviewer-2 statistician.** No perspective interrogated the *strength* of the anchor as a transfer claim. R²=0.99 sensor-free across 4 footwear types is a small-N condition sweep on (almost certainly) one robot and one gait family; the −7.17 dBA is a mean over those conditions. A sixth angle asks: what is the variance, how many real trials, is "high heels" actually the hardest contact shift or just the most rhetorically vivid one, and does GRF-R² off-distribution even *correlate* with downstream policy SR (the card asserts the link H4 is supposed to measure but treats as established). This would change conclusions: if the GRF estimator's headline R² rests on a thin condition sweep, then Finding 1 — currently the briefing's bedrock 9/10 — is itself softer than "most reliable," and the whole DE-RISK recommendation should foreground *replicating the anchor's measurement* before building the three-reward bake-off on top of it. The briefing treats the anchor as bedrock and litigates the framing; reviewer-2 would litigate the bedrock.

---

##### 5. Overall grade and top fixes

**Grade: B.**

A genuinely good research instinct (law-grounded reward out-transfers learned reward on contact shift) wrapped in a synthesis that over-claims its own novelty against the card. The briefing's strongest move is demoting "distribution-free" to "wider-envelope-on-contact-regime" and promoting H5 to the headline — that is correct and publishable advice. Its weakest move is mischaracterizing a card that has already done most of the strong-foil homework, then leaning on an unverifiable citation (ConTraIRL) to manufacture the gap.

Top things to fix, in order:
1. **Retract or verify the strawman claim.** The card already benches TRIRL/FLORA/Robometer and pre-registers FLORA as the falsifier. Re-scope Finding 3 from "the card chose a weak foil" to "promote FLORA from the Related table into the H1 *prediction* as the head-to-head baseline." Drop or substantiate ConTraIRL 2606.03017 — it is not in the vault and cannot be cited as dated evidence.
2. **Make the reward-hacking / degenerate-gait probe a required gate, not the third bullet.** A frozen GRF estimator used as the *sole* objective has an obvious failure mode (minimize impact → stand still / shuffle). This is a correctness risk to the whole bet and deserves promotion above the framing edits.
3. **Cost both sides of the wall-time comparison.** Finding 5 asserts action-transfer may win on deployment time but never costs VIRAL's distillation against the reward loop's real-rollout count. Specify the measurement (real rollouts to convergence for each of the three arms) before claiming a winner.
4. **Replicate the anchor before building on it.** Add a line confirming the QuietWalk R²/dBA numbers and their trial counts; the entire DE-RISK plan inherits the anchor's measurement validity.

---

### A3 (Direction review)

Topic: Controller-Gain-Aware Sim-to-Real — the bet that co-optimizing PD gains $(K_p, K_d)$ *jointly with* the dynamics-randomization distribution captures a non-empty **gain×dynamics interaction term** that the standard "tune gains for sysID, then randomize dynamics" recipe structurally misses, *and* that the co-optimized DR variable beats a runtime adaptive-gain residual at matched success rate (preventing the mismatch beats patching it).

Novelty check ran via alphaxiv `discover_papers` (two queries, both returned results) plus `get_paper_content` on the top new threat (2604.14484). Verdict: **still-whitespace** on the H2 interaction-term + H4 prevent-vs-patch core, with one new theory paper (2604.14484) that pre-empts the *mechanism* behind H3 but not the central bet.

#### Prompt 1 — Five Perspectives

**1. PRACTITIONER (builds/deploys daily).**
- *Position*: Gains are not a free hyperparameter you sweep — real controllers cap achievable $K_p/K_d$, and the gain you train against must match the firmware the robot actually ships with. Co-optimizing a *distribution* of gains is academically clean but a deployment engineer can only set one gain per robot, so the result has to collapse to a single shippable $(K_p, K_d)$ or it is useless on hardware.
- *Strongest evidence*: [[2604.02523|Tune to Learn]] is the practitioner's vindication — stiff gains with the *lowest* sysID error give the *worst* transfer and amplify high-frequency oscillation on the real arm; the field's fidelity metric is anti-correlated with the deploy goal. [[2507.23445|Physics-Guided Gain Regularization]] shows the failure concretely: on a 110:1 gearbox a DR-RNN develops unbounded equivalent gains (~4.0) and a persistent 5 Hz oscillation that a measured-gain constraint (<2.0) kills. This is exactly the deploy-time pathology academics gloss over.
- *Only-this-perspective insight*: The runtime competitors A3 wants to beat ([[2505.00991|DexCtrl]], [[2606.06218|TAM (Torque Adaptation)]], [[2602.14174|Direction Matters]]) exist because *in production you cannot re-train per robot* — runtime adaptation is not a weaker baseline, it is the operationally honest answer to per-unit gain drift. A3 must show the co-optimized policy generalizes across the actual gain spread of a fleet, not just beats a residual on one arm.

**2. ACADEMIC (knows the peer-reviewed literature).**
- *Position*: The closed-loop coupling A3 invokes is real and now has formal backing — the controller genuinely is part of the plant the policy controls. But the literature already names the gain as a transfer variable (not a fidelity one), so the *novel* contribution is narrowly the interaction-term claim (H2), not the framing.
- *Strongest evidence*: [[2604.14484|Nonasymptotic Gain-Dependent BC Theory]] (found via alphaxiv, a direct theory companion to Tune-to-Learn) proves the stationary error variance is $\sigma^2 K_p/(2K_d)$, strictly monotone increasing in stiffness and decreasing in damping over the entire stable orthant, and factorizes failure probability into a *gain-dependent amplification index* × validation loss — so "training loss cannot predict closed-loop performance." That is H3's oscillation-suppression mechanism turned into a theorem. [[2505.24068|DiffCoTune]] already *co-tunes* differentiable-sim params and differentiable-controller PD gains jointly (+60% sim-to-sim, Go1 +1.04 m, 34 cm biped jump).
- *Only-this-perspective insight*: The card's own table concedes the trap: DiffCoTune co-optimizes gains+sim *per-system from real rollouts to match deployment* and never randomizes a distribution, so it cannot exhibit an interaction term — but a reviewer will ask why a *distribution* should beat per-system matching at all, since matching is the tighter objective. H2 must argue the interaction term buys *robustness across the dynamics spread*, not point performance, or DiffCoTune dominates.

**3. SKEPTIC (thinks it's wrong or already solved).**
- *Position*: "Gain × dynamics interaction term" risks being a vacuous reframing — gains and dynamics already co-enter the closed-loop matrices $A_K, B_K$ (2604.14484 makes this explicit), so *of course* their effect is non-separable; demonstrating super-additivity in a metric is almost guaranteed by construction and tells you nothing actionable. And H4 may already be lost: runtime adaptation is established and strong.
- *Strongest evidence*: [[2505.00991|DexCtrl]] jointly predicts action + adaptive gains and beats fixed-gain baselines on contact-rich dexterity "without excessive randomization"; [[2602.14174|Direction Matters]] hits 91% SR across 4 contact tasks vs 67% best baseline by regulating stiffness online; [[2509.23075|In-Hand Articulated Tools]] reaches 100% SR / 0.0 mm tip gap with an online tactile+torque residual. Three independent runtime systems already recover the transfer A3 wants to recover by training-distribution design. If any matches co-optimization (H4 falsifier), A3 reduces to DexCtrl.
- *Only-this-perspective insight*: The card never specifies the *magnitude* of the interaction term needed to matter. A statistically-significant-but-1% super-additive term clears the falsifier yet fails to justify the training-cost blowup the Risks box admits. The bet needs a pre-registered effect-size threshold, not just "non-empty."

**4. ECONOMIST (follows money/incentives).**
- *Position*: This direction is cheap to run and benchmark-legible, which is exactly why it is attractive — and exactly why it risks being an incremental ablation dressed as a thesis. The payoff structure rewards a clean full-factorial {gain}×{dynamics}×{joint} table on an existing suite, not a hard new capability.
- *Strongest evidence*: The targets are saturation benchmarks: [[2602.23253|SPARR]] already hits 95–100% on [[2407.08028|AutoMate]] and 74.5% relative on NIST; [[2305.17110|IndustReal]] hits 80–100% on NIST pegs/gears/NEMA. When the headroom is single-digit, an interaction-term paper competes for the last few points — high citation-per-effort, low capability delta. The genuinely un-saturated substrate is [[2603.15469|RoCo Challenge]]'s sub-mm planetary-gearbox Onsite track (paired sim+real, 300+ demos, 60+ teams) — whoever owns the *first paired-track gain×dynamics result* captures the benchmark narrative.
- *Only-this-perspective insight*: The incentive to pick AutoMate/NIST (cheap, sim-numbers available) is the trap; the value is concentrated entirely in the paired-real RoCo/RoboChallenge tracks that DexCtrl-style papers have *not* yet posted gain-co-optimization numbers on. Commit only if the experiment lives on the paired real track, else this is a leaderboard footnote.

**5. HISTORIAN (has seen prior waves).**
- *Position*: This is the latest turn of the recurring "the controller is part of the learning problem" cycle — from operational-space/impedance control (Hogan, Khatib) through variable-impedance learning to action-space studies. Each wave re-discovers that *how* you actuate matters as much as *what* you command; A3 is the sim-to-real-distribution instalment of that lineage.
- *Strongest evidence*: The card's own table is a fossil record: [[2312.03673|Action Space Study]] (Joint Velocity transfers best across 250 PPO agents, Joint Torque unsafe for contact), [[2210.13702|DeXtreme]] (action-smoothness reward as implicit gain proxy, 27.8 vs 14.8), [[2511.15200|VIRAL]] (delta-action critical, RSI 95% vs <10%). Every wave found action/gain structure decisive but stopped short of co-optimizing it with the dynamics distribution — the same "named-but-unaddressed" pattern the card flags.
- *Only-this-perspective insight*: Historically these "interaction term" results survive when the mechanism is named (here: oscillation-spectrum amplification, now provable via 2604.14484) and die when they stay a benchmark delta. The DR lineage (1710.06537 dynamics-randomization onward) shows wider randomization buys robustness only up to the over-conservatism cliff Physics-Guided Regularization flags — so the historian's warning is that gain randomization, naively widened, reproduces the *overly-conservative-policy* failure DR already hit. The win is a *narrow physics-seeded* gain prior, not a bigger grid.

#### Prompt 2 — Contradiction Map

1. **Direct clashes.**
   - *Skeptic vs Academic on H2's substance*: Skeptic says super-additivity is guaranteed-by-construction (gains and dynamics co-enter $A_K, B_K$, per 2604.14484) and therefore vacuous; Academic says the interaction term is the *one* genuinely novel claim. They clash on whether "non-separable in the closed-loop matrices" implies "super-additive in transfer SR" — it does not automatically, and that gap is the whole experiment.
   - *Practitioner vs Economist on benchmark choice*: Practitioner wants the result to collapse to one shippable gain on real hardware; Economist wants the paired-real RoCo track for narrative ownership. These align on avoiding AutoMate-sim but clash on emphasis — deploy-feasibility (one gain, hardware box) vs leaderboard-firstness (distribution result on paired track).
   - *Skeptic vs Practitioner on H4*: Skeptic says runtime adaptation (DexCtrl, Direction Matters) likely already matches/beats co-optimization, killing H4; Practitioner says runtime adaptation is *mandatory* in production regardless, so even if H4 wins on a benchmark the deployed system will still want a runtime layer — i.e., the "prevent vs patch" dichotomy is false, real systems do both.

2. **Strongest vs weakest.**
   - *Strongest: Academic.* It is the only perspective grounded in a freshly-surfaced formal result (2604.14484) that both supports the framing and sharply bounds what is novel, and it correctly isolates DiffCoTune as the baseline H2 must beat-by-distribution.
   - *Weakest: Historian.* Directionally right and useful for the over-conservatism warning, but it argues by analogy and cannot adjudicate whether *this specific* interaction term is real; its strongest concrete contribution (narrow physics-seeded prior) is already in the card's Risks box.

3. **Resolving question.** On [[2603.15469|RoCo Challenge]]'s paired Simulation+Onsite tracks, does full-factorial {gain-rand} × {dynamics-rand} × {joint co-optimization} show a *pre-registered, effect-size-thresholded* super-additive real-SR margin (H2) that simultaneously **survives** a head-to-head against a [[2505.00991|DexCtrl]]-style runtime adaptive-gain predictor at matched SR (H4)? That single experiment resolves the Skeptic-vs-Academic clash (is the interaction term real and large) and the Skeptic-vs-Practitioner clash (is prevent ≥ patch) at once.

4. **Universal agreement (likely true).** Every perspective accepts that controller gains are a *transfer* variable, not a fidelity one — the closed-loop coupling is real, stiff/low-sysID-error gains transfer worst, and gains belong in the transfer-distribution conversation. [[2604.02523|Tune to Learn]] (empirical inversion) and [[2604.14484|Nonasymptotic BC Theory]] ($\sigma^2 K_p/(2K_d)$ monotonicity, failure = amplification × loss) jointly nail this down. The FP *framing* is solid; only the *interaction-term + prevent-beats-patch* bet is contested.

5. **Blind spot (addressed by none).** No perspective addressed **sim-side gain fidelity** — whether the simulator's PD/actuator model is even faithful enough that a "co-optimized gain distribution" in sim maps to anything on hardware. If the sim's actuator dynamics (motor bandwidth, torque saturation, communication delay) are themselves mis-specified, the gain×dynamics interaction measured in sim is an artifact of the wrong actuator model, and the whole H2 super-additivity could be a simulation pathology rather than a transferable effect. The card debates *which* gains to randomize but never *whether the sim renders gains correctly* in the first place.

#### Prompt 3 — Synthesis Briefing

1. **CEO summary.** A3's framing is on firm ground and just got firmer: the controller-is-part-of-the-plant thesis is now backed by both an empirical inversion ([[2604.02523|Tune to Learn]]) and a formal failure theory ([[2604.14484|Nonasymptotic BC Theory]], $\sigma^2 K_p/(2K_d)$). But the *publishable bet* is narrow and contested. H2 (a non-empty gain×dynamics interaction term in a *randomized* distribution) is genuine whitespace — no paper found co-optimizes gains *with a dynamics distribution* or measures super-additivity; [[2505.24068|DiffCoTune]] co-tunes per-system without a distribution, the runtime trio (DexCtrl/Direction Matters/In-Hand) adapts at deploy-time. The risks are that (a) super-additivity may be guaranteed-but-tiny, clearing the falsifier without earning the training cost, (b) H4's "prevent beats patch" may lose outright to established runtime adaptation, and (c) the interaction could be a sim-actuator-model artifact. Commit only with a pre-registered effect-size threshold on the paired-real RoCo track and an honest sim-gain-fidelity check.

2. **Five key findings, ranked by reliability.**
   - **F1 (most reliable): Gains are a transfer variable, not a fidelity variable.** Stiff/lowest-sysID-error gains transfer worst and amplify oscillation. *Supports*: Practitioner, Academic, Historian. *Challenges*: none. Backed empirically (2604.02523) and now theoretically (2604.14484: failure = amplification × loss, $\sigma^2 K_p/(2K_d)$ monotone).
   - **F2: The H2 interaction-term + H4 prevent-vs-patch core is real whitespace.** alphaxiv returned no paper that randomizes gains *jointly with a dynamics distribution* and measures super-additivity. *Supports*: Academic, Historian. *Challenges*: Skeptic (says it's guaranteed-but-vacuous). DiffCoTune (per-system, no distribution) is the nearest miss and the card concedes it.
   - **F3: H3's mechanism is now pre-empted as a standalone theoretical contribution.** [[2604.14484|Nonasymptotic BC Theory]] already proves the oscillation/amplification-vs-SR link H3 proposes to demonstrate. *Supports*: Academic, Skeptic. *Challenges*: Practitioner (the *empirical* deployment-spectrum measurement is still unowned). H3 should cite it as the formal precedent and pivot to the RL+contact regime the theory explicitly excludes (it is BC-only, linearized, free-space).
   - **F4: H4 is the front-line risk — established runtime adaptation may match co-optimization.** *Supports*: Skeptic, Practitioner. *Challenges*: Academic (prevention should generalize across the dynamics spread where a fixed-gain+residual does not). DexCtrl (beats fixed-gain), Direction Matters (91% vs 67%), In-Hand Articulated (100% SR) are strong incumbents.
   - **F5 (least settled): The right substrate is the paired-real track, not sim benchmarks.** AutoMate/NIST are saturated (SPARR 95–100%, IndustReal 80–100%); RoCo's sub-mm Onsite track has no posted gain-co-optimization numbers. *Supports*: Economist, Practitioner. *Challenges*: none on merit, but cost/access to the paired real track is the open question.

3. **Hidden cross-perspective connection.** The Practitioner's hardware-gain-box constraint, the Economist's narrow-headroom warning, and the Historian's DR-over-conservatism cliff are the *same* constraint viewed three ways: the feasible gain region is small (hardware caps + physics-seeded prior + avoid the over-randomization cliff that Physics-Guided Regularization flags), which means the gain×dynamics distribution A3 randomizes over is *narrow by necessity*. That narrowness is simultaneously the reason the interaction term might be small (Skeptic's worry) and the reason the experiment is cheap and the deployed result is shippable (the upside). The narrow-prior strategy already in the Risks box is therefore not a mitigation — it *is* the thesis.

4. **Actionable insight for the researcher: DE-RISK, then commit conditionally.** Do not commit blind and do not drop — the framing is too well-supported to drop and the core is too contested to commit blind. **Concrete next experiment** (de-risking H2+H4 in one shot, pre-registered): on [[2603.15469|RoCo Challenge]]'s paired Simulation+Onsite planetary-gearbox track, run the full-factorial {gain-rand} × {dynamics-rand} × {joint} within the hardware-admissible gain box seeded by Tune-to-Learn's compliant/overdamped prior, with (i) a *pre-declared* super-additivity effect-size threshold on real SR (not just "non-empty"), and (ii) a matched-SR head-to-head against a DexCtrl-style runtime adaptive-gain predictor and a TAM-style torque residual. Gate the commit on **both** the interaction term clearing threshold **and** co-optimization beating the runtime residual on the *real* track. Before that, run the blind-spot check: verify the sim's actuator/PD model is faithful (bandwidth, saturation, delay) so the measured interaction is not a sim artifact. Position H3 as citing 2604.14484's theory and extending it to the contact-rich RL regime the theory excludes.

5. **Frontier question.** Is the gain×dynamics interaction term an *intrinsic closed-loop property* (so co-optimization is fundamentally needed) or an *artifact of an imperfect simulator actuator model* (so a better sim erases it)? If the latter, the entire forward-sim A-cluster is attacking a self-inflicted gap, and the field should be improving actuator fidelity (real-to-sim, Cluster B) rather than randomizing over its own modeling error.

---

#### Prompt 4 — Peer Review (independent)

*Reviewer stance: skeptical Stanford robotics faculty. Grounded against the A3 card (Sim2Real.md L259-334) and the cited KH notes. Verdict up front: the framing is genuinely strong and the empirical anchor checks out verbatim, but the briefing inflates its formal backing, over-specifies its substrate, and never confronts the one benchmark result that actively argues against its own substrate choice.*

---

##### 1. Confidence scores (1-10, reliability)

**F1 — Gains are a transfer variable, not a fidelity variable. Score: 9/10.**
Verified verbatim in KH 2604.02523 L38: "stiff... gains, despite yielding the lowest system identification errors, resulted in lower sim-to-real transferability and exacerbated high-frequency oscillations." This is the single most load-bearing and best-supported claim in the card. The one deduction: the source actually flags *stiff and overdamped* as the lowest-sysID/worst-transfer pair, while compliant+overdamped is best for BC — so "overdamped" lands on both sides depending on context, and the Risks-box "compliant/overdamped prior" inherits that ambiguity. Not fatal, but the prior is less crisp than F1 implies.

**F2 — H2 interaction-term + H4 prevent-vs-patch is real whitespace; DiffCoTune is the nearest miss. Score: 7/10.**
The DiffCoTune scoping is accurate: KH 2505.24068 confirms it co-tunes sim+controller params per-system from real rollouts ("intermediate tuning domain," explicitly vs "sysId-then-tune"), never randomizes a distribution, never measures an interaction term. So the *mechanistic* gap is real. The deduction is that "no paper measures super-additivity" is an absence-of-evidence claim resting on one alphaxiv pass with degraded MCP (per prior-audit pattern, mcp__alphaxiv__* has been non-callable); it is a search-bounded null, not a proof of whitespace. Skeptic's "guaranteed-but-vacuous" challenge is also unrebutted: a non-empty interaction term in a *narrow* feasible gain box can be statistically real and practically negligible, and the card's own Risk-(a) concedes this.

**F3 — H3's mechanism is pre-empted as a standalone theoretical contribution by the BC theory paper. Score: 4/10.**
This is the weakest finding and the briefing's biggest over-reach. The paper doing the pre-emption (2604.14484, "Nonasymptotic BC Theory," σ²Kp/2Kd) is **not in KnowledgeHub and is not cited anywhere in the A3 card or the whole Sim2Real doc** (grep-confirmed). The CEO summary leans on it as half the two-pillar foundation ("empirical inversion + formal failure theory"), but the card itself has no formal anchor — H3's actual in-card precedent is Physics-Guided Gain Regularization (2507.23445), an empirical oscillation-suppression result, not a theorem. So F3's "pivot H3 because the theory already owns it" is built on a citation the document does not contain and the vault has not verified. The pivot recommendation (move H3 to RL+contact, which the theory excludes as BC-only/linearized/free-space) may well be correct, but it is currently unfalsifiable from inside this vault.

**F4 — H4 is the front-line risk; runtime adaptation may match co-optimization. Score: 8/10.**
Well-grounded and appropriately the "deciding" hypothesis. Incumbent effect sizes verify: Direction Matters 91% vs 67% (KH 2602.14174 L37), In-Hand 100% SR (KH 2509.23075 L39), TAM 76.2 vs 47.6 (KH 2606.06218 L39). The Academic's rebuttal ("prevention should generalize across the spread") is asserted, not evidenced — these incumbents already win on contact tasks *without* "excessive randomization" (DexCtrl), so the prior should arguably favor the incumbent until A3 shows a generalization regime where runtime adaptation provably cannot reach. Honest framing; I would have scored 9 if the generalization argument had any cited support.

**F5 — The paired-real RoCo track is the right substrate, not saturated sim benchmarks. Score: 6/10.**
SPARR 95-100% AutoMate / 74.5% NIST and IndustReal saturation verify (KH 2602.23253, 2305.17110), so "sim benchmarks are saturated" holds. RoCo's paired Simulation+Onsite planetary-gearbox tracks and the Sim-to-Real Cliff verify (KH 2603.15469). Two deductions. (i) Over-specification: the card and briefing call it "sub-mm" — the KH note says only "simplified planetary gearbox," with no sub-mm tolerance posted; that descriptor is unsupported. (ii) The fatal omission: RoCo's *own headline finding* is that end-to-end VLA beat modular perception-action pipelines on the real track. A3's co-optimized-DR-gain proposal sits in the modular/explicit-control family, so the chosen substrate's marquee result points away from A3's mechanism. The briefing never engages this, and "Challenges: none on merit" is therefore wrong — the substrate carries a structural challenge to the thesis.

Mean = 6.8. Min = 4 (F3).

---

##### 2. Weakest link

**F3, and behind it the CEO summary's "formal failure theory" pillar.** The whole "H3 is pre-empted, pivot it" recommendation depends on 2604.14484, which is neither ingested nor cited. **To verify:** ingest 2604.14484 into KH and confirm three things the briefing asserts: (a) that it actually proves an oscillation/amplification-vs-SR link (not merely a generic BC sample-complexity bound), (b) that the σ²Kp/2Kd term genuinely couples controller gain to transfer error rather than being a tracking-error bound, and (c) the stated scope limits (BC-only, linearized, free-space) that license the RL+contact pivot. Until then, treat H3's "already owned by theory" status as unverified and keep H3 as a live empirical contribution.

---

##### 3. Bias check

**Skeptic + Practitioner over-dominated; Academic under-delivered.** The card and synthesis are unusually self-critical (good), but the balance tipped: the two strongest findings (F1, F4) and all three Risks are framed in Practitioner/Skeptic terms (hardware gain box, narrow headroom, runtime-incumbent threat, sim-artifact worry). The Academic perspective is invoked mainly as a *foil* that gets out-argued (F2 "guaranteed-but-vacuous" beats it; F4 generalization claim is asserted without support; F3 hands the theory contribution away entirely). The net effect is a briefing that argues itself toward "narrow, contested, de-risk-then-maybe" — defensible, but the Academic case for why the interaction term *should* be large (a real closed-loop coupling argument) was never built, only assumed away. The "Hidden cross-perspective connection" is elegant but circular: it uses the narrowness three perspectives all observe to simultaneously explain why the effect is cheap to test (pro) and why it might be vacuous (con), which is rhetorically tidy but means the same fact is doing double duty for opposite conclusions.

---

##### 4. Missing perspective

**The Controls/Systems theorist (closed-loop identifiability and actuator modeling).** The Frontier question — is the interaction intrinsic or a sim-actuator artifact — is exactly a controls question, and no perspective in the synthesis owns it. A controls reviewer would ask: with a high-fidelity actuator model (bandwidth, saturation, transport delay, drive-train compliance, the 110:1 / planetary-gearbox backlash RoCo actually has), does the gain×dynamics term survive? If the entire forward-sim A-cluster is randomizing over its own modeling error (the Frontier-question's own admission), then the correct response is Cluster B (real-to-sim actuator identification), not A3. This sixth angle could invert the conclusion from "de-risk then commit to A3" to "A3 is conditional on a blind-spot check that, if it fails, redirects the whole effort to Cluster B." The briefing names this risk but files it under "actionable insight, run the check first" rather than treating it as a gate that could kill the direction — a controls theorist would make it the primary gate, not a footnote.

---

##### 5. Overall grade

**B+.**

Strong framing on a verified empirical inversion (F1 is A-grade work), an honest and correctly-scoped novelty argument against the nearest miss (DiffCoTune), and a genuinely good pre-registered de-risking experiment. It loses the A- band on three concrete defects, in priority order:

1. **Fix F3's phantom citation (top fix).** Either ingest and verify 2604.14484 and add it to the A3 card as the formal anchor, or drop the "formal failure theory" pillar from the CEO summary and the "H3 is pre-empted" claim. Right now the briefing's two-pillar foundation has one pillar the document cannot see.
2. **Confront RoCo's VLA-beats-modular result.** The chosen substrate's headline finding argues against A3's mechanism family; F5's "Challenges: none on merit" must be downgraded and the tension addressed, or a different paired-real substrate justified.
3. **Promote the sim-artifact check from insight to gate, and add the controls perspective.** If the interaction is an actuator-model artifact, A3 should redirect to Cluster B — make that a first-class kill condition, not the last sentence of the actionable insight.
4. Minor: drop the unsupported "sub-mm" descriptor on RoCo; reconcile the compliant-vs-overdamped prior ambiguity in the Risks box against Tune-to-Learn's actual stiff-AND-overdamped finding.

Bottom line: commit-conditionally is the right call, but the briefing currently presents the framing as "firmer" than the vault evidence supports, because its firmest-sounding new support (the formal theory) is the one claim with no grounding in the document under review.

### B (Cluster review)

Topic: the coherence + central shared-bottleneck thesis of Cluster B — *"Real-to-Sim-to-Real: Grounding the Simulator."* Cluster framing (Cluster-Overview row): *"Recovering $(\phi^\star, \psi^\star)$ from real data is the new bottleneck — and a chosen law caps it."* Cluster intro: *"Invert reality into the simulator first — recover appearance and dynamics from real data — then the forward transfer comes nearly for free, because a simulator predicts reality forward no better than it captured reality backward."* The grouping logic: B1 recovers the *joint state* (appearance+physics), B2 recovers the *parameters* of a fixed law, B4 recovers the *law itself*, B3 is the *data-engine* that consumes all three over a fold-back loop. I am reviewing whether this carves the problem at the right joint and whether the shared inversion-is-the-ceiling bet is sound — not re-reviewing each direction.

Grounding: read Sim2Real.md L90-101 (Cluster Overview) + L335-653 (all of Cluster B). KH notes read: Arcadia 2512.00076 (B3 topThreat), D-REX 2603.01151 (B2 topThreat / B1 anchor), SLAT-Phys 2603.23973 (B2 closest pre-emption), plus MASIV/REALM/NCLaw frontmatter checks. alphaxiv `discover_papers` WORKED (3 calls returned ranked results); `get_paper_content` worked via the `url` arg (read APIC 2606.03355 + MOSIV 2603.06022 from saved full-text via grep).

#### Prompt 1 — Five Perspectives

**1. PRACTITIONER (builds/deploys policies daily).**
- *Core position*: The cluster's "forward transfer comes nearly for free once you invert well" is the single most dangerous sentence in the doc. In practice the per-scene reconstruction cost *is* the deployment cost, and it does not amortize — every new object or scene re-pays it. The bottleneck a practitioner actually hits is not "is my inversion lossy" but "I cannot afford to invert every object on the line."
- *Strongest evidence*: Re3Sim (2502.08645) reports ~51.5 s background + 60.5 s *per object* reconstruction; D-REX (2603.01151) needs ≥20 demos/object and recovers *mass only*; the card itself flags "reconstruction is per-scene expensive" as B1-Risk-1 and "grounding cost per object" as B3-Risk-3. PolaRiS (2512.16881) needing <20 min/scene is sold as cheap and is still prohibitive at fleet scale.
- *Only-this-perspective tells you*: The whole cluster is bet on inversion being the ceiling, but for a deployed system the *binding* constraint is inversion throughput, which is exactly what B2's amortization attacks — so B2 is the only direction with a deployment-realistic payoff, and B1/B4's per-scene joint-fitting bets may be scientifically true yet operationally inert. Commit weight should follow deployment cost, not elegance.

**2. ACADEMIC (knows the peer-reviewed literature).**
- *Core position*: The cluster's carve is genuinely clean and the "invert-first" framing is well-supported, but the literature has moved faster than the card's "the field still treats this as solved by better engines" claim admits — the *non-monotone* fidelity→transfer evidence is stronger and more general than one navigation counter-example.
- *Strongest evidence*: Real-to-Sim GS (2511.04665) joint-ablation (removing color *or* physics collapses r) is a real, load-bearing result and the per-object-class deformable Δr (rope 0.901 vs Isaac Lab 0.237) is striking. But Lower-Fidelity Sim2Real (2207.10821) showing lower fidelity *helped* nav transfer is not a quirk — it is the DR/regularization story (too-faithful sim overfits). VR-Robo (2502.01536) and GASE (2606.17520) both show high-fidelity inversion *does* help nav, so the field already has the both-signs evidence; the open question is correctly the *law*, not the direction.
- *Only-this-perspective tells you*: The cluster's deepest unproven assumption is *monotonicity*, and the academic record already contains a monotonicity violation the card treats as navigation-local — so H1 (monotone on manipulation) is the highest-risk, highest-value experiment, and the card should not assume the navigation counter-example fails to generalize; it should *test* whether contact-rich manipulation has its own "too-faithful overfits" regime.

**3. SKEPTIC (thinks the bet is wrong or already solved).**
- *Core position*: The cluster's headline ("inversion fidelity is the forward-transfer ceiling") is half tautology and half already-occupied. The tautology: of course forward ≤ backward fidelity if the sim is a deterministic function of the recovered state — the card even writes it as a definitional lower bound. The occupied part: the closed real→sim→real loop (B3) and the amortized inference net (B2) both have named existence proofs the card concedes (Arcadia, SLAT-Phys), so the surviving novelty is a thin slice.
- *Strongest evidence*: Arcadia (2512.00076) states B3's H1 falsifier *verbatim* ("removing any stage reverts to one-shot training") and reports feedback-on>off (LIBERO 88.5 vs 86.9). SLAT-Phys (2603.23973) already does feed-forward single-RGB explicit Young's+density+Poisson recovery at ~9.9s/object — the exact amortized predictor B2 proposes, minus the diff-sim supervision. CASHER (2412.01770) already shows +55% from unsupervised deployment-scan fold-back. The card's defense in all three cases is "but not over *successive rounds* / not *in clutter* / not *diff-sim-supervised*" — which is a real but narrow wedge.
- *Only-this-perspective tells you*: The card has honestly drawn the whitespace as a *residual* after conceding the mechanism (commendable rigor), but a reviewer will read "monotone-across-rounds" and "zero-demo-in-clutter" as engineering deltas, not conceptual ones — so the publication risk is not being scooped, it is being judged incremental. The bet must be sold on the *measurement* (the law / the SR-vs-parameter-distance frontier / the drift-vs-gate trajectory), not the mechanism.

**4. ECONOMIST (follows money/incentives).**
- *Core position*: Real-to-sim-to-real is where the data-cost incentive and the benchmark-saturation incentive collide, and the cluster is structurally aligned with the winning side: synthetic-data-beats-real-data is the narrative that lets academia compete with industry teleoperation fleets. That makes B3 (twin-as-data-engine) the most fundable and B1/B4 (fidelity laws) the most citable-but-least-funded.
- *Strongest evidence*: The corpus is saturated with synthetic-beats-real claims — Genie Sim 3.0 (2601.02078) 1,500 synthetic episodes beating real zero-shot (R²=0.94), SIM1 (2604.08544) 76% from de-novo where real-data baselines hit 0%, RoboTwin 2.0 (2506.18088) +24.4% real few-shot. Every lab with a 3DGS pipeline ships one; the benchmark substrate (RoboTwin/RoboChallenge/LIBERO) is where the citations are. D-REX, MASIV, SLAT-Phys all 2025-2026 — the field is in a land-grab.
- *Only-this-perspective tells you*: The crowding *is* the signal — a dozen joint-inversion engines means the engine is no longer a differentiator and the money is moving to (a) data-engine throughput and (b) the *evaluation/selection* layer (which twin do I trust). That makes B1's offline-twin-ranker framing (H2) and B3's exchange-rate the economically live questions; the engine-building H-rows are where the researcher will be out-resourced by industry.

**5. HISTORIAN (has seen prior waves).**
- *Core position*: This is the system-identification pendulum swinging back, dressed in 3DGS. The "recover the true model then plan in it" program is as old as adaptive control and model-based RL; it has repeatedly lost to model-free robustness (DR, end-to-end) precisely because *recovery is brittle and the recovered model is never quite right*. The cluster's "invert first, transfer is free" is the optimistic half of a cycle that historically corrected toward "don't trust the recovered model, hedge it."
- *Strongest evidence*: The card itself contains the historical correction inside it — Real-is-Sim (2504.03597) runs a *continuous 60 Hz correction* "precisely because uncorrected twin error compounds," and Phys2Real (2510.11689) inverse-variance-*weights* the estimate against a prior rather than trusting it. Offline Domain Randomization (2506.10133) proving DR *is* maximum-likelihood estimation is the historian's punchline: the field already discovered that estimation and randomization are the same operation viewed two ways.
- *Only-this-perspective tells you*: Every prior sysID wave died on the *open-loop trust* assumption — that a recovered model, once good enough, can be deployed forward without online correction. B3's fold-back loop and D-cluster's deploy-time adaptation are the historically-vindicated hedges; B1/B4's "invert once, transfer forward" is the historically-defeated bet. The card's strongest survival move is that it has *already absorbed* the hedge (B3 gates, D adapts) — so the cluster as a *whole* is on the right side of history even where B1/B4 alone are not.

#### Prompt 2 — Contradiction Map

**(1) Where perspectives directly clash.**
- *Practitioner vs Academic/Skeptic on what the binding constraint is*: Practitioner says inversion *throughput* (per-object cost) is binding and B1/B4's fidelity laws are operationally inert; Academic/Skeptic treat the fidelity→transfer *law* as the live scientific object. Concrete clash: is the cluster's bottleneck "$(\phi^\star,\psi^\star)$ recovery quality" (card's claim, academic-aligned) or "$(\phi^\star,\psi^\star)$ recovery *cost*" (practitioner)? The card picks quality; the practitioner says that is the wrong joint.
- *Historian vs the cluster's own intro*: Intro says forward transfer "comes nearly for free" once inverted; Historian (backed by Real-is-Sim's 60 Hz correction inside the card) says open-loop forward trust is the assumption every prior sysID wave died on. Direct contradiction on whether inversion is *sufficient* for forward transfer.
- *Skeptic vs Academic on novelty size*: Skeptic says Arcadia/SLAT-Phys/CASHER have occupied the mechanisms and only engineering deltas remain; Academic says the *measurement* (monotone law, per-class Δr) is genuinely unmeasured and conceptually load-bearing. They agree on the facts, disagree on whether the residual is publishable-as-novel.

**(2) Strongest vs weakest perspective.**
- *Strongest: Historian.* It is the only perspective that explains *why* the cluster is internally hedged (B3 gates, D adapts, Real-is-Sim corrects) and predicts which sub-bets survive — and its claim is corroborated by papers already inside the card, not external speculation. It reframes the cluster's coherence as "the field re-learning that recovered models need online hedging," which is both true and decision-relevant.
- *Weakest: Economist.* Its read (crowding = signal, money moves to data-engines/eval) is directionally right but least *falsifiable* and least specific to the technical bet — it would apply to almost any hot sim2real cluster. It informs *which direction to fund*, not whether the *thesis* is sound.

**(3) The one question that resolves the biggest contradiction.**
Is the fidelity→forward-transfer map **monotone on contact-rich manipulation**, or does it have a "too-faithful overfits" regime like navigation? This resolves Practitioner-vs-Academic (if non-monotone, the cheap low-fidelity twin can win and throughput stops being a separate axis from quality) AND Historian-vs-intro (a non-monotone law means inversion is *not* sufficient and the forward step needs the hedge). It is exactly B1-H1, which is why H1 is the cluster's true go/no-go, not a per-direction detail.

**(4) What every perspective agrees on (likely true).**
The *grouping is correct and the recovered model must not be trusted open-loop*. All five accept that real-to-sim-to-real is the right frame and that some form of correction/gating/weighting around the recovered model is necessary (Practitioner: amortize+fallback; Academic: measure don't assume; Skeptic: the conceded mechanisms all add a hedge; Economist: eval/selection layer is where value is; Historian: every wave needed the hedge). Universal agreement: a grounded twin produces *training-valid* (not just test-valid) data, and grounding beats generic assets — RialTo's 90%-vs-10% (2403.03949) is the proof none dispute.

**(5) The blind spot none addressed.**
*Identifiability and observation sufficiency.* The entire cluster assumes the real data *contains* enough signal to recover $(\phi^\star,\psi^\star)$ — but mass/friction/stiffness are famously *jointly unidentifiable* from passive video (you cannot separate stiffness from friction without exciting the right modes), and the card's own MOSIV-adjacent space confirms this ("ambiguities like distinguishing stiffness from friction can't be resolved by category"). No perspective asks: *for which object classes is the inverse problem even well-posed?* B2's amortized net and B4's learned law both inherit a non-identifiable target on under-actuated observations — the law could be measurable yet the recovery non-unique, and no H-row tests observation-sufficiency (active excitation) as the precondition for the whole inversion program.

#### Prompt 3 — Synthesis Briefing

**(1) CEO summary.** Cluster B carves the sim-to-real problem at a defensible joint — *invert reality into the simulator first, because forward transfer is upper-bounded by inversion fidelity* — and its four directions (joint-state / parameters / law / data-engine) are non-overlapping recoveries of different parts of $(\phi^\star,\psi^\star)$. The shared bet is sound *as a lower bound* but the cluster oversells "transfer comes nearly for free": the field already contains a fidelity-non-monotonicity result, a 60 Hz online-correction admission, and named existence proofs (Arcadia, SLAT-Phys, CASHER) for the closed loop and the amortized net — so the surviving novelty is not the mechanisms but three *measurements* nobody has run (the per-class monotone fidelity→r law, the zero-demo-in-clutter SR-vs-parameter-distance frontier, the round-over-round monotone-vs-drift trajectory). The cluster is on the right side of the sysID-vs-robustness historical cycle *only because it has already internalized the hedge* (B3 gates, D-cluster adapts); the directions that bet on open-loop forward trust (B1/B4 in isolation) are the historically weakest, and the binding real-world constraint (inversion throughput) points the commit weight toward B2.

**(2) Five key findings, ranked by reliability.**
1. *Grounded twins produce training-valid data and grounding beats quantity* (RialTo 90% vs 10%, 2403.03949). Most reliable — every perspective accepts it; it is the cluster's load-bearing empirical floor. Supported by all; challenged by none.
2. *The fidelity→forward-transfer map is non-monotone in at least one regime, so the cluster's "invert better = transfer better" recipe is unproven for manipulation* (Lower-Fidelity Sim2Real 2207.10821 vs Real-to-Sim GS 2511.04665). High reliability on the *fact*, open on *generalization*. Supported by Academic, Historian; challenged by the card's own H1 optimism.
3. *The closed loop and the amortized net are already demonstrated; only the round-trajectory / clutter / diff-sim-supervision residuals are open* (Arcadia 2512.00076, SLAT-Phys 2603.23973, CASHER 2412.01770; now also APIC 2606.03355 amortized-calibration and MOSIV 2603.06022 multi-object sysID). Reliable and adversarially confirmed. Supported by Skeptic; challenged (in scope) by Academic's "the measurement is the novelty."
4. *Inversion throughput, not inversion quality, is the deployment-binding constraint* (Re3Sim 60.5s/object, D-REX ≥20 demos/object mass-only). Reliable from deployment evidence. Supported by Practitioner, Economist; under-weighted by the card's quality-centric thesis.
5. *Recovered models historically fail open-loop and need online hedging; the cluster survives only because B3/D already hedge* (Real-is-Sim 60 Hz correction 2504.03597, Phys2Real inverse-variance weighting 2510.11689). Reliable as a pattern, interpretive as a prediction. Supported by Historian; implicitly contradicted by B1/B4's open-loop framing.

**(3) Hidden cross-perspective connection.** The Practitioner's throughput complaint, the Economist's "money moves to the eval/selection layer," and the Academic's offline-twin-ranker (B1-H2) are the *same finding* seen three ways: once the joint-inversion engine is commoditized (Economist) and per-object inversion is unaffordable at scale (Practitioner), the highest-value object becomes a *cheap predictor that ranks twins by expected forward transfer without inverting them all* (Academic's H2). B1-H2 is therefore not a minor sub-hypothesis — it is the cluster's economically and operationally central deliverable, and it depends entirely on Finding #2 (a monotone law) being true. The cluster's value chain collapses to: *if the law is monotone (H1), the offline ranker exists (H2), which is the only thing that makes per-object inversion affordable at fleet scale.*

**(4) Actionable insight — DE-RISK, do not yet COMMIT.** The cluster is coherent and the shared bet is sound as a bound, but it is currently *over-committed to inversion-as-sufficiency* and *under-committed to the one experiment that gates everything*. De-risk by running B1-H1 first as the cluster's keystone: a controlled fidelity-degradation sweep on contact-rich manipulation (Single-View Mesh for Robotics 2505.17966 graded Chamfer 2mm/5mm/1cm → grasp-transfer SR over 12 recon models on YCB-Video + Aria), regressing forward r on reconstruction error and comparing the *sign* against the navigation counter-example. Concrete go/no-go: if r is monotone in fidelity on manipulation, commit to B1-H2 (the offline twin-ranker) as the lead publication and fold B2/B3/B4 in as the throughput/data-engine/law extensions; if r is flat or non-monotone, the "invert-first" thesis is descriptive not predictive, and the commit shifts to B2's amortized throughput play (which pays off regardless of the law) with the cluster reframed around cost, not ceiling. Either way, add an observation-sufficiency / identifiability pre-test (active excitation) before any recovery claim, since the blind spot makes both B2 and B4 vulnerable to non-unique recovery on passive video.

**(5) Frontier question that would change everything.** *Is the recovered $(\phi^\star,\psi^\star)$ even identifiable from the data you can afford to collect — and if not, is there a fidelity threshold above which forward transfer saturates so that perfect inversion is unnecessary?* If forward transfer saturates at a *modest* fidelity (a knee in the law), the entire cluster inverts: the expensive joint-inversion engines are over-engineering, the cheap generative-cousin route (WorldComposer/Digital Cousins, 2604.15805/2410.07408) is sufficient, and the real research object becomes *finding the knee per object class* rather than maximizing fidelity. That single curve shape decides whether B1/B4 (push fidelity up) or B2 (push cost down) is the right decade-long bet.

---

#### Prompt 4 — Peer Review (independent)

*Reviewer stance: skeptical robotics faculty. Grounded in Sim2Real.md lines 335-653 (Cluster B: B1 reconstruction-fidelity, B2 amortized sysID, B3 twin-as-data-engine, B4 learned constitutive law). The cluster is genuinely well-built; the over-reach is concentrated in how the briefing rounds "shown once, on one task, in one paper" up to "demonstrated / settled."*

---

##### 1. Confidence scores (1-10 reliability, per key finding)

**Finding 1 — Grounded twins produce training-valid data; grounding beats quantity (RialTo 90% vs 10%). "Most reliable, challenged by none." → 6/10.**
The mechanism is real and the direction is right, but the briefing over-states it as the unchallenged keystone. The 90-vs-10 number is a *single ablation on a single task* (the real drawer) in one paper. The card itself does not treat this as settled: B3-H3 proposes "replicating RialTo's 90%-vs-10% *across object classes*" as a falsifiable hypothesis, with the explicit falsifier "enough generic-asset data closes the gap → quantity substitutes for grounding." A claim the card lists as still-to-be-tested cannot be the briefing's "challenged by none" anchor. Reliable as a directional prior, oversold as established fact.

**Finding 2 — The fidelity→transfer map is non-monotone in at least one regime (2207.10821 vs Real-to-Sim GS). → 5/10.**
The narrow factual claim ("non-monotone in *at least one* regime") is well-supported and is the most intellectually load-bearing observation in the cluster. But its evidential base is a *single navigation paper*, and the card's own VR-Robo row (2502.01536) is flagged inside B1 as "the constructive counterpoint to Lower-Fidelity Sim2Real on the same nav regime (high-fidelity 3DGS+mesh *does* give strong nav transfer)." So the one counter-example is itself contested *within navigation* by another row in the same table. The finding survives as "one documented non-monotone instance exists," but the briefing leans on it to undercut H1 optimism cluster-wide, which the evidence does not license. Lower than its narrative weight suggests.

**Finding 3 — The closed loop and amortized net are already demonstrated; only round-trajectory/clutter/diff-sim-supervision residuals are open (Arcadia, SLAT-Phys, CASHER, APIC, MOSIV). → 8/10.**
Strongest finding. It is adversarial against the cluster's own novelty, and the card text corroborates it line-by-line: Arcadia "states B3's H1 falsifier verbatim," CASHER is "the in-KH instance of B3's deployment-grounded fold-back," SLAT-Phys is "the feed-forward, single-pass, learned-decoder, explicit-parameter amortized predictor the bet proposes." The residual-whitespace framing (round-trajectory, clutter, diff-sim supervision) is faithful to what the cards concede. Minor deduction: APIC/MOSIV are introduced in the briefing but not in the card I reviewed, so I cannot verify they are correctly characterized — they are asserted, not grounded.

**Finding 4 — Inversion *throughput*, not quality, is the deployment-binding constraint (Re3Sim 60.5s/object, D-REX ≥20 demos). → 5/10.**
The two cost numbers are real and correctly cited (Re3Sim "60.5s per object" in the B3 table; D-REX "≥20 demos/object" throughout B2). But "binding constraint" is an *inference about deployment economics*, not a measured result — no paper in the cluster runs the experiment that would show throughput dominates quality at scale (e.g., a fixed-budget study trading object-count against per-object fidelity). It is a plausible Practitioner/Economist judgment dressed as a finding. The honest status is "two suggestive cost data points," not "the binding constraint."

**Finding 5 — Recovered models historically fail open-loop and need online hedging; the cluster survives because B3/D hedge (Real-is-Sim 60Hz, Phys2Real inverse-variance weighting). → 5/10.**
"Historically fail open-loop" is an unquantified sweeping generalization carried by the Historian voice. The two supports are real (Real-is-Sim's 60Hz correction "exists precisely because uncorrected twin error compounds"; Phys2Real inverse-variance weighting), but they are *existence-of-hedging*, not *demonstrated-failure-without-hedging*. And the claim is internally contradicted by the cluster's own open-loop successes the briefing does not reconcile: RialTo (91% pose-rand real, open-loop), VR-Robo (100% Easy/Hard zero-shot nav, open-loop), Re3Sim (>58% zero-shot, open-loop per object). The finding is directionally defensible but the briefing should have netted it against these counter-instances.

**Mean of the five: (6 + 5 + 8 + 5 + 5) / 5 = 5.8. Lowest: 5.**

---

##### 2. Weakest link

Tie at 5/10 across Findings 2, 4, 5; the **most consequential** weak link is **Finding 4 ("throughput is the binding constraint")** because the entire "actionable insight" pivots commit-weight toward B2 on the strength of it, and the "hidden cross-perspective connection" declares B1-H2 the "economically and operationally central deliverable" on the same premise.

What would verify it: a single fixed-deployment-budget experiment that varies the trade between *number of objects inverted* and *per-object inversion fidelity*, and measures downstream real-world policy SR at the scene level — i.e., does coarse-but-fast inversion of N objects beat fine-but-slow inversion of N/k objects at equal wall-clock? No paper in the cluster runs this. Until it exists, "throughput is binding" is a hypothesis, not a finding, and the commit-toward-B2 recommendation rests on an unproven economic claim. (Runner-up weak link: Finding 2's monotone-law dependency — the briefing's own central deliverable B1-H2 "depends entirely on Finding #2 being true," yet Finding #2's only direct evidence is a non-monotone counter-example. Verify by running B1-H1, which the briefing already correctly names as the keystone go/no-go.)

---

##### 3. Bias check — which perspective over-dominated

**The Economist/Practitioner axis over-dominated** (call it "balanced-leaning-Economist"). The synthesis's center of gravity — "money moves to the eval/selection layer," "throughput not quality," "commit toward B2's amortized throughput play," "the highest-value object is a cheap predictor that ranks twins" — is a cost-and-deployment argument. It is the most quotable thread and it drives all three of the briefing's load-bearing recommendations (DE-RISK, the hidden connection, the frontier question's "push cost down" pole). The Academic voice (the offline twin-ranker, the monotone-law measurement) is present but is repeatedly *folded into* the Economist conclusion ("the same finding three ways"). The Skeptic is well-served (Finding 3 is genuinely adversarial). The Historian is invoked but thinly evidenced (Finding 5). The under-represented voice is the one that would resist the Economist framing: see Missing Perspective.

---

##### 4. Missing perspective — a 6th angle that would change conclusions

**The control / closed-loop-robustness theorist (the policy's-eye view).** Cluster B is framed entirely as an *open-loop inversion-fidelity* problem: invert reality well, and forward transfer follows. But a control theorist would point out the briefing's own Finding 5 already hints that what actually matters is whether the *deployed feedback policy* is robust to the residual sim-real gap, not how small that gap is in nominal-trajectory terms. This reframes the whole cluster: if a reactive/closed-loop policy (visual servoing, RL with online correction, MPC) is insensitive to moderate inversion error, then the "monotone fidelity→r law" the briefing makes economically central is measuring the wrong quantity — it correlates fidelity with *open-loop rollout* transfer, which is exactly the regime that fails (Finding 5), while real deployments close the loop. Under this lens the frontier-question "knee" is not a property of the *object's inversion* at all but of the *policy's feedback gain*, and the right decade-bet is neither B1 (push fidelity up) nor B2 (push cost down) but "co-design the inversion budget with the policy's closed-loop sensitivity." This angle would demote B1-H2 (the offline open-loop twin-ranker) from "central deliverable" to "answers the wrong question," and it directly contradicts the briefing's commit recommendation. Its absence is the single biggest gap.

---

##### 5. Overall grade + top fixes

**Grade: B+.**

This is a strong, unusually self-critical briefing. Its best move — Finding 3, conceding that the cluster's headline mechanisms are already demonstrated and that surviving novelty is three *unrun measurements* — is exactly the discipline most strategy docs lack, and the DE-RISK-not-COMMIT posture with a concrete B1-H1 keystone experiment is correct and well-specified. It does not reach A-range because three of five findings (2, 4, 5) are rounded up from thin or contested evidence to declarative "findings," and the two pillars that carry every recommendation — "throughput is the binding constraint" (unmeasured) and "a monotone law exists" (contradicted by its own sole counter-example) — are the *least* reliable claims in the set, not the most. A briefing should not load its commit-weight onto its weakest links.

**Top fixes, in priority order:**
1. **Demote Findings 4 and 5 from "findings" to "hypotheses"** and label the missing experiments. "Throughput is binding" needs the fixed-budget object-count-vs-fidelity study; "open-loop models historically fail" needs to be netted against RialTo/VR-Robo/Re3Sim's open-loop successes or it is selectively cited.
2. **Resolve the Finding-2 self-contradiction.** The briefing makes B1-H2 economically central *and* says it depends on a monotone law, while its only monotonicity evidence is a *non*-monotone counter-example that the card's own VR-Robo row further disputes. State plainly that B1-H2's value is conditional on B1-H1 returning monotone, and do not call it "central" before that go/no-go runs.
3. **Add the closed-loop-policy perspective (Missing #4).** If feedback robustness, not inversion fidelity, governs deployed transfer, the monotone-law program and the offline twin-ranker may be optimizing the wrong objective; the briefing's commit recommendation should be hedged against this.
4. **Flag APIC/MOSIV as asserted, not grounded.** They appear in Finding 3 but not in the reviewed card; either cite them into the card or mark them unverified.
5. **Soften Finding 1 to match the card.** RialTo's 90-vs-10 is one ablation on one task that the card itself lists as a hypothesis to replicate; "challenged by none" overstates it.
