### B1 (Direction review)

Topic: the card's First-principles bet, verbatim — "Across rigid/articulated/deformable object classes there is a **monotone, measurable fidelity→r law** — degrading reconstruction error in controlled steps predicts forward r per object class — and the joint-inversion advantage is **widest on deformables** ([[2511.04665|Real-to-Sim GS]] 0.901 vs [[2511.04831|Isaac Lab]] 0.237 on rope, vs 0.915 vs 0.649 push-T), the regime [[2207.10821|Lower-Fidelity Sim2Real]]'s navigation counter-example does *not* cover. Falsifiable: if forward r is insensitive to reconstruction error (flat law) *or* the deformable Δr equals the rigid Δr, the arrow does not flip and one number generalizes."

#### Prompt 1 — Five Perspectives

**1. PRACTITIONER (builds/deploys policies daily).**
- *Core position*: The thing that actually decides deployment is not whether a monotone fidelity→r curve exists in aggregate; it is per-scene reconstruction cost and whether the twin survives the *control-dynamics* term, which the card itself concedes is a separate residual ([[2512.19562|REALM]]'s "misaligned control dynamics", H5). A law that needs a graded fidelity sweep × N object classes × forward rollouts to fit is itself a real-robot data-cost problem of the kind it claims to escape.
- *Strongest supporting evidence*: [[2511.04665|Real-to-Sim GS]] is per-scene 3DGS+physics fitting (the card flags "per-scene cost"); [[2505.17966|Single-View Mesh for Robotics]] shows most single-view recon models take "tens/hundreds of seconds per scene" and miss the 2 mm target (median ~5 mm YCB, ~1 cm Aria) → ~50% grasp transfer. The fast cheap routes ([[2604.15805|WorldComposer]] r=0.91 from one panorama, [[2512.16881|PolaRiS]] <20 min/scene r=0.9) all hit r≈0.9 with *light* physics — suggesting the marginal r past "good enough appearance" is small in practice.
- *Only-this-perspective tells you*: The bet's dependent variable (forward sim-real correlation r) is itself expensive to measure — every point on the fidelity→r curve needs paired real rollouts. Budget the real-robot rollout count *before* committing; if the law needs >~6 fidelity steps × 3 classes × paired real evals, the experiment costs more than the policy it would rank.

**2. ACADEMIC (knows the peer-reviewed literature).**
- *Core position*: The literature already says fidelity→transfer is non-monotone and task-dependent — the card's own [[2207.10821|Lower-Fidelity Sim2Real]] (lower fidelity → 100% Spot nav vs 40-67% high-fidelity) is the canonical counter-example, and the newer alphaxiv hit 2602.17101 (Burde et al., CTU) finds reconstruction fidelity has *negligible* effect on grasp success once pose is accurate. So the live science is not "does a monotone law exist" (the evidence says often not) but "in which regime (deformable, contact-rich) does it hold," which is exactly the narrowed H1/H4 framing.
- *Strongest supporting evidence*: 2602.17101 benchmarks 12 recon methods (NeRF/SDF/photogrammetry) on YCB-V and concludes "reconstruction artifacts significantly decrease grasp-pose candidates but have a negligible effect on grasping performance given an accurately estimated pose" — pose error dominates. Counterweight in the card's favor: [[2511.04665|Real-to-Sim GS]]'s joint ablation (removing color *or* physics collapses r) and [[2605.26638|HyperSim]] (75%→95% from reconstruction fidelity) show fidelity *does* bind in soft-body/contact regimes.
- *Only-this-perspective tells you*: The bet must be re-scoped from "monotone law exists across all classes" (the literature already partly refutes this) to "the fidelity→r slope is steep on deformables/contact-rich and flat on rigid-pose-dominated grasping" — that contrast (steep-deformable vs flat-rigid) is the publishable, defensible claim and it is *the inverse symmetry* of H4, not H4's "widest Δr" framing.

**3. SKEPTIC (thinks it's wrong or solved).**
- *Core position*: The whitespace is thinner than claimed. 2602.17101 already ran the controlled reconstruction-fidelity → grasp-success sweep across 12 models, and [[2508.11117|Robot Policy Eval (Sim2Real)]] + the brand-new 2606.10366 ("Practical Recipe for Improving Sim-and-Real Correlation," Tsinghua, Jun 2026) already supply the sim-real correlation protocol *and* find "proxy gaps are dominated by simulator-level fidelity rather than object-level task mismatch" — i.e. the headline conclusion the card wants is partly already in print.
- *Strongest supporting evidence*: 2606.10366 reports REALM Spearman ρ=0.700→0.875 after light post-training and explicitly finds a *non-monotone* relationship in adaptation-data scale (Tune-10 optimal, Tune-20 degrades) — a non-monotonicity result already published in this exact sim-real-correlation lane. The card's own table has 6 papers reporting r≈0.9 ([[2502.08645|Re3Sim]] 0.924, [[2512.16881|PolaRiS]] 0.9, [[2604.15805|WorldComposer]] 0.91, [[2603.01151|D-REX]], [[2512.19562|REALM]] 0.92) — the r>0.9 number is saturated, not novel.
- *Only-this-perspective tells you*: The card conveniently ignores that "fidelity → r" is confounded by *which fidelity axis* (geometry vs appearance vs physics vs control). 2602.17101 shows geometry-fidelity is nearly irrelevant for rigid grasping once pose is right; the card's joint-ablation evidence is from *one* paper ([[2511.04665|Real-to-Sim GS]]) on *one* soft-body task pair. A single-paper ablation is not a "law."

**4. ECONOMIST (follows money/incentives).**
- *Core position*: Real-to-sim is where the compute/data-cost arbitrage lives — every lab wants a cheap simulator that *certifies* a policy without real rollouts, so the incentive is to publish an r>0.9 headline and a benchmark, not a careful per-class law. The card is swimming with that current (benchmark-chasing: [[2512.19562|REALM]], [[2508.11117|Robot Policy Eval]], 2606.10366 all race to be "the" sim-real-correlation benchmark), which makes the *law* (a negative-results-prone, expensive, less citable artifact) the contrarian, under-incentivized bet.
- *Strongest supporting evidence*: [[2604.08544|SIM1]]'s framing is explicitly economic — "27× cost reduction, 6.8× throughput" vs real data; the whole deformable-data-engine cluster ([[2604.08544|SIM1]], [[2606.15338|SimWeaver]] $0.03/traj) sells on cost. NVIDIA ([[2508.11117|Robot Policy Eval]]) and the Isaac Lab ecosystem profit from the "buy better sim" narrative the card's first-principle attacks.
- *Only-this-perspective tells you*: The deformable-widest-Δr finding (H4) is the *commercially* load-bearing one — it tells a vendor exactly where the forward simulator (Isaac Lab 0.237 on rope) is worthless and a real-to-sim twin is mandatory. Frame the paper as "where you *must* invert vs where forward DR suffices" (a buy/build map), not as an abstract law; that is the version that gets cited by the people with GPUs.

**5. HISTORIAN (has seen prior waves).**
- *Core position*: This is the pixel-vs-latent and high-vs-low-fidelity cycle replaying. The field swung "more realism → better transfer" (early DR/photoreal), then [[2207.10821|Lower-Fidelity Sim2Real]] (2022) swung it back ("less is more for nav"); the card is the next half-swing ("fidelity binds, but only where forward engines fail — deformables"). Every prior wave ended not at a universal law but at a *regime map* (DR helps vision-gap, hurts dynamics-gap; latent WMs win sample-efficiency, lose long-horizon fidelity).
- *Strongest supporting evidence*: The DR/sim2real history (Tobin DR → automatic DR → [[2207.10821|Lower-Fidelity Sim2Real]]'s reversal) and the Dreamer pixel→latent lineage both resolved into "it depends on the gap you're closing." The card's H5 (control-dynamics is a separate residual) is the *same* lesson the dynamics-vs-appearance split taught a decade ago.
- *Only-this-perspective tells you*: Bet on the *regime map*, not the *single law*. Historically the universal-monotone-law framings ("fidelity always helps") got falsified within ~2 years; the survivors were the conditional findings ("for contact-rich deformables, joint inversion is necessary because forward solvers can't model the dynamics"). Write the conditional, not the universal — the card's falsifier ("deformable Δr = rigid Δr") is the *real* contribution, the monotone law is the decoration.

#### Prompt 2 — Contradiction Map

1. **Direct clashes.**
   - *Academic/Skeptic vs the card's "monotone law"*: 2602.17101 says reconstruction fidelity is *negligible* for rigid grasping given accurate pose; the card's H1 predicts a *monotone* fidelity→r on contact manipulation. These collide on rigid objects — the Skeptic's read is that the law is already shown flat where pose dominates.
   - *Practitioner vs Economist on H4*: Practitioner says deformable joint-inversion is too per-scene-expensive to deploy ([[2505.17966|Single-View Mesh]] timing, per-scene 3DGS); Economist says H4 (deformable Δr widest) is precisely the commercially valuable, must-invert finding. Same finding, opposite verdicts on whether it ships.
   - *Skeptic vs everyone on novelty*: Skeptic says r>0.9 and the correlation protocol are saturated (6+ papers, 2606.10366); the card's Thesis says REALM only "names" the gap and the law is unmeasured. They clash on whether 2606.10366's "simulator-fidelity dominates" already *is* the law.

2. **Strongest vs weakest.**
   - *Strongest: ACADEMIC.* It is the only one that both cites the refuting evidence (2602.17101, [[2207.10821|Lower-Fidelity Sim2Real]]) *and* salvages a sharper, defensible claim (steep-deformable / flat-rigid slope contrast) — it improves the bet rather than just attacking or cheerleading.
   - *Weakest: ECONOMIST.* Its incentive story is real but does not touch whether the law is *true*; "where you must invert" is a reframing of H4, not independent evidence. Useful for positioning, weak for de-risking.

3. **The one question that resolves the biggest contradiction.**
   - *Does the fidelity→r slope differ by object class with a measured sign-flip — steep/monotone on deformable+contact-rich, flat/negligible on rigid-pose-dominated — when the SAME controlled fidelity-degradation protocol is applied to both?* This is the single experiment that adjudicates Academic vs Skeptic: if the slope is flat on rigid (matching 2602.17101) but steep on deformable (matching [[2511.04665|Real-to-Sim GS]]'s 0.901-vs-0.237 rope gap), the conditional law survives and the universal one dies — which is the publishable result.

4. **What every perspective agrees on (likely true).**
   - The fidelity→transfer relationship is *regime-/object-class-dependent, not one universal monotone curve* — and the regime where joint real-to-sim inversion is genuinely necessary is **deformable / contact-rich** manipulation, because forward engines (Isaac Lab 0.237 on rope) model that dynamics worst. Every perspective lands here from a different direction (Academic: literature; Skeptic: confound by axis; Economist: must-invert map; Historian: regime-map precedent; Practitioner: that's where per-scene cost is justified).

5. **The blind spot none addressed.**
   - *Causal direction / spurious correlation.* Nobody questioned whether a fitted fidelity→r curve is even *causal*: r could rise with fidelity because higher-fidelity twins are built from objects/tasks that are intrinsically easier to transfer (selection bias across the 12 recon models / object set), not because fidelity *causes* transfer. The whole bet treats reconstruction error as an intervenable cause, but the proposed test (regress realized r on recon error across heterogeneous objects) measures a correlation, not an intervention — only a *within-object* controlled degradation (same object, artificially degraded) isolates causation, and the card mixes that (H1's "controlled steps") with cross-object regression (H2's ranker) without flagging the inferential gap.

#### Prompt 3 — Synthesis Briefing

**CEO summary.** B1 bets that reconstruction fidelity is the binding ceiling on forward sim-to-real transfer and that this obeys a measurable, per-object-class monotone law whose advantage is widest on deformables. The mechanism the bet rests on (joint appearance+physics inversion) is consensus and the r>0.9 headline is saturated across 6+ papers, so the *only* live, defensible contribution is the **conditional slope contrast** — fidelity binds steeply on deformable/contact-rich tasks but is nearly flat on rigid-pose-dominated grasping — and even that is now partially pre-empted: a Feb-2026 benchmark (2602.17101) already shows reconstruction fidelity is negligible for rigid grasping once pose is accurate, and a Jun-2026 paper (2606.10366) already reports "simulator-fidelity dominates" plus a non-monotonicity result in the same lane. The bet survives only if re-scoped to the deformable regime with a within-object controlled degradation that isolates causation; as a universal monotone-law claim it does not survive a literature check.

**5 key findings, ranked by reliability.**
1. *Fidelity→transfer is regime-dependent, not a universal monotone law.* (Supported: Academic, Skeptic, Historian, Practitioner; evidence [[2207.10821|Lower-Fidelity Sim2Real]] nav reversal + 2602.17101 rigid-grasp negligibility. Challenged by: the card's H1 universal framing only.) Highest reliability.
2. *The deformable regime is where joint inversion is genuinely necessary.* (Supported: all five; evidence [[2511.04665|Real-to-Sim GS]] 0.901 vs Isaac Lab 0.237 rope, [[2604.08544|SIM1]] 76% vs 0%, [[2606.15338|SimWeaver]]. Challenged by: Practitioner only on *deployability/cost*, not on truth.) High.
3. *The r>0.9 sim-real-correlation headline is saturated and not itself novel.* (Supported: Skeptic, Economist; evidence [[2502.08645|Re3Sim]] 0.924, [[2512.16881|PolaRiS]] 0.9, [[2604.15805|WorldComposer]] 0.91, [[2512.19562|REALM]] 0.92, 2606.10366. Challenged by: card Thesis, which says the *law* not the number is the contribution.) High.
4. *Reconstruction fidelity is negligible for rigid grasping once pose is accurate.* (Supported: Skeptic, Academic; evidence 2602.17101 directly. Challenged by: [[2511.04665|Real-to-Sim GS]] joint ablation + [[2605.26638|HyperSim]] 75→95% — but those are soft-body/contact, not rigid grasp, so the two coexist as the slope-contrast.) Medium-high (single benchmark, sim-only, pose-confounded).
5. *Control-dynamics misalignment is a separate residual fidelity alone won't close (H5).* (Supported: Practitioner, Historian, card itself; evidence [[2512.19562|REALM]] names "misaligned control dynamics". Challenged by: none directly — but untested, so reliability capped.) Medium.

**Hidden cross-perspective connection.** The Academic's "steep-deformable / flat-rigid slope contrast," the Economist's "must-invert vs forward-DR-suffices map," and the Historian's "regime map not universal law" are *the same object viewed three ways*. The publishable artifact is a 2-axis regime map (object-class × dominant-gap-term) on which the fidelity→r slope is annotated — steep where forward physics fails (deformable/contact) and flat where pose dominates (rigid grasp). That map is simultaneously the scientific finding (Academic), the commercial deliverable (Economist), and the historically-durable form (Historian) — none of them named it as the unifying output, but all three point at it.

**Actionable insight for the researcher: DE-RISK, do not commit as written.** The universal monotone-law framing is partially pre-empted and partly already-refuted; the deformable-conditional slope contrast is still whitespace. Concretely: (1) drop "monotone law across all classes" as the headline; reframe to "the fidelity→r slope flips sign-of-importance by object class — steep on deformable/contact-rich, flat on rigid-pose-dominated." (2) Cite and beat 2602.17101 head-on as the rigid-side null result your deformable-side positive result contrasts against. (3) *Next experiment* (the one that resolves the biggest contradiction): take a *single* deformable object and a *single* rigid object; apply the **identical** controlled reconstruction-degradation protocol (degrade the same Chamfer steps 2 mm→5 mm→1 cm per [[2505.17966|Single-View Mesh for Robotics]]'s grading) to *the same object* (within-object, so it isolates causation, closing the blind spot); regress realized forward r (paired real rollouts) on degradation for each; the deliverable is the *difference of slopes*. If the deformable slope is steep-monotone and the rigid slope is flat (matching 2602.17101), the conditional law is established and the result is publishable; if both are flat, the arrow does not flip and B1 should be dropped into A-cluster (forward DR suffices) territory.

**Frontier question that would change everything.** Is reconstruction fidelity an *intervenable cause* of forward transfer, or a *proxy* for an underlying object-transferability that a cheaper signal (e.g. contact-richness / deformability score) predicts directly — i.e. could you skip building the twin entirely and route "invert-vs-forward-DR" off a property of the object alone? If yes, the entire real-to-sim inversion engine becomes optional for a large slice of objects, and the bet's premise (inversion is the ceiling) inverts.

#### Prompt 4 — Peer Review (independent)

*Independent skeptical pass on the B1 synthesis briefing. Card under review: `Mechanism/Sim2Real.md` lines 339–418. Claims spot-checked against `_KnowledgeHub_/` notes and, for the two pre-emption papers, against arXiv directly.*

**Verification done before grading.**
- 2602.17101 (Burde, Burget, Sattler, CTU, "Benchmarking the Effects of Object Pose Estimation and Reconstruction on Robotic Grasping Success") **verified on arXiv**: it genuinely concludes "reconstruction artifacts significantly decrease the number of grasp pose candidates but have a negligible effect on grasping performance given an accurately estimated pose," and is **simulation-only** (grasps generated on reconstructed meshes, executed on the ground-truth mesh). The briefing's Finding #4 caveat ("single benchmark, sim-only, pose-confounded") is accurate. **This paper is nowhere in the vault** (`grep` finds it only inside the prior analyst review part), so the threat it poses to B1 is real and currently uncited.
- 2606.10366 ("A Practical Recipe Towards Improving Sim-and-Real Correlation for VLA Evaluation," Wang et al.) **is in KH**. Its KH note supports "simulator-fidelity dominates object-asset mismatch" ("the inherent fidelity of the simulator's core mechanisms ... is a more critical determinant ... than minor discrepancies in object assets"). **Caveat the briefing glosses:** the paper's non-monotonicity is in *fine-tuning-data amount* (Tune-10 optimal, Tune-20 degrades), **not** in *reconstruction-fidelity→r*. The briefing's CEO summary phrase "a non-monotonicity result in the same lane" is a category slip — it is the same *benchmark lane*, not the same *axis* B1's law lives on. Minor, but it slightly over-states pre-emption.
- The card (lines 339–418) cites **neither** 2602.17101 nor 2606.10366; 2606.10366 appears only in cluster C (line 689). So the briefing's "DE-RISK, cite-and-beat 2602.17101 head-on" is correctly diagnosing a genuine blind spot, not a strawman.
- Deformable numbers (rope 0.901 vs Isaac Lab 0.237; push-T 0.915 vs 0.649; SIM1 76% vs 0%) and the saturation set (Re3Sim 0.924, PolaRiS 0.9, WorldComposer 0.91, REALM 0.92) all **verify against their KH notes**.

---

**1. CONFIDENCE SCORES (1–10 reliability).**

1. **Fidelity→transfer is regime-dependent, not a universal monotone law — 9/10.** Two independent counter-examples from opposite regimes (2207.10821 nav reversal; 2602.17101 rigid-grasp negligibility, now arXiv-verified) plus four-perspective agreement. The only reason it is not 10 is that both counter-examples are *outside* the deformable regime B1 actually wants to claim, so they refute the *universal* framing without touching the *conditional* one — which is exactly the briefing's own re-scope. Well-handled.

2. **The deformable regime is where joint inversion is genuinely necessary — 7/10.** Numbers verified, and the forward-engine failure on rope (0.237) is a striking, mechanism-plausible gap. Docked because the load-bearing rope number is **a single paper, single task-pair** (2511.04665), and SIM1's 76%-vs-0% is a *data-engine* result, not a fidelity→r measurement. "Genuinely necessary" is supported as *forward-engines-model-deformables-worst*; it is not yet supported as *reconstruction-fidelity-binds-monotonically-on-deformables*, which is the actual bet. The briefing slightly conflates "inversion is necessary here" with "the monotone law holds here."

3. **The r>0.9 headline is saturated and not novel — 8/10.** Five distinct papers at r≈0.9–0.92, each verified in KH. Strong. Held below 9 only because saturation of *the number* does not by itself establish saturation of *the per-class slope law* — the card's Thesis rebuttal ("the law, not the number") is a legitimate distinction the briefing acknowledges but then under-weights. The finding is true; its implication for novelty is the contested part.

4. **Reconstruction fidelity is negligible for rigid grasping once pose is accurate — 6/10.** Now arXiv-verified, so higher than a blind read would warrant. But every weakening qualifier is real and the finding self-flags them: **single benchmark, simulation-only, and pose-confounded** (the result is conditional on "accurate pose" — it says pose error dominates, not that fidelity is globally irrelevant). It cannot bear weight as a general rigid-side null; it is one sim-only data point pointing the right direction.

5. **Control-dynamics misalignment is a separate residual (H5) — 4/10.** The briefing itself caps this: "Challenged by: none directly — but untested." It rests on REALM *naming* "misaligned control dynamics" as a phrase, not on any measurement that it is a *separable, irreducible* term after joint appearance+physics inversion. Plausible and consistent with a decade of dynamics-vs-appearance precedent (Historian), but currently a hypothesis wearing a finding's clothes.

Mean of scores: (9+7+8+6+4)/5 = **6.8**. Lowest: **4**.

**2. WEAKEST LINK.** Finding #5 (H5, control-dynamics residual). To verify it you need the exact experiment the card's H5 already specifies but no cited paper has run: **correlation with appearance+physics inversion alone vs. + control-alignment, on the same twin/task, reporting the residual r closed by the control term.** Until that ablation exists, "separate residual inversion won't close" is an assertion. A cheaper interim check: confirm REALM's "misaligned control dynamics" is a *quantified* contributor to its residual r (0.92→0.88 on default/unseen), not merely a named failure mode — the KH note suggests it is named, not decomposed.

A secondary weak link inside the otherwise-strong Finding #1/#4 axis: the rigid-side null (2602.17101) is **pose-confounded by construction**. The paper's own framing is "pose error dominates *given accurate pose, fidelity is negligible*." That is not the same as "fidelity is flat for rigid grasping" — it is "fidelity is flat *in the regime where pose is already solved*." The briefing's reframe ("flat on rigid-pose-dominated") mostly absorbs this, but the proposed within-object experiment must **hold pose fixed at ground truth** or it will re-confound, and the briefing does not say so.

**3. BIAS CHECK.** The **Academic over-dominated**, by the synthesis's own admission ("Strongest: Academic"). Four of five findings, the entire re-scope ("steep-deformable / flat-rigid slope contrast"), the headline experiment, and the grade-A taste-call all trace to the Academic's literature read. The Economist contributed only positioning (its own contradiction-map entry calls it "weakest for de-risking"), and the Practitioner's hard constraint — *the dependent variable r is itself expensive to measure, every fidelity-step needs paired real rollouts* — is the most decision-relevant input in the whole packet and is **demoted to a single parenthetical** in Finding #2 ("Challenged by: Practitioner only on deployability/cost"). That is a real imbalance: a literature-correct experiment that is unaffordable is not a good experiment, and the briefing lets the Academic's correctness crowd out the Practitioner's feasibility. Verdict: **Academic-dominant, with the Practitioner under-weighted on cost.**

**4. MISSING PERSPECTIVE — the Statistician / causal-inference reviewer.** The analyst's own blind-spot note (#5: causal direction / selection bias across the 12 recon models) is the single most consequential observation in the packet, and it was raised and then *not* promoted into the five findings. A dedicated causal-inference perspective would change the conclusion, not just decorate it: the whole bet treats reconstruction error as an **intervenable cause**, but H2's twin-ranker regresses realized r on recon error across **heterogeneous objects** — pure observational correlation, vulnerable to the confound that high-fidelity twins are built from intrinsically-easier-to-transfer objects. The briefing's "within-object controlled degradation" fix is the right instinct, but only a Statistician would (a) demand it be the *primary* deliverable rather than step (3), (b) flag that H1 (within-object, causal) and H2 (cross-object, observational) **measure different estimands and cannot share one "law,"** and (c) insist pose be clamped to ground truth so the rigid-side comparison is not re-confounded. This sixth voice would downgrade H2 from "offline twin-ranker" to "descriptive correlation pending an instrument," materially shrinking the claimed contribution.

**5. OVERALL GRADE — A-.**

This is a strong, intellectually honest briefing. It does the rare thing: it actively hunts for the evidence that kills its own bet (2602.17101, 2606.10366), verifies-adjacent the numbers, ranks findings by reliability with the soft ones explicitly capped, and converges five perspectives on a sharper, *more defensible* claim than the card made (steep-deformable / flat-rigid slope contrast). The "regime map, not universal law" synthesis is correct and the de-risk recommendation is actionable and falsifiable. Held back from A/A+ by three fixable defects.

**Top things to fix (in priority order):**
1. **Promote causation to a finding, not a footnote.** The within-object vs cross-object distinction (H1 causal / H2 observational) is the real frontier; make the within-object controlled-degradation experiment **the headline deliverable**, and state explicitly that H2's twin-ranker is an observational claim that cannot be validated by the same data. This is the single most important fix.
2. **Clamp pose to ground truth in the rigid-side comparison, and say so.** 2602.17101's null is *conditional on accurate pose*; the slope-contrast experiment must fix pose or it inherits the same confound it is trying to contrast against.
3. **Re-weight the Practitioner's cost constraint into the go/no-go.** Before committing, budget the paired-real-rollout count (≈ fidelity-steps × object-classes × rollouts). If the law-fitting experiment costs more real-robot time than the policy it would rank, the bet is self-defeating regardless of whether the law is true — this belongs in the actionable insight, not a parenthetical.
4. **Tighten one over-claim:** 2606.10366's non-monotonicity is in *fine-tuning data amount*, not *reconstruction fidelity*; downgrade "non-monotonicity result in the same lane" to "in the same benchmark lane (on a different axis)" so the pre-emption claim is exact.
