### B2 (Direction review)

Topic: the bet that an **amortized observation→parameter network**, trained on differentiable-sim rollouts, recovers constitutive parameters for *unseen objects in clutter at zero per-object demos* and reproduces the gradient-sysID-vs-DR-on-OOD advantage ([[2603.01151|D-REX]] 9–10/10 vs DR 4–9/10 below the DR support) **on objects it never saw**, with the falsifiable measurement being the *SR-vs-parameter-distance frontier* for {amortized inference} vs {per-object gradient sysID} vs {DR}.

Novelty stress-test: alphaxiv `discover_papers` worked (after fixing the keywords-array JSON shape). Surfaced two threats the card never cites — **PhyPush (2605.26284)** and **UniPhy (2505.16971)** — plus HyperDynamics (2103.09439) and a 2024 visuo-tactile interactive-perception line (2411.09020 / 2405.12634). `get_paper_content` confirmed PhyPush and UniPhy details. Verdict below: **partially-pre-empted**.

#### Prompt 1 — Five Perspectives

**1. PRACTITIONER (builds/deploys policies daily).**
- *Core position*: The card's binding constraint at deploy time is not "per-object optimization is slow" — it is that the amortized net needs a *segmented, clutter-robust per-object observation* it does not get for free, and a *robot interaction* to disambiguate mass/friction that vision alone cannot resolve. The "zero per-object demos, single forward pass" framing quietly assumes away the very interaction step that makes [[2603.01151|D-REX]]/ASID (2404.12308) work.
- *Strongest supporting evidence*: D-REX's 4.8–12.0% mass error and 86% SR come *after* ≥20 interaction demos/object; [[2510.11689|Phys2Real]]'s 57%-vs-23% OOD edge is *online per-object* estimation. PhyPush (2605.26284) shows the practitioner's reality directly — it needs *one physical push* per object even to beat an F/T-sensor baseline OOD; pure-vision feed-forward (SLAT-Phys 2603.23973) recovers stiffness/density but is silent on *mass and kinetic friction*, the two parameters that actually decide grasp/push success.
- *Only this perspective tells you*: Build the clutter+segmentation pipeline and the "one cheap probing action" budget *first*; the inverse-map net is the easy 20%. If your amortized net still needs one push per object (it almost certainly will for mass/friction), your headline "zero per-object demos" is false advertising — reframe to "one-probe amortized" before a reviewer does it for you.

**2. ACADEMIC (knows the peer-reviewed literature).**
- *Core position*: The "per-object gradient recovery is the unit of sysID" assumption the card claims to challenge is *already substantially retired* in the literature — amortized/meta-learned inverse maps over object physics predate the card by years (HyperDynamics 2103.09439, ICML'21-era), and 2024–2026 has a dense feed-forward cluster (SLAT-Phys, [[2410.20357|Dynamics-as-Prompts]], PhyPush). The card's own Related table concedes this with SLAT-Phys ("the feed-forward, single-pass, learned-decoder, explicit-parameter amortized predictor the bet proposes — realized").
- *Strongest supporting evidence*: [[2506.10133|Offline Domain Randomization]] proves DR *is* MLE of a parameter distribution (weak+strong consistency, α-informativeness) — the card uses this as its "offline corpus carries the inverse map" license, but the same theorem is double-edged: it says a *well-tuned DR already concentrates on the true parameter*, which is exactly the H2 falsifier ("DR matches amortized estimation across the frontier"). UniPhy (2505.16971) shows a unified cross-material constitutive model, but its *inference still optimizes a fresh latent per instance* — confirming the card's narrow claim (forward-pass amortization is not yet standard for the constitutive vector) while refuting the broad one (cross-object inverse maps are novel).
- *Only this perspective tells you*: Your contribution is not "amortization exists" — that ship sailed. It is the *exact triple conjunction nobody has co-reported*: forward-pass amortized **explicit** mass+friction+stiffness, **in clutter (N>1)**, with a **robot-policy-SR-vs-parameter-distance frontier vs DR**. Write the paper as that conjunction or it reads as SLAT-Phys+PhyPush stapled together.

**3. SKEPTIC (thinks it is wrong or already solved).**
- *Core position*: The whitespace is an artifact of slicing the axis finely enough to exclude the papers that already did it. PhyPush (2605.26284) is a physics-guided Transformer that, from a single push, jointly estimates **mass and friction**, is trained on Isaac Lab rollouts, generalizes to **unseen objects and surfaces** (47 real configs), and **beats an F/T-privileged baseline OOD** on both parameters — that is the H1+H4 core (learned inverse map, multi-parameter, unseen objects, OOD-robust) minus only the word "clutter" and minus the robot-SR plot.
- *Strongest supporting evidence*: PhyPush's OOD result (physics-guided loss cuts friction error >28% vs data-driven; friction R² 0.58 vs baseline-collapse 0.15 on unseen mass) is precisely the "learned beats the strong baseline off-distribution" story the card sells as its frontier. SLAT-Phys already amortizes the **explicit Young's+density+Poisson** vector in ~9.9 s/object (~121× over NeRF2Physics). Between PhyPush (mass+friction, interaction) and SLAT-Phys (stiffness, vision), the *full constitutive vector is already amortized across two papers*. The card's reserved novelty — clutter + diff-sim-rollout supervision + SR-frontier — is the leftover, not the thesis.
- *Only this perspective tells you*: The H2 falsifier is the real threat, not clutter. [[2506.10133|Offline Domain Randomization]]'s consistency theorem means a *properly offline-fit DR* converges to the true parameter — so "amortized beats DR off the support" may vanish once the DR baseline is itself offline-MLE-fit rather than hand-tuned. If you don't fix the DR baseline to ODR, you are beating a strawman.

**4. ECONOMIST (follows money/incentives).**
- *Core position*: This direction is cheap and legible to fund precisely because the expensive parts are externalized: differentiable sims (DiffTaichi/MPM, Warp), a frozen 3D-generative encoder (TRELLIS, à la SLAT-Phys), and a sim like Isaac Lab are all donated infrastructure. The publication incentive favors the *forward-pass-amortization narrative* (fast, scalable, demo-friendly) over the *honest one-probe-per-object* reality, because "zero-demo" sells and "needs a push" does not.
- *Strongest supporting evidence*: The benchmark stack the card leans on — [[2402.08191|THE COLOSSEUM]] (14 perturbation factors, 30–50% single-perturbation SR drop, R̄²=0.614 sim↔real) and [[2306.15668|Physion++]] (~50–55% near-chance) — are *forward-perturbation / passive-video* harnesses, not parameter-recovery+robot-SR loops. The card admits this repeatedly ("does not by itself close the parameter-recovery loop"). That mismatch is an incentive tell: the available benchmarks reward the cheap-to-measure half (perturbation SR drop), not the costly half (real-robot SR vs parameter distance) the thesis actually rides on.
- *Only this perspective tells you*: The grant-winning version and the true version diverge here. Budget the *real-robot clutter rig* (the one thing no donated benchmark gives you) as the gating cost; if you cannot afford ~N-object real-SR sweeps, you will publish the recovery-error half and the "beats DR on OOD SR" headline will be sim-only — which is the field's most over-saturated, least-believed claim.

**5. HISTORIAN (has seen prior waves).**
- *Core position*: This is the third turn of the "amortize the per-instance optimization" wheel. HyperDynamics (2103.09439) already meta-learned object/agent dynamics via hypernetworks conditioned on interactions in 2021; PAC-NeRF/NCLaw (2304.14369) made per-scene system-ID the unit; the card now proposes amortizing it again. The "learned inverse map vs explicit optimization" swing mirrors the pixel-vs-latent world-model cycle and the sysID-vs-DR oscillation: each generation claims the amortized/learned version subsumes the optimization version, and each time the optimization version survives as the high-accuracy fallback.
- *Strongest supporting evidence*: The card's own structure recapitulates the lineage — gradSim (2104.02646, 2021 root) → ASID (2404.12308, few-demo) → D-REX (2603.01151, per-object) → SLAT-Phys/PhyPush (2026, amortized) is a textbook accuracy-vs-amortization Pareto march. The Risks section already encodes the historical lesson ("fall back to a per-object D-REX loop where the amortized estimate is low-confidence") — i.e. the optimization fallback never dies.
- *Only this perspective tells you*: Don't bet on amortization *replacing* per-object recovery — that framing loses every cycle. Bet on the *hybrid routing* (amortized prior → confidence gate → per-object refinement), which is where every prior wave actually settled. The defensible, citable contribution is the *gate*: when does the forward-pass estimate suffice and when must you pay for the gradient loop, quantified on the SR-vs-parameter-distance frontier.

#### Prompt 2 — Contradiction Map

1. **Direct clashes.**
   - *SKEPTIC vs ACADEMIC/the card on whitespace*: Skeptic says PhyPush (mass+friction, unseen objects, OOD-beats-baseline) + SLAT-Phys (explicit stiffness vector, feed-forward) already cover H1+H4; the card/Academic say no single paper co-reports forward-pass + clutter + robot-SR-frontier + diff-sim supervision. They clash on whether "co-reported in one paper" is a real contribution or a slicing artifact.
   - *PRACTITIONER vs the card's "zero per-object demos"*: Practitioner says mass/friction is not vision-resolvable, so an interaction (one push, à la PhyPush) is mandatory — directly contradicting H1's "single zero-demo forward pass." The card hedges this only for Dynamics-as-Prompts (online multi-episode), not for its own headline.
   - *SKEPTIC vs the card on the DR baseline*: Skeptic + Economist say [[2506.10133|Offline Domain Randomization]]'s consistency theorem implies an *offline-fit* DR may erase the OOD gap (the H2 falsifier), whereas the card cites the same paper as *support* for amortization. Same theorem, opposite conclusions.

2. **Strongest vs weakest.**
   - *Strongest: SKEPTIC.* It is the only perspective grounded in a freshly-surfaced, on-axis 2026 paper (PhyPush) that the card does not cite, and it correctly identifies the load-bearing falsifier (the ODR-fit DR baseline) rather than the decorative one (clutter). Its claims are checkable against PhyPush's reported OOD R² numbers.
   - *Weakest: ECONOMIST.* Directionally right about externalized infra and benchmark-mismatch incentives, but it argues about *why* the narrative is shaped, not *whether the bet is true* — it cannot by itself move the novelty verdict, only the framing.

3. **The one question that resolves the biggest contradiction.**
   *When the DR baseline is itself fit by offline-domain-randomization MLE ([[2506.10133]]) rather than hand-tuned, does an amortized inverse map still beat it on real-robot SR as test parameters move off the DR support — and does the amortized net need ≥1 probing interaction (PhyPush-style) to do so?* This single experiment resolves the whitespace clash (is the gap real or an artifact of a weak DR baseline) AND the zero-demo clash (forward-pass vs one-probe) at once.

4. **What every perspective agrees on (likely true).**
   That a **learned/amortized inverse map generalizing to unseen objects already exists and works** (HyperDynamics, SLAT-Phys, Dynamics-as-Prompts, PhyPush all demonstrate it), so the *raw mechanism* is not novel; the only live question is the *specific conjunction* (clutter + explicit multi-parameter + real-robot SR-vs-parameter-distance frontier vs a properly-fit DR baseline) and whether it survives a strong baseline. Every lens treats the per-object gradient loop as a *surviving fallback*, never as fully replaced.

5. **The blind spot (addressed by none).**
   *Identifiability and observation-action coupling.* No perspective asks whether mass and friction are even *jointly identifiable* from the chosen observation without an *informative* exploratory action — PhyPush needs a *carefully-aligned CoM push*; ASID uses *Fisher-information-guided* exploration (echoed by Sampling-Based SysID 2505.14266). An amortized net conditioned on a *passive* or *arbitrary* observation may be regressing a prior, not recovering a parameter — and in clutter, occlusion makes the per-object observation actively non-identifiable. The card's H-set measures recovery error but never tests *identifiability* as a function of the action that produced the observation.

#### Prompt 3 — Synthesis Briefing

1. **CEO summary (nuance, not headline).** B2's underlying mechanism — a learned, amortized observation→parameter inverse map that generalizes to unseen objects — is no longer whitespace: 2026 work the card does not cite (PhyPush 2605.26284: amortized mass+friction from one push, unseen objects, beats an F/T-privileged baseline OOD) plus the card's own SLAT-Phys (feed-forward explicit Young's+density+Poisson) already co-cover the H1/H4 core across two papers. What remains genuinely uncontested is the *conjunction the card reserves*: forward-pass amortization **in clutter (N>1)**, supervised by **differentiable-sim rollouts**, evaluated on a **real-robot SR-vs-parameter-distance frontier against a properly offline-fit DR baseline**. That is a defensible but narrow paper, and it is exposed on two flanks — the "zero per-object demos" claim (mass/friction likely needs one probing action) and the DR baseline (an ODR-MLE-fit DR may erase the OOD gap the thesis sells).

2. **Five key findings, ranked by reliability.**
   1. *The amortized-inverse-map mechanism is already demonstrated for unseen objects (incl. explicit mass+friction).* Most reliable — PhyPush + SLAT-Phys + Dynamics-as-Prompts + HyperDynamics. Supported by SKEPTIC, ACADEMIC, HISTORIAN; not seriously challenged. Downgrades the bet from "new mechanism" to "new conjunction."
   2. *Mass and kinetic friction are not reliably vision-only; an informative interaction is needed.* High reliability — PhyPush needs a push; ASID/Sampling-Based-SysID need active exploration; SLAT-Phys is silent on friction. Supported by PRACTITIONER, HISTORIAN, the blind-spot; challenged only by the card's H1 "single zero-demo forward pass."
   3. *The H2 OOD-beats-DR claim hinges on the DR baseline's strength.* Medium-high — [[2506.10133]]'s consistency theorem cuts both ways. Supported by SKEPTIC, ECONOMIST; the card cites the same paper as support, so this is contested by construction.
   4. *Clutter (the reserved novelty) is a real, under-served gap — but it is a robustness axis, not a thesis.* Medium — supported by the absence of any surfaced clutter+per-object-physics+robot-SR paper (closest, Emerging Extrinsic Dexterity 2603.09882, does clutter manipulation without explicit per-object parameter recovery); challenged by PRACTITIONER (segmentation/occlusion may make it ill-posed).
   5. *The right contribution is a hybrid amortized-prior→confidence-gate→per-object-refinement, not replacement.* Medium — argued by HISTORIAN, foreshadowed in the card's own Risks; speculative because no surfaced paper yet reports such a gate on the SR-vs-parameter-distance frontier (this is the cleanest whitespace).

3. **Hidden cross-perspective connection.** PRACTITIONER's "you need a probing action" and the BLIND SPOT's "identifiability requires an informative action" and HISTORIAN's "amortization never fully replaces optimization" are the same fact wearing three hats: amortization can supply a *prior over the inverse map* but parameter *recovery* (vs prior-regression) still demands an information-bearing interaction — which is exactly why every prior wave settled on a hybrid. The card treats the probing action as an inconvenience to minimize ("zero-demo"); it is actually the load-bearing variable, and the SR-vs-parameter-distance frontier is most novel when re-cast as SR-vs-(parameter-distance × interaction-budget).

4. **Actionable insight for the researcher: DE-RISK, do not COMMIT as written.** Three concrete moves. (a) *Reframe the thesis from "zero per-object demos" to "one-probe amortized inference vs the per-object gradient loop in clutter"* — this survives the PRACTITIONER/identifiability flank and still beats PhyPush on the clutter + robot-SR + diff-sim-supervision axes it leaves open. (b) *Fix the DR baseline to offline-domain-randomization MLE ([[2506.10133]]), not hand-tuned DR*, before claiming any OOD-frontier win — otherwise Finding 3 sinks the headline in review. (c) *Make the deliverable the confidence gate* (amortized prior → when-to-refine), per HISTORIAN/Finding 5, which is the one piece no surfaced paper reports. **Concrete next experiment** = the Prompt-2-Q3 study: on a 2–4-object clutter rig, plot real-robot grasp/push SR vs test-parameter distance from the prior mean for {amortized one-probe inference} vs {per-object D-REX-style gradient loop} vs {offline-MLE-fit DR}, sweeping mass+friction across and beyond the DR support, reporting SR, wall-clock at matched compute, and a recovery-vs-identifiability check (recovery error as a function of the probing action's informativeness). If amortized one-probe tracks the gradient loop's SR past the DR support and the gate routes the low-confidence tail to refinement, the bet is alive; if ODR-fit DR closes the gap, drop to the gate-only contribution.

5. **Frontier question that would change everything.** Is there a *task-agnostic, minimal-information probing action* (a single learned push/poke) such that one amortized forward pass conditioned on it recovers the **full constitutive vector for every object in a cluttered scene** at the *information-theoretic floor* of interactions — i.e. can the per-object loop be replaced not by "more vision" but by "one optimally-informative action shared across the scene"? If yes, B2 stops being a recovery-accuracy paper and becomes an *active-amortized-identifiability* paper, which is the version that out-runs PhyPush/SLAT-Phys instead of overlapping them.

---

#### Prompt 4 — Peer Review (independent)

*Reviewer stance: skeptical Stanford robotics faculty. Direction under review: B2 — Amortized Differentiable System-ID (Sim2Real.md L419-493). Briefing under review: the "DE-RISK, do not COMMIT" synthesis.*

The briefing is sharp and mostly correct in its instinct (downgrade from "new mechanism" to "new conjunction"; fix the DR baseline; make the gate the deliverable). But it earns its caution by leaning on one factual error and one strawman, and it under-credits how much of its own punchline the card already states. Verified against the vault and the live literature below.

---

##### 1. Confidence scores (1-10 reliability, with justification)

| # | Finding | Score | Justification |
|---|---|---|---|
| **1** | Amortized-inverse mechanism already demonstrated for unseen objects → downgrade to "new conjunction" | **8/10** | Mechanism existence is solid — verified: **PhyPush 2605.26284 is real** (Fujii et al., U. Nottingham/Bologna; mass+friction from one push, unseen objects, beats an F/T-privileged baseline by >10% OOD), and **SLAT-Phys 2603.23973 is in KH** with an explicit Young's+density+Poisson decoder. The downgrade is right. **But the briefing's framing is wrong on a load-bearing fact**: its CEO summary says "the card does not cite" SLAT-Phys — SLAT-Phys appears **5×** in the doc, is a full Related-table row, and anchors H1 and H4. Half the "no longer whitespace" punch is the card's *own* citation re-narrated as a discovery. Docked 2 points for that. |
| **2** | Mass/friction is not vision-only; an informative interaction is needed | **9/10** | Most reliable finding, and independently verified: PhyPush *needs a push*; ASID (2404.12308, in KH) and Sampling-Based SysID (2505.14266) need active exploration; SLAT-Phys recovers Young's/density/Poisson, **not** friction or kinetic mass-from-vision (confirmed in the KH note — silent on friction). Physically correct: kinetic friction and mass are unobservable without an interaction that excites them. Only reason it isn't 10: see the strawman caveat in §2 below — the card already conditions on "interaction history," so the *fact* is right but its framing as an un-addressed flank is half-built. |
| **3** | H2 OOD-beats-DR hinges on DR-baseline strength; ODR-MLE-fit DR may erase the gap | **8/10** | The strongest *strategic* catch. 2506.10133's consistency result genuinely cuts both ways — if DR is the MLE of the offline parameter distribution and you fit it properly offline, the "estimation beats DR off-support" thesis can collapse to "estimation ≈ DR" exactly where the paper sells its advantage. The card cites the same paper as *support*, so this is contested by construction and the briefing is right to weaponize it. Slightly <9 because the OOD claim is about extrapolation *beyond* the DR support, where even an MLE-fit DR has no mass — so the gap may survive at the tail; the briefing concedes this in its "past the DR support" hedge. |
| **4** | Clutter is a real under-served gap but a robustness axis, not a thesis | **6/10** | Directionally right and the most important deflation in the briefing — clutter is what the card *reserves* as novelty (H3, Risk-3), yet H3 is a wall-clock/compute argument (N optimizations vs one pass), which is an efficiency axis, not a science axis. The "no surfaced clutter+per-object-physics+robot-SR paper" claim rests on absence-of-evidence; Emerging Extrinsic Dexterity (2603.09882) is **not in KH** and I could not confirm it does what the briefing implies, so the comparison is soft. Medium is correct. |
| **5** | Right contribution is a hybrid amortized-prior → confidence-gate → per-object-refinement | **6/10** | The most defensible *paper*, the least defensible *claim of novelty*. The hybrid is well-motivated (it is the standard amortized-inference-then-refine pattern, and the card's Risk-1 already proposes "fall back to a per-object D-REX loop where the amortized estimate is low-confidence" — i.e., the gate is **already in the card's Risks**). So the briefing's "the one piece no surfaced paper reports" is partly true (no one reports it *on the SR-vs-parameter-distance frontier*) and partly re-selling the card's own fallback as the headline. Speculative by the briefing's own admission. |

**Mean of the five: 7.4. Lowest: 6.**

---

##### 2. Weakest link

**Finding 4 (clutter), tied with the briefing's central reframe.** Two specific things would verify or kill it:

1. **The "no surfaced paper" absence claim** needs a positive search, not absence-of-evidence. Emerging Extrinsic Dexterity (2603.09882) is not in KH and is invoked only as "closest." Verify: does any 2025-26 paper do **explicit per-object parameter recovery on ≥2 objects in a cluttered scene with a robot-SR endpoint**? Until that search is run and comes back empty, "cleanest whitespace" is asserted, not shown.

2. **The strawman in the headline reframe.** The briefing's hidden-connection and Actionable-(a) treat "you need a probing action" as a flank the card ignores ("the card treats the probing action as an inconvenience to minimize"). **The card's First-principle explicitly conditions the amortized map on `(RGB-D, interaction history)`** (L434) and "zero per-object demos" means *zero per-object gradient-optimization demos*, not zero interaction. So the card already grants the interaction; the briefing's strongest rhetorical move (recast the frontier as SR-vs-(parameter-distance × interaction-budget)) is a *refinement* of the card, not a correction of a blind spot. That recast is genuinely valuable — but it should be sold as "sharpen H1's interaction-budget axis," not "the card missed that you need an interaction." Verifying this just requires re-reading L424/L434, which is why it's low-cost and high-leverage to fix.

---

##### 3. Bias check — which perspective over-dominated

**SKEPTIC over-dominated, with HISTORIAN as co-pilot.** Findings 1, 3, and the whole "downgrade / de-risk / don't commit" arc are SKEPTIC moves; the "every prior wave settled on a hybrid, amortization never replaces optimization" thread is HISTORIAN. The briefing even flags its own collapse: it states PRACTITIONER, BLIND-SPOT, and HISTORIAN are "the same fact in three hats" — that is not five independent perspectives converging, it is **one argument wearing three name-tags and being counted thrice** for reliability (Findings 2 and 5 both lean on it). PRACTITIONER appears only as a deflationary "occlusion makes it ill-posed" caveat; ECONOMIST shows up once (Finding 3) and never costs out the *one-time training corpus* that is the whole efficiency case (generating a wide differentiable-sim object/parameter corpus is not free, and that compute is the real comparator against the per-object loop). ACADEMIC is invoked only to certify Finding 1. **Net: a skeptic-historian consensus that is internally consistent because it is largely one voice.** That makes the briefing *safe* but possibly over-cautious — it never lets an optimist argue that "amortized prior + one cheap shared probe" could be a genuinely new operating point rather than a re-run of the hybrid.

---

##### 4. Missing perspective (a 6th angle that would change conclusions)

**The IDENTIFIABILITY / EXPERIMENTAL-DESIGN theorist** — distinct from "you need a probing action," which only says *that* an interaction is needed. The missing voice asks: *which* parameters are jointly identifiable from a single shared push in clutter, and *what is the Cramér-Rao / Fisher-information floor* on recovery error as a function of probe informativeness? This reframes the whole bet:

- It would show that **mass and friction are partially confounded from a single planar push** (the push excites a product/ratio, not both independently) — so PhyPush's "one push → mass *and* friction" may be recovering a constrained 1-D manifold, not the full 2-vector, and a clutter scene with one shared probe is even more degenerate. That is a *physics* obstruction the briefing's efficiency framing (N loops vs one pass) never sees.
- It directly upgrades the frontier question (which the briefing already gestures at with "information-theoretic floor of interactions") from a slogan into the actual contribution: **active-amortized-identifiability** — co-design the probe to maximize Fisher information about the constitutive vector, then amortize the inverse map conditioned on that optimal probe. Sampling-Based SysID (2505.14266, FIM-guided exploration) and ASID are exactly the seeds for this and are *already in the card's table* but unused by the briefing.

If this perspective is right, the conclusion shifts: the de-risked paper is **not** "one-probe amortized inference vs the gradient loop in clutter" (briefing's Actionable-a) but "*optimally-informative* shared-probe amortized identifiability with a per-parameter identifiability budget" — which is a stronger, more novel paper than the gate-only fallback the briefing retreats to.

---

##### 5. Overall grade + top fixes

**Grade: B+.**

A strong, actionable peer-synthesis that correctly deflates the card's biggest over-reach (the "new mechanism" claim and the hand-tuned-DR baseline) and lands the single most useful experiment (the SR-vs-parameter-distance frontier with an offline-MLE-fit DR baseline). It loses the A band on three faults a Stanford committee would catch immediately: (i) a **factual error** — claiming the card "does not cite" SLAT-Phys when it cites it 5×; (ii) a **strawman** — presenting "you need a probing action" as an un-addressed flank when the card conditions on interaction history; (iii) **perspective collapse** — counting one skeptic-historian argument as three independent reliability votes.

**Top fixes, in order:**
1. **Correct the SLAT-Phys claim.** It is cited. The honest statement is "PhyPush 2605.26284 is the *only* genuinely uncited 2026 paper that closes the H1/H4 mechanism, and it does so *with* an interaction" — that is still a strong downgrade and it is true.
2. **Drop the strawman; keep the recast.** Don't say the card ignores the probe. Say: sharpen H1 by making interaction-budget an explicit axis of the frontier (SR-vs-parameter-distance × interaction-budget). The recast is the briefing's best idea; it survives without the false setup.
3. **Add the identifiability floor before the gate.** The confidence gate (Actionable-c) is sound but it is *already the card's Risk-1 fallback*. The differentiated contribution is the Fisher-information/identifiability analysis of a shared probe in clutter — promote that from the frontier-question slogan to the headline deliverable, and the paper out-runs PhyPush instead of overlapping it.
4. **Cost the amortization corpus.** Before claiming amortization "buys" anything over the per-object loop, the briefing must put the one-time differentiable-sim corpus-generation compute on the same axis as N per-object optimizations — otherwise H3's efficiency argument is unfalsifiable.

---

### B3 (Direction review)

Topic: B3 bets that a **per-task object-grounded real→sim→real fold-back loop, *gated on a B1 reconstruction-fidelity check*, improves monotonically across N rounds where the *ungated* loop drifts, and that the per-round gain scales with the reconstruction-fidelity exchange rate** ([[2504.03597|Real-is-Sim]] 30 sim ≈ 30 real rising with twin fidelity). Falsifiable: if the ungated loop never drifts, or the exchange rate is flat in fidelity, the conditional-fold-back mechanism buys nothing over [[2512.00076|Arcadia]]'s one-pass result.

#### Prompt 1 — Five Perspectives

**1. PRACTITIONER** (deploys policies daily)
- *Core position*: The twin-as-data-engine half is real and already paying rent — [[2403.03949|RialTo]]'s 90%-vs-10% (target vs generic twin) and [[2605.26638|HyperSim]]'s 75%→95% with 35 demos are the kind of deltas a lab actually feels. But the *closed-loop multi-round* half is an operations nightmare: every round means a fresh real deployment campaign, a re-reconstruction (Re3Sim ~51.5s background / 60.5s per object is best-case; a kitchen-scale re-scan is hours), retraining a VLA, and re-deploying — the wall-clock per round is days, so "monotone across N rounds" is N×(deploy+scan+retrain), and the experiment dies on robot-time before it tests the hypothesis.
- *Strongest evidence*: [[2412.01770|CASHER]]'s +55% fold-back gain *only* materialized where SR was already ≤20% (rescue regime), and it was a *single* unsupervised pass — the practitioner reads this as "fold-back helps a broken policy once," not "fold-back compounds round over round." [[2504.03597|Real-is-Sim]] needed a *continuous 60 Hz correction* precisely because twin error compounds in seconds; that is the practitioner's lived proof that the ungated loop drifts.
- *Only this perspective says*: The fidelity gate is not a research nicety, it is the only thing standing between you and a twin that silently rots — but computing "did this fold lower sim-real r" requires a held-out real validation set *per round per object*, and that real-data cost is the hidden tax that makes the gate itself expensive. Budget the gate's data cost or the whole loop is unaffordable.

**2. ACADEMIC** (knows the peer-reviewed literature)
- *Core position*: The card is honest that the *headline* (closed loop > one-shot) is conceded to [[2512.00076|Arcadia]]; the live, publishable claim is narrow and correct — nobody has reported a *per-round SR trajectory on a per-task object twin with a fidelity gate*. But the literature is closing on the *trajectory* sub-claim faster than the card admits: iterative self-improving data engines now routinely report round-over-round curves.
- *Strongest evidence*: [[2503.22634|Empirical Analysis (Wei/Tedrake)]] already nails H4 (mixing-ratio optimum, physics-fidelity paramount for contact) and H2's spirit (sim-data value tracks physical fidelity, power-law scaling) on a *grounded* planar-push task; [[2604.13645|CFG-ADDA]] gives the mechanism (~50%-variance representation alignment, (0.016,0.3) band). So H2 and H4 are *substantiated background*, not open questions — the paper's novelty must live entirely in H5 (the gate) and H1's *per-round* framing.
- *Only this perspective says*: Re-label the contribution honestly. H2/H4 are confirmatory replications; H3 is a RialTo replication across object classes; the *only* genuinely new result is H5 (gated-vs-ungated drift). Write the paper as "an ablation that isolates *when* fold-back is safe," not as "closed loops beat one-shot" — the latter framing invites a desk-reject on Arcadia/CASHER priority.

**3. SKEPTIC** (thinks it's wrong or already solved)
- *Core position*: The whitespace is thinner than claimed. The card's own falsifier — "if the ungated loop never drifts, the gate buys nothing" — is the likely outcome, because the systems that fold back *already* implicitly filter (CASHER filters on task success; [[2510.20813|GSWorld]] uses DAgger on a *fixed* twin to avoid re-grounding drift entirely). The field may have already routed around the drift problem by *not re-grounding the twin every round*.
- *Strongest evidence*: **[[2509.23829|DexFlyWheel]] (NeurIPS 2025)** runs an explicit iterative self-improving flywheel, reports the per-round SR trajectory (16.5%→81.9% over i=1..3), and *Appendix A.5 extends to iteration 5 and characterizes the diminishing-returns curve* — i.e., the "improvement-as-trajectory-not-single-delta" framing of H1 is already in print. It even filters rollouts on success (a de-facto quality gate) and never drifts within 5 rounds. If a sim-only flywheel doesn't drift in 5 rounds, the burden is on B3 to show *deployment re-grounding specifically* drifts where DexFlyWheel doesn't.
- *Only this perspective says*: Your headline experiment may produce a *null* (ungated never drifts at N≤5), and that is a publishable result *only if* you frame it as "characterizing when re-grounding is dangerous," not as a method paper. Pre-register the null. The convenient omission is that *success-filtering already gates* — you must show a fidelity gate beats a success-filter, not just beats no-gate.

**4. ECONOMIST** (follows money/incentives)
- *Core position*: This narrative is bankrolled by the twin-data-engine industrial complex — [[2601.02078|Genie Sim 3.0]] (platform-scale, humanoid), [[2605.26638|HyperSim]] (Huawei CloudRobo), [[2506.18088|RoboTwin 2.0]] (731-object benchmark) all profit from "synthetic beats real," because they sell the synthetic. "Closed-loop self-improvement" is the natural next slide in that deck because it implies the data engine compounds for free.
- *Strongest evidence*: Every cited fold-back result is from a group that owns a twin platform (CASHER/RialTo = Agrawal/Gupta's real-to-sim line; Arcadia = a full-lifecycle *framework* paper). The incentive is to claim the loop is *unconditionally* self-improving (more compounding = more platform value); B3's contrarian bet (it drifts without a gate) is *anti-vendor*, which is good science but means no big lab will fund the negative result.
- *Only this perspective says*: The benchmark substrate is the moat. [[2506.18088|RoboTwin 2.0]] gives you the object-twin generation + unseen-object SR protocol *for free*, but it covers only the SR leg — there is **no named benchmark for round-over-round monotone-vs-drift** (the card concedes this at H1's NOTE). Whoever defines that benchmark owns the citation annuity; the method is commoditized, the *eval protocol* is the defensible asset. Ship the protocol.

**5. HISTORIAN** (has seen prior waves)
- *Core position*: This is the DAgger / iterated-data-aggregation story wearing a 3DGS costume. DAgger (2011) already taught the field that naively aggregating self-generated data drifts unless you correct distribution shift; the "fidelity gate" is the 2026 re-discovery that *aggregation needs a corrector*. The pixel-vs-latent and grounded-vs-generic-twin cycle is the same model-based-RL "is my model good enough to plan in" question Dreamer-lineage work has circled for a decade.
- *Strongest evidence*: [[2504.03597|Real-is-Sim]]'s continuous 60 Hz correction is literally the "your learned/reconstructed model is wrong and must be perpetually corrected" lesson from model-based RL; [[2503.10118|RSR Loop]] re-grounds *parameters* per round (the sysID-flavored ancestor), B3 re-grounds *assets+policy* (the richer, drift-prone descendant). History says: the richer the thing you re-ground, the worse the compounding error — which *supports* B3's drift premise but also warns the gate will need to be conservative to the point of rarely accepting a fold.
- *Only this perspective says*: Watch for the swing-back. Every "closed loop self-improves" wave (model-based RL, GAN self-training, RL from self-play) ended with "...only with a stability mechanism (target networks, replay, KL trust regions)." Your fidelity gate *is* that stability mechanism — frame it in that lineage and the contribution reads as inevitable-in-hindsight (good), but also means reviewers will ask why a trust-region-style soft gate isn't enough vs your hard B1 reconstruction check.

#### Prompt 2 — Contradiction Map

**(1) Direct clashes.**
- **SKEPTIC vs the CARD (H1 novelty)**: Card says the per-round monotone-improvement *trajectory* is "untouched"; Skeptic counters that [[2509.23829|DexFlyWheel]] already publishes a per-round SR trajectory (16.5%→81.9%, extended to i=5 with diminishing returns) — the *trajectory framing* is pre-empted, only *deployment-grounded re-grounding* drift remains genuinely open.
- **SKEPTIC vs PRACTITIONER on drift**: Practitioner asserts the ungated loop *will* drift (Real-is-Sim's 60 Hz correction as proof). Skeptic asserts it *won't* at N≤5 because everyone already success-filters (DexFlyWheel, CASHER) — so the gate is redundant. They directly contradict on the central falsifier.
- **ACADEMIC vs CARD on contribution weight**: Card treats H2/H4 as live hypotheses with their own tests; Academic says they are *already settled* by [[2503.22634|Wei/Tedrake]] and [[2604.13645|CFG-ADDA]] and should be demoted to background — inflating them as "hypotheses" weakens the paper.

**(2) Strongest vs weakest.**
- *Strongest*: **SKEPTIC** — it is the only perspective anchored to a specific *named threat* (DexFlyWheel's already-published per-round trajectory + implicit success-gate) that directly attacks the central falsifier, and it reframes the likely outcome (a null) into a publishable plan. Evidence-bound and adversarial.
- *Weakest*: **ECONOMIST** — its insight (own the benchmark, vendors won't fund negative results) is shrewd and the benchmark-gap observation is real, but it's about *strategy around* the bet, not whether the bet is *true*; it cannot adjudicate the drift question.

**(3) The one question that resolves the biggest contradiction.**
> **Does a deployment-grounded fold-back loop that re-reconstructs the twin from imperfect real scans each round actually drift within N≤5 rounds when rollouts are already success-filtered — i.e., does a *fidelity gate* beat a *success filter*?** This is the Practitioner-vs-Skeptic crux. If success-filtering alone keeps it monotone (DexFlyWheel's regime), the gate is redundant and the bet collapses to a replication. If re-grounding-from-real-scans drifts where sim-rollout-aggregation does not, the gate is the whole contribution.

**(4) Universal agreement (likely true).**
Every perspective agrees the **twin-as-data-engine** premise is sound and already proven: grounding (not quantity) drives the target-twin advantage (RialTo 90-vs-10), and synthetic-from-a-grounded-twin has measurable real-demo value (Real-is-Sim 30≈30, HyperSim 75→95). H3 will replicate; the generative-capacity-of-a-grounded-twin first principle is safe.

**(5) Blind spot (none addressed).**
**What the fidelity gate's *false-accept / false-reject* behavior costs in closed loop.** B1 reconstruction-fidelity (sim-real r) is a *proxy* for "this fold improves policy SR" — nobody asked whether r-improvement actually predicts SR-improvement per fold. A gate that maximizes reconstruction r could *reject* folds that add useful action-distribution coverage (the very thing CASHER's deployment scan adds) and *accept* photorealistic-but-task-irrelevant re-grounds. The gate's *metric-validity* — does the gating signal correlate with the thing you actually want — is untouched by every perspective and is the real risk.

#### Prompt 3 — Synthesis Briefing

**(1) CEO summary.** B3 is a *good replication wrapped around one genuinely novel ablation*. The twin-as-data-engine thesis it rests on is settled and safe (RialTo, HyperSim, Real-is-Sim, RoboTwin 2.0), and its honest concession that the closed-loop headline belongs to Arcadia/CASHER is correct. The defensible novelty is *narrow*: an isolated study of whether a *fidelity-gated* fold-back stays monotone where an *ungated* one drifts, on a *per-task object* twin, over *successive* rounds. But that novelty is partially pre-empted on its weakest leg — [[2509.23829|DexFlyWheel]] already publishes the per-round improvement *trajectory* (and its saturation) for a sim-only flywheel, and existing fold-back systems already success-filter, so the live question shrinks to "does *deployment-grounded re-reconstruction* drift where *sim-rollout aggregation* does not, and does a reconstruction-fidelity gate beat a plain success filter?" Commit *only* if you reframe the paper around that crux and the gate's metric-validity, and pre-register the null.

**(2) Five key findings, ranked by reliability.**
1. *The twin-as-data-engine premise (H3) will replicate; grounding beats quantity.* — Supported by PRACTITIONER, ACADEMIC, HISTORIAN, and the universal-agreement point (RialTo 90-vs-10, HyperSim 75→95). Challenged by no one. **Highest reliability.**
2. *H2/H4 (exchange-rate scaling, mixing-ratio optimum) are already substantiated, not open.* — Supported by ACADEMIC (Wei/Tedrake, CFG-ADDA) and SKEPTIC; challenged by the CARD, which still lists them as hypotheses. Reliable that they'll confirm; unreliable as *novel contributions*.
3. *The per-round improvement-trajectory framing of H1 is partially pre-empted.* — Supported by SKEPTIC (DexFlyWheel's 16.5→81.9 over i=1..5). Challenged by the CARD ("untouched at object-twin scale"). The *object-twin + deployment-grounded* qualifier keeps a sliver open. **Moderate reliability of pre-emption.**
4. *The ungated loop may not drift at N≤5 because systems already success-filter — risking a null on the central falsifier.* — Supported by SKEPTIC; challenged by PRACTITIONER (Real-is-Sim 60 Hz correction = drift exists) and HISTORIAN (richer re-grounding → worse compounding). Genuine, unresolved — this is the experiment. **Moderate, contested.**
5. *The gate's metric-validity (does sim-real r predict per-fold SR gain?) is the unexamined risk.* — Supported by the blind-spot analysis; addressed by no perspective directly. Speculative but high-leverage. **Lowest reliability, highest novelty.**

**(3) Hidden cross-perspective connection.** PRACTITIONER ("the gate needs a per-round held-out real set, which is the hidden cost"), HISTORIAN ("the gate is the DAgger/target-network stability mechanism this wave always rediscovers"), and the BLIND SPOT ("does the gating signal even predict SR?") converge on one object: **the gate is simultaneously the contribution, the cost driver, and the unvalidated assumption.** The whole paper's fate rides on whether *reconstruction-fidelity r* is the right gating variable — if it isn't, the Practitioner's data cost is wasted, the Historian's stability mechanism gates on the wrong signal, and the Skeptic's null arrives. Everything routes through one untested correlation.

**(4) Actionable insight for the researcher: DE-RISK, then COMMIT to a narrowed version.** Do *not* commit to the card as written (H1's trajectory framing is exposed to DexFlyWheel; H2/H4 are replications). Instead:
- **Next experiment (the crux, cheap, decisive):** On *one* contact-rich per-task object twin (reuse RoboTwin 2.0's object-twin generation so the SR substrate is free), run the fold-back loop for N=5 rounds under three arms — (a) ungated, (b) success-filter only (the DexFlyWheel-style baseline), (c) B1-fidelity-gated — and report (i) the per-round unseen-object SR trajectory and (ii) **the per-fold correlation between sim-real r change and SR change** (the metric-validity test the blind spot exposes). This single 3-arm experiment resolves the Practitioner/Skeptic drift contradiction *and* tests whether the gate variable is even the right one, before you pay for a full N-round campaign.
- **Reframe**: title the paper around "*when* deployment re-grounding is safe / *what* signal predicts a safe fold," not "closed loops beat one-shot." Pre-register the null (per Skeptic). Demote H2/H4 to confirmatory background (per Academic). Position the round-over-round monotone-vs-drift *protocol* as the artifact (per Economist) since no named benchmark exists.

**(5) Frontier question that would change everything.** *Is reconstruction fidelity (sim-real r) actually the right gating variable, or does safe fold-back require gating on **action-distribution coverage gain** instead?* If the thing that makes a fold useful is the *new states/recoveries* the deployment scan adds (CASHER's +55% came from deployment coverage, not photorealism) rather than how photorealistic the re-reconstruction is, then a fidelity gate is gating on the wrong axis — and the real contribution is a *coverage-aware* gate, which reframes B3 entirely and reconnects it to the active-learning / DAgger lineage the Historian flagged.

---

#### Prompt 4 — Peer Review (independent)

*Reviewer stance: skeptical Stanford robotics faculty. Briefing reviewed against the B3 card (`Sim2Real.md` L494-573) and one external verification (DexFlyWheel `2509.23829`, KH-confirmed).*

**Verdict in one line.** The briefing is unusually honest and its central recommendation (de-risk via a 3-arm N=5 experiment, then commit to a narrowed version) is correct. But it leans on one external pre-emption (DexFlyWheel) harder than the evidence supports, and the synthesis is tilted toward the SKEPTIC voice. The real contribution survives the discount; the framing needs the surgery the briefing itself prescribes.

---

##### 1. CONFIDENCE SCORES (1-10 reliability)

**Finding 1 — Twin-as-data-engine premise (H3) replicates; grounding beats quantity. → 9/10.**
This is the safest claim in the briefing and the card is right to treat it as settled. RialTo 90-vs-10 and HyperSim 75→95 are direct, the mechanism (grounded twin samples are training-valid, not just test-valid) is first-principles sound, and no perspective dissents. Docked one point only because "grounding beats quantity" is asserted as a *main effect* (H3's design: grounded×volume factorial) that nobody in the cited corpus has actually run as a clean 2×2 — RialTo's 10% generic arm is at one volume, not "generic-asset data at any volume." The premise is safe; the specific factorial claim is mildly untested.

**Finding 2 — H2/H4 (exchange-rate, mixing-ratio) are already substantiated, not open. → 8/10.**
Correct and well-sourced. CFG-ADDA (2604.13645) gives a mechanism-backed mixing band (0.016, 0.3) and Empirical-Cotraining (2503.22634) gives the contact-task α-sweep; together they convert H4 from hypothesis to confirmation and H2's "value scales with fidelity" is half-shown by Real-is-Sim's exchange rate. The briefing's instruction to demote both to confirmatory background is the right editorial call. Held at 8 not 9 because H2 specifically ("exchange rate *rises with twin fidelity*") is NOT yet shown — Real-is-Sim measures the exchange rate at one fidelity; the *fidelity-derivative* is genuinely open. The briefing slightly over-collapses H2 into "substantiated" when only its level, not its slope, is established.

**Finding 3 — H1's per-round-trajectory framing is partially pre-empted (DexFlyWheel). → 6/10.**
This is the load-bearing external claim and it is *directionally* right but *over-stated in specifics*. I verified 2509.23829 in KH: DexFlyWheel does publish a rising-and-saturating self-improving-loop result (16.5%→81.9%) and it is a sim-based residual-RL flywheel — so "a self-improving loop already shows a trajectory that saturates" is true and the pre-emption is real. BUT (a) the KH note reports the *endpoints* 16.5/81.9, not the explicit per-round "i=1..5" curve the briefing quotes — that granularity is an extrapolation, not a documented figure; and (b) DexFlyWheel aggregates *simulation rollouts* and re-reconstructs nothing from deployment, so it does not touch the drift mechanism (re-grounding from imperfect real scans) that is B3's actual novelty. The briefing's own hedge ("sim-rollout aggregation," "the object-twin + deployment-grounded qualifier keeps a sliver open") is correct and is why the pre-emption shrinks the contribution rather than killing it. Scored 6 because the claim is true at the level it matters but is dressed in a precision the source does not support.

**Finding 4 — Ungated loop may not drift at N≤5 (null risk on the central falsifier). → 6/10.**
The single most important and most genuinely uncertain claim. The contradiction is real: PRACTITIONER (Real-is-Sim's 60 Hz correction proves uncorrected twin error compounds) vs SKEPTIC (existing fold-back systems already success-filter, so the naive ungated arm is a strawman nobody ships). Both are defensible from the card. The briefing is right that this is *the experiment* and right that the null is live. Capped at 6 because the briefing does not resolve the most damaging version of the SKEPTIC point: if the honest baseline is "success-filter," then arm (a) ungated is a strawman and the real contest is (b) success-filter vs (c) fidelity-gate — which the proposed 3-arm design *does* set up, but the briefing never states that the drift finding is only interesting if (c) beats (b), not if (c) beats (a). That gap is the difference between a result and an artifact.

**Finding 5 — Gate metric-validity (does sim-real r predict per-fold SR gain?) is the unexamined risk. → 5/10.**
Correctly identified as the highest-leverage, lowest-confidence item, and the Prompt-3 cross-perspective convergence (the gate is contribution + cost-driver + unvalidated assumption simultaneously) is the sharpest insight in the whole briefing. But it is rated 5 because it is *asserted as the crux without any evidence either way* — the briefing has no data, no cited result, and no prior that sim-real r tracks per-fold SR gain at the fold granularity. The frontier question (Prompt 5) actually undermines this finding: CASHER's +55% came from deployment *coverage*, not photorealism, which is positive evidence that r is the *wrong* gating variable. So Finding 5 is simultaneously the most important and the least substantiated; the briefing is honest about that, which is why it earns 5 and not lower.

**Mean = 6.8. Min = 5.**

---

##### 2. WEAKEST LINK

Finding 5 (gate metric-validity), at confidence 5. The entire paper, per the briefing's own Prompt-3 synthesis, routes through one untested correlation: *does a change in reconstruction-fidelity r predict the sign and size of per-fold SR change?* Nothing in the card or the corpus establishes this. **Specific information needed to verify:** a per-fold scatter of (Δ sim-real r) vs (Δ unseen-object SR) across rounds and across accepted/rejected folds — i.e., does the gating signal have non-trivial rank-correlation with the thing it is supposed to gate? This is cheap (it is a byproduct of the proposed 3-arm experiment, arm c logs r at every fold) and decisive. If that correlation is near zero, the contribution collapses to "a coverage-aware gate" per the frontier question — a *different* paper. The briefing should make logging this correlation a *primary* endpoint of experiment 1, not a secondary check; right now it is listed second ("(ii) the per-fold correlation"), and it should be (i).

---

##### 3. BIAS CHECK

**The SKEPTIC over-dominated.** Three of the five findings (2, 3, 4) are framed as SKEPTIC wins, the headline CEO summary adopts the SKEPTIC's "narrow the claim" posture wholesale, and the single external citation introduced (DexFlyWheel) is a SKEPTIC pre-emption. This produces a briefing that is *defensively excellent* but *offensively thin*: it is very good at saying what B3 cannot claim and comparatively quiet on what makes the surviving sliver worth a paper. The PRACTITIONER appears mainly as a cost-warning (the per-round held-out real set) and the ECONOMIST/ACADEMIC only to demote H2/H4. The ACADEMIC voice in particular is under-used on the *positive* side — there is a real theoretical contribution latent here (a stability condition for re-grounding loops: fold-back is contractive iff the gate's accept-region keeps the reconstruction operator's spectral effect below 1) that no perspective develops, because the synthesis was busy subtracting claims rather than sharpening the one that remains. Net: not balanced; tilt toward SKEPTIC by roughly one full voice.

---

##### 4. MISSING PERSPECTIVE

**The EXPERIMENTALIST / measurement-statistician.** Every finding turns on detecting *monotonicity vs drift over N≤5 rounds* on *success-rate* endpoints, and no perspective asks whether that is even statistically detectable. With per-task object twins and unseen-object SR, a single round's SR estimate has a binomial standard error of roughly ±10-15% at typical eval-set sizes (n≈30-50). A 5-point "monotone vs drifting" trajectory built on points that wide cannot distinguish a true drift from sampling noise — the headline falsifier (Finding 4) may be *unfalsifiable at N=5* not because the loop doesn't drift but because the experiment lacks the power to see it. This would change the conclusions: it makes "pre-register the null" insufficient (you also have to pre-register an effect size and a power analysis, or commit to many more rounds / larger eval sets / paired seeds), and it strengthens the case for reporting a *continuous* gating signal (sim-real r is measured, not sampled, so it has far less variance than SR) as the primary readout — which loops back to and reinforces Finding 5. A measurement perspective would also catch that "monotone across rounds" is a fragile claim form: a single noisy dip breaks monotonicity, so the right statistic is a trend test (isotonic / Mann-Kendall on the trajectory), not literal round-over-round monotonicity. None of the five voices raises power, variance, or the right trend statistic.

---

##### 5. OVERALL GRADE: A-

A genuinely strong research-strategy briefing. It does the hard, ego-unfriendly thing — it talks its own direction *down* to the defensible core, concedes the headline to Arcadia/CASHER explicitly, and lands a de-risking experiment that is cheap, 3-armed, and decisive. The cross-perspective convergence on "the gate is contribution, cost, and unvalidated assumption at once" is a real insight, and the frontier question (coverage-gate vs fidelity-gate) is the right next paper. It is held below A because (i) the one external pre-emption it newly introduces is quoted with more precision than the source supports, (ii) it is tilted toward subtraction (SKEPTIC) at the expense of sharpening the surviving claim, and (iii) it has a statistical-power blind spot that could make its own central falsifier undetectable.

**Top things to fix, in order:**
1. **Promote metric-validity (Finding 5) to the primary endpoint of experiment 1.** Log Δr-vs-ΔSR per fold and report the rank-correlation *first*. If r doesn't predict SR gain, you have the coverage-gate paper, not the fidelity-gate paper — know that before round 2, not after the campaign.
2. **Fix the baseline so the drift finding can't be a strawman.** The interesting contrast is (b) success-filter vs (c) fidelity-gate. State explicitly that the result is only publishable if (c) beats (b); (a) ungated-drifts is table-setting, not the claim.
3. **Add a power analysis and switch from literal monotonicity to a trend test.** Pre-register an effect size, eval-set size, and a Mann-Kendall/isotonic trend statistic on the trajectory — otherwise N=5 SR points cannot separate drift from binomial noise.
4. **Soften the DexFlyWheel citation to match the source.** It pre-empts "a self-improving loop shows a saturating trajectory," not "the per-round i=1..5 deployment-re-grounding curve" — DexFlyWheel aggregates sim rollouts and re-reconstructs nothing, which is precisely the axis B3 keeps. State that distinction in the card rather than letting the pre-emption sound broader than it is.
5. **Develop the positive ACADEMIC contribution.** Frame the gate as a *contraction condition* for re-grounding loops (fold-back is stable iff the accept-region bounds the reconstruction operator's amplification) — that turns the artifact from "a protocol" into "a stability criterion," which is the version of B3 worth a top-venue submission.

---

### B4 (Direction review)

Topic: the bet that a *learned constitutive law* (neural stress-strain map in a differentiable MPM), not its parameters, is the binding constraint on geometry extrapolation — beating a parameter-only fit on held-out geometry up to 1M particles AND surviving a closed robot real→sim→real loop (dropped into Real-to-Sim GS's pipeline).

Verbatim bet (from First-principles): "A learned constitutive law beats a parameter-only fit (e.g. PhysTwin) **on held-out geometry up to 1M particles** — reproducing NCLaw's order-of-magnitude extrapolation advantage and <1e-3 loss — *and* survives a **closed robot real→sim→real loop**: dropped into Real-to-Sim GS's pipeline in place of the fixed soft-body model, it holds forward correlation across material variation a single parameter fit cannot cover."

Novelty stress-test (alphaxiv `discover_papers` WORKED): surfaced DeformMaster (2605.09586), SoMA (2602.02402), D-REX (2603.01151), SIM1 (2604.08544), RSR-Loop (2503.10118), Contact-Aware Neural Dynamics (2601.12796). Pulled full content on DeformMaster, SoMA, D-REX.
- **DeformMaster (2605.09586)** = the single most threatening paper. It learns a *Mixture of Constitutive Experts* (spatially-varying Neo-Hookean/Corotated/StVK blend with *learnable* Young's modulus + Poisson per material patch) inside a differentiable MPM, *plus* a neural residual, from real PhysTwin video; demonstrates generalization to novel actions, novel views, and **material-parameter variation** (0.3x scaling produces fracture). This crowds the "learned constitutive form in a manipulation-relevant continuum" space harder than MASIV. BUT: it never runs H1's held-out-*geometry*-vs-parameter-fit isolation (it evaluates future-prediction on the *same* object classes), and it is *not* a closed robot real→sim→real loop (it is a world model evaluated on human-interaction video; "MPC potential" is named, not demonstrated). So both card deltas survive — narrowly.
- **SoMA (2602.02402)** closes a robot real-to-sim loop on soft bodies (T-shirt folding, action-conditioned) but with *force-driven hierarchical GNN dynamics on Gaussian splats* — the black-box route the card explicitly concedes "caps extrapolation." Confirms the robot-loop slot is being filled by the black-box competitor, not by a conservation-structured law.
- **D-REX (2603.01151, Abbeel/Malik)** is a closed real→sim→real loop but rigid-body *mass* identification (parameter fit) — off the learned-law axis entirely.

Verdict: **partially-pre-empted**. The "learn the form" half is now consensus (card concedes this via MASIV/UniPhy); the *specific* conjunction (geometry-isolation H1 + conservation-structured-law-in-robot-loop H2) is still open, but DeformMaster's MoCE + neural-residual occupies the conceptual high ground and SoMA already owns the robot-loop slot with the black-box route. The whitespace is a *narrow seam*, not a clear field.

---

#### Prompt 1 — Five Perspectives

**1. PRACTITIONER (builds/deploys policies daily).**
- *Core position*: The bet is asking me to swap a *fixed soft-body model* inside Real-to-Sim GS's r=0.915 loop for a *learned neural stress-strain map* — but a learned MPM law is a heavier, slower, less-debuggable object than a 4-scalar PhysTwin fit, and the loop already hits r>0.9 without it. The practical question is not "does the form generalize" but "is the geometry-extrapolation gain worth the differentiable-MPM rollout cost and the loss of a parameter I can sanity-check by hand."
- *Strongest supporting evidence*: D-REX (2603.01151) is the deployment reality check — its *parameter* (mass) identification took 5-20 min/object, 30-35 min reconstruction, and a force-aware policy hit 86% grasp success conditioned on a *single scalar*. EgoPhys (2606.16202) already closes a robot loop (xArm6, 77.6% config-error reduction, *no real fine-tuning*) on the *parameter-prior* side. A learned-law rollout in the loop must beat these on wall-clock and robustness, not just on a held-out-geometry error plot.
- *The one thing only this perspective says*: The 1M-particle target is a graphics-benchmark number, not a robot number — manipulation deformables (cloth, cable, food) live at thousands of particles with contact, and the Risk note already concedes "differentiable MPM is mature for soft bodies, not rich contact." Scope the bet to where MPM differentiability holds or you will spend the project fighting the simulator, not proving the thesis.

**2. ACADEMIC (knows the peer-reviewed literature).**
- *Core position*: The card's literature read is honest about MASIV/UniPhy making "learn the form" consensus, but it predates DeformMaster (2605.09586), which is the stronger prior art: MoCE is a *spatially-varying, multi-expert learnable* constitutive field with a neural residual — a more aggressive "learn the form" than MASIV — and it already shows material-parameter-variation generalization. The live novelty is therefore narrower than the card states: not "learned law in a deformable world model" (done, repeatedly) but specifically the *head-to-head learned-law-vs-parameter-fit-on-held-out-geometry* isolation (H1), which the literature genuinely has not run.
- *Strongest supporting evidence*: NCLaw (2304.14369) reports orders-of-magnitude generalization to 1M particles / unseen geometry but *for recovery, not against a parameter-fit control arm*. UniPhy (2505.16971) beats NCLaw on recovery (elastic 5.2e-6 vs 2.4e-4) but again no parameter-fit-on-geometry head-to-head. RAFL (2603.22039) is the *only* paper running the learned-correction-vs-parameter-sysID comparison on held-out morphology — and it reports parameter sysID showing *negative transfer* — but with a *black-box residual field*, not a conservation-structured law. So H1's exact comparison is a real gap; the card correctly identifies it.
- *The one thing only this perspective says*: The falsifier is already half-answered by RAFL — "learned-beats-parameter on unseen morphology" is shown; the *only* surviving scientific question is whether the *conservation structure* buys anything over RAFL's unstructured residual. That collapses the contribution from "learned law matters" to "the *structure* of the learned law matters" — a much narrower, ablation-shaped claim that a single conference paper can carry but cannot headline.

**3. SKEPTIC (thinks it is wrong or already solved).**
- *Core position*: The whitespace is mostly an artifact of how the card slices "what is recovered." DeformMaster learns the form *and* shows parameter-variation generalization; SoMA closes the robot loop; RAFL beats parameter sysID on held-out morphology. The card survives only by demanding *all three at once with a conservation-structured law* — a conjunction so specific it reads as gerrymandered whitespace rather than a discovered gap.
- *Strongest supporting evidence*: The card's own H1 falsifier note concedes RAFL "already shows a learned correction beating parameter sysID on unseen morphology with parameter sysID showing negative transfer." The card's own H2 falsifier concedes "RAFL's black-box residual matches the conservation-structured law in the loop → the structure buys nothing." DeformMaster's ablation already shows MoCE > single-Neo-Hookean expert — i.e., *flexible learned material beats fixed-form* is demonstrated, on real video, in a continuum sim. The convenient omission: the card lists DeformMaster's predecessor MASIV as "topThreat" but the alphaxiv search shows DeformMaster (May 2026) is the actual frontier and it is *absent from the card's table entirely*.
- *The one thing only this perspective says*: If RAFL's *unstructured* residual already beats parameter sysID and even *negative-transfers* the baseline, the most likely experimental outcome is that RAFL and the conservation-structured law tie on the loop — falsifying H2's value-add — because the marginal value of conservation structure shrinks exactly when a flexible residual is available. The bet's modal outcome is a null result on its headline claim.

**4. ECONOMIST (follows money/incentives).**
- *Core position*: This direction is cheap to *start* (reuse NCLaw/MASIV code, PhysGaia GT, VoMP ALRE) and expensive to *finish* (a real robot loop), which is exactly the incentive trap — the recovery-fidelity leg is publishable on benchmarks alone, so the rational move under grant/citation pressure is to ship H1 on PhysGaia/VoMP and *defer* the robot loop indefinitely, which is precisely the gap the field already has too many of.
- *Strongest supporting evidence*: Look at who owns the robot-loop slot: SoMA = Shanghai AI Lab (ICML 2026 target, well-resourced), D-REX = Abbeel/Malik/USC-PSI. These are compute-heavy industrial-academic labs. EgoPhys = UCSD/Xiaolong Wang. The robot-loop existence proofs are coming from groups with real-robot fleets; an academic betting on H2 without a robot competes on the leg where the money is, and loses on the leg (H1 benchmarks) where the money already moved on. PhysGaia (2506.02794) and VoMP (2510.22975, 166 objects, ALRE 0.0409) exist precisely because the recovery-benchmark market is saturating — benchmark-chasing has already arrived.
- *The one thing only this perspective says*: The defensible, fundable wedge is *not* the robot loop (you will be out-resourced) but the *negative result* — a rigorous H5 + H1 showing *where the learned form does and does not beat a parameter fit*, i.e., a calibration/identifiability paper. That is cheap, it is contrarian, it cites everyone, and no well-funded lab will bother to write it because it does not sell a system.

**5. HISTORIAN (has seen prior waves).**
- *Core position*: This is the pixel-vs-latent and analytical-vs-learned world-model cycle replayed at the constitutive-law granularity. Every cycle, the field swings from "learn it end-to-end" (data-driven GNN dynamics) to "structure it" (NCLaw embeds conservation) to "hybridize" (DeformMaster = MPM + neural residual + mixture experts), and the hybrid usually wins on benchmarks while the *structured-purist* claim ("structure is load-bearing") quietly loses its headline to "structure helps a little, residuals do the rest."
- *Strongest supporting evidence*: The Dreamer lineage and the DR/sim2real history both show the same arc: pure analytical (system ID, fixed law) → pure learned (black-box dynamics, AdaptiGraph 2407.07889) → hybrid (RAFL's residual-over-base-sim, DeformMaster's PNPGD residual-over-MPM). The card's bet is structurally a *purist* bet (conservation-structured law must beat the residual), and history says the residual usually catches up — RAFL and DeformMaster are *already* the residual wave arriving.
- *The one thing only this perspective says*: The durable contribution from prior cycles was never "structure won" — it was the *clean isolation experiment* that told the field *how much* structure is worth (e.g., the inductive-bias ablations that outlived the architectures). H4 (ablate NCLaw's rotation-equivariance + undeformed-equilibrium priors, gate on conservation residuals not reconstruction loss) is the experiment that survives even if the headline bet dies — build the paper around H4, not H1/H2.

---

#### Prompt 2 — Contradiction Map

1. **Where 2+ perspectives directly clash.**
   - *Skeptic vs Academic on H2's modal outcome*: Skeptic predicts RAFL's unstructured residual *ties* the conservation-structured law in the loop (null result, structure buys nothing); Academic agrees the question has collapsed to "does structure matter" but treats it as a *live, answerable* ablation rather than a foregone null. Specific claim in tension: whether conservation structure retains *any* extrapolation edge once a flexible residual is admitted.
   - *Practitioner vs Economist on where to spend*: Practitioner says scope down to MPM-valid soft bodies and prove the loop on real cloth/cable; Economist says do *not* fight for the robot loop (out-resourced by SoMA/Shanghai-AI-Lab, D-REX/Abbeel) and instead ship the cheap benchmark/identifiability paper. Direct clash: robot-loop-as-the-go/no-go (the card's own H2 framing) vs robot-loop-as-the-trap.
   - *Historian vs the card's First-principle*: card asserts "the constitutive form is the binding constraint"; Historian says the hybrid (form + residual) is what wins and the purist "form alone" claim historically loses its headline.

2. **Strongest vs weakest perspective.**
   - *Strongest: ACADEMIC* — every claim is anchored to a real metric (NCLaw 1e-3 / UniPhy 5.2e-6 / RAFL negative-transfer) and it correctly narrows the novelty to the one comparison the literature has genuinely not run (learned-law-vs-parameter-fit on held-out geometry), while flagging that DeformMaster post-dates and out-flanks the card's "topThreat."
   - *Weakest: HISTORIAN* — directionally right about the residual wave but argues by analogy; the constitutive-law-granularity cycle is young enough that "the residual always catches up" is a prior, not evidence. Useful for de-risking framing (lean on H4), not for the commit/drop decision.

3. **The one question that resolves the biggest contradiction.**
   Does a conservation-structured learned law (NCLaw-style) beat RAFL's *unstructured* residual-acceleration field on held-out geometry/morphology — controlling for the *same* base differentiable sim and the *same* training data? This single head-to-head resolves Skeptic-vs-Academic (is the structure load-bearing or decorative) AND tells the Practitioner/Economist whether the robot loop is worth fighting for. It is the bet, stripped to its only un-pre-empted core.

4. **What every perspective agrees on (likely true).**
   "Learn the constitutive *form* from video" is solved/consensus (NCLaw, UniPhy, MASIV, DeformMaster all demonstrate it), so it cannot be the contribution; the *only* remaining novelty lives in (a) the learned-law-vs-parameter-fit isolation on held-out geometry and (b) whether conservation *structure* beats an unstructured residual. Every perspective also agrees the robot-loop leg is the expensive, under-resourced one.

5. **The blind spot (topic NONE addressed).**
   *Sensing and observability of the law itself.* Every perspective debated whether the learned form generalizes, but none questioned whether a constitutive law is *identifiable* from the actual sensor stream a robot has at deploy time (single RGB-D, occluded by the gripper, sparse contact). DeformMaster needed Grounded-SAM-2 + CoTracker3 + Depth-Anything-3 + TRELLIS offline; SoMA needed multi-view RGB. The card's H3 names "single RGB-D" but no perspective interrogated whether the law is observable *at all* under manipulation occlusion — the actual binding constraint may be identifiability, not extrapolation.

---

#### Prompt 3 — Synthesis Briefing

**(1) CEO summary.** B4 bets that a *conservation-structured learned constitutive law* beats a parameter-only fit on held-out geometry and survives a closed robot real→sim→real loop. The "learn the functional form from video" premise the bet rests on is now firmly consensus (NCLaw, UniPhy, MASIV, and especially the newer DeformMaster, which the card's table omits), so the contribution cannot be "learn the form." Two specific deltas genuinely survive a literature check: H1 (the learned-law-vs-parameter-fit *isolation on held-out geometry* nobody has cleanly run) and H2 (a *conservation-structured law*, not a black-box residual, inside a real robot loop). But both are under threat: RAFL already shows a *learned residual* beating parameter sysID on held-out morphology (with parameter sysID negative-transferring), which collapses the bet from "learned beats parameter" to the much narrower "*structure* beats residual"; and SoMA/D-REX/EgoPhys already occupy the robot-loop slot via the black-box or parameter-prior routes that better-resourced labs own. The honest read is *partially-pre-empted*: a narrow, ablation-shaped seam survives, not an open field.

**(2) Five key findings, ranked by reliability.**
1. *"Learn the form" is consensus and cannot headline the paper.* (Supported: Academic, Skeptic, Historian, and the card itself; challenged by: none.) Anchored in NCLaw, UniPhy, MASIV, DeformMaster.
2. *The only un-pre-empted scientific question is whether conservation structure beats an unstructured residual on held-out geometry.* (Supported: Academic, Skeptic; challenged by: Practitioner who would reframe it as a cost question, not a science question.) Anchored in RAFL's learned-residual-beats-parameter-sysID + negative-transfer result.
3. *DeformMaster (2605.09586) is stronger, more recent prior art than the card's named "topThreat" MASIV and is missing from the table.* (Supported: Academic, Skeptic; challenged by: none — it is a factual omission.) MoCE = spatially-varying learnable multi-expert constitutive field + neural residual in differentiable MPM, with material-parameter-variation generalization.
4. *The robot-loop leg (H2) is the under-resourced trap, not the safe go/no-go.* (Supported: Economist, Practitioner; challenged by: the card, which calls H2 "the go/no-go that supplies the missing proof.") Anchored in SoMA = Shanghai AI Lab, D-REX = Abbeel/Malik, EgoPhys = UCSD already closing loops.
5. *Identifiability under manipulation occlusion, not extrapolation, may be the real binding constraint.* (Supported: inferred blind-spot, weakly by Practitioner; challenged by: none — unaddressed.) Anchored in DeformMaster's heavy offline perception stack vs the card's "single RGB-D" H3 claim.

**(3) Hidden cross-perspective connection.** The Academic's "the question has collapsed to does-structure-matter," the Skeptic's "modal outcome is a null on H2," and the Historian's "the residual always catches up" are the *same observation* from three vantage points: once RAFL admitted a flexible learned residual that beats parameter sysID, the bet silently mutated from a *broad* claim (learned form is the binding constraint) into a *narrow* ablation (conservation structure out-extrapolates an unstructured residual). The whole direction now hinges on one head-to-head that none of the three expects to win cleanly — and the Economist's wedge (write the *identifiability/calibration* negative result) is the only framing where that expected-null becomes the *asset* rather than the failure.

**(4) Actionable insight for the researcher: DE-RISK (do not COMMIT as framed, do not DROP).** The headline bet ("conservation-structured learned law beats parameter fit, and survives the robot loop") is partially pre-empted and its modal outcome on H2 is a null. But there is a defensible, cheap, contrarian paper inside it. Concretely:
   - *Reframe the contribution* from "learned law beats parameter fit" (RAFL-pre-empted) to "**how much does conservation *structure* buy over an unstructured residual, and when does it stop buying anything**" — an isolation/identifiability result, not a system.
   - *First experiment (the crux, ~weeks, no robot)*: on identical real video, fit four arms — (a) PhysTwin parameter fit, (b) RAFL-style unstructured residual over the *same* base differentiable sim, (c) NCLaw conservation-structured learned law, (d) the law with H4's structural priors *ablated* — and score held-out-geometry extrapolation on PhysGaia GT trajectories + VoMP ALRE. This single 2×2 (structured-vs-residual × priors-on-vs-off) directly answers the only surviving question (finding 2) and *is* H4, which the Historian flags as the experiment that outlives the architectures.
   - *Gate the robot loop (H2) behind that result*: only spend on the Real-to-Sim GS loop swap if arm (c) decisively beats (b) on held-out geometry; if it ties, pivot to the Economist's identifiability/calibration paper (H5 + the occlusion blind spot) and *do not* fight SoMA/D-REX for the loop.
   - *Add DeformMaster (2605.09586) to the table and reposition it as the topThreat* over MASIV; honestly state that the surviving novelty is the structure-vs-residual isolation, not the learned form.

**(5) Frontier question that would change everything.** Is a constitutive law even *identifiable* from the sensor stream a robot actually has at deploy time — single RGB-D, gripper-occluded, sparse-contact, no offline multi-foundation-model perception stack? If the law is unobservable under manipulation occlusion (the blind spot), then both H1's extrapolation and H2's loop are moot and the entire B-cluster's $(\phi^\star,\psi^\star)$ recovery premise is capped by *observability*, not by parameterization — which would redirect the whole direction from "learn a better law" to "what is the minimal interaction that makes the law identifiable."
