Both issues are below.

Grounding note: I read the C2 card (Sim2Real.md L733-809) and the analyst part (012-C2-1). I verified the two pre-emption anchors against source abstracts because they are external to the KnowledgeHub (`2505.09828` and `2301.03962` are both MISSING from `_KnowledgeHub_/`; every other cited ID is present). Verification confirmed both papers exist and are roughly as described, but with a material caveat that changes finding 2 (see below).

---

##### 1. Confidence scores (each 1-10, with one-line justification)

**Finding 1 — "The headline is settled, not the contribution." Score: 9/10.**
Four independent in-vault anchors (Sim2Val 2506.20553, SureSim 2510.04354, Sim2Real Betting 2604.24018, X4Val 2606.05159) ship the biased-portfolio-beats-single result, the card itself concedes it, and no perspective disputes it. The only reason this is 9 not 10: "settled" is doing slightly more work than the evidence (each paper demonstrates the result on its own setup, not a head-to-head equal-compute single-vs-portfolio comparison, which is what H1 itself proposes to run, implying it is not yet literally closed).

**Finding 2 — "The H2/H3/H5 mechanism is pre-empted by MFMC subset-selection." Score: 6/10.**
This is the briefing's most consequential and most over-stated claim. I verified 2505.09828 (AETC-OPT): it is genuine, it does explore-vs-exploit budget allocation and informative-subset use under a fixed budget, and 2301.03962 genuinely isolates diversity as a decomposition term separate from member count. So the *budget-allocation logic* and the *diversity-is-a-distinct-term* fact are indeed pre-empted. BUT both papers target classical point/mean-MSE estimators (ACV / MLBLUE for AETC-OPT; squared/cross-entropy/Poisson point loss for the diversity decomposition). C2's estimator class is finite-sample-valid PPI / sequential-betting *confidence intervals* (SureSim, Sim2Real Betting, N-SCORE). "Pre-empts the mechanism" therefore over-reaches: the mechanism pre-empted is the allocation heuristic, not the provable-CI object, and the diversity decomposition does not literally cover CI *width*. The card's actual whitespace (diversity-vs-count under anytime-valid CIs with a pilot cost) survives this more than the briefing's "largely pre-empted" framing admits. Down-graded because the headline finding mis-states what the prior art proves.

**Finding 3 — "The real lever is residual decorrelation against real; diversity-vs-count is a diminishing-returns curve, not a categorical property." Score: 8/10.**
Mechanically correct for any control-variate / PPI estimator: the variance reduction is governed by residual correlation against the real estimand, so "diversity" only helps via decorrelated residuals, and the gain is a concave function of added sources. The Sim2Val MCF 0.07-to-0.61 datapoint is real supporting evidence. Minus 2 because it is stated as a refutation of the card's framing when it is closer to a reformulation: the card's "diversity = different physics approximations" is a reasonable proxy for "decorrelated residuals," and whether the proxy holds is itself the PRACTITIONER's empirical question, not a settled fact.

**Finding 4 — "H2's falsifier is near-impossible, so H2 is rigged-to-confirm; H5 is the genuinely falsifiable core." Score: 7/10.**
The logic is sound: identical unbiased copies add zero control-variate information, so "redundant copies tighten the bound as much as diverse bias" cannot occur for a correct estimator, making that specific falsifier vacuous. This is a real, fixable flaw in the card. But the score is held to 7 because the claim slightly overshoots: "redundant copies" in practice means *domain-randomization variants of one engine* (the PRACTITIONER's own point), which are NOT identical copies and DO carry partially decorrelated residuals, so a DR-bank-vs-diverse-engine-bank sweep at matched count is a perfectly meaningful, non-rigged experiment. H2 is poorly *worded*, not inherently rigged. H5 being the cleaner falsifiable core is correct.

**Finding 5 — "The pilot-sample / policy-dependence problem is the unclaimed robotics-specific whitespace." Score: 5/10.**
This is explicitly flagged as an inference, and it is the most valuable idea in the briefing (it is the one thing that converts a junior sweep into a defensible contribution). But it is the least verified: no cited paper is shown to leave this specific gap open, the claim that MFMC's "durable contribution was pilot-robustness" is asserted from the HISTORIAN's general recollection rather than a named result, and "policy-dependence of bank informativeness" is plausible but untested even in argument. High value, low current evidentiary support, hence 5.

**Mean of the five scores: (9 + 6 + 8 + 7 + 5) / 5 = 7.0. Lowest: 5.**

---

##### 2. Weakest link

The weakest link is **finding 5 (pilot-sample / policy-dependence whitespace, 5/10)** by confidence, but the most *dangerous* weak link is **finding 2's over-claim**, because the entire "DE-RISK, do not commit" recommendation and CEO summary hang on "the mechanism is pre-empted."

What would verify each:
- For finding 2: read 2505.09828 and 2301.03962 in full (not abstracts) and check one specific thing — does AETC-OPT's optimality / the diversity decomposition transfer to a *finite-sample-valid confidence-interval width* objective, or only to point-estimator MSE / expected loss? If only the latter, the pre-emption is partial and the card's whitespace is larger than the briefing concedes, which flips the recommendation from "de-risk/junior" toward "narrow-but-real." I verified the abstracts (both papers exist and target point/mean estimators); the full-text confirmation of the estimator-class gap is the missing step. Both papers should also be ingested into the KnowledgeHub so this claim is vault-grounded rather than a prompt-injected external citation.
- For finding 5: a literature check on whether any of Sim2Val/X4Val/SureSim/N-SCORE already report sensitivity of their correlation/weight estimates to pilot-sample size, and whether bank weights are re-estimated per policy. If they already pilot-stabilize, the whitespace shrinks.

---

##### 3. Bias check — which perspective over-dominated

**ACADEMIC over-dominated, with HISTORIAN as amplifier.** The synthesis's spine (findings 1, 2, the CEO summary, the resolving question, the actionable insight) is the ACADEMIC's "2505.09828 pre-empts this" thesis, and the analyst's own Prompt-2 named ACADEMIC the strongest perspective. Three of five findings and the headline recommendation are ACADEMIC-framed. The cost: the briefing inherits ACADEMIC's blind spot — it treats "the general MFMC theory exists" as near-decisive against the direction, without pressure-testing whether that general theory actually covers the *provable-CI + robotics-residual + pilot-cost* triple, which is exactly where PRACTITIONER and the un-promoted ECONOMIST benchmark-moat angle live. PRACTITIONER's "your diverse engines may share residual failures" is correctly surfaced but then folded under the ACADEMIC frame rather than allowed to stand as an independent reason the whole bet could be empirically dead (if robot sims are residually too correlated, no statistics saves it). ECONOMIST was explicitly demoted as "sociology, cannot kill or save the bet," which is the right call for truth-value but under-weights that the ECONOMIST's benchmark-substrate idea is the single most defensible *positive* path the briefing offers.

---

##### 4. Missing perspective (a 6th angle that would change conclusions)

**The METROLOGIST / ESTIMATION-THEORIST who asks: what is the actual estimand, and is it even identifiable from this design?** Every perspective argues about *how well* the CI is tightened; none audits *what real-world quantity the CI is around* and whether it is the deployment-relevant one. Concretely: PPI/betting give a valid CI on the mean real success rate of one policy on one task distribution under one set of paired real rollouts. The deployment question is a *ranking* over fast-iterating policies under distribution shift between the paired-calibration trials and the deployment trials. A metrologist would point out that finite-sample CI validity is conditional on the paired-real sample being exchangeable with the target population, and the moment the policy or task distribution shifts (the realistic case the ECONOMIST raised and finding 5 gestures at), the "provable" guarantee is provable about the *wrong* estimand. This changes the conclusion: it is not merely that the bank advantage must be "re-paid per policy" (an economics framing), it is that the *coverage guarantee itself may not hold* across policy releases — which is a correctness problem, not a cost problem, and it is a stronger reason to demand the policy-agnostic-bank frontier result before committing. It also reframes the Conformal-PPI row (2510.16166, already in the card) from a minor shift-robustness footnote into the central load-bearing tool. None of the five perspectives elevate it.

---

##### 5. Overall grade + top fixes

**Grade: B+.**

Rationale: this is a strong, intellectually honest briefing — it concedes its own headline, names a specific dated pre-emption, surfaces a non-obvious cross-perspective convergence (the pilot-estimation object), and lands a genuinely good frontier question (policy-agnostic bank). That is well above the median direction review. It is held below A- by three things: (a) the load-bearing pre-emption claim over-states what the verified prior art proves (point-MSE / expected-loss vs finite-sample CI width), and rests on two papers absent from the vault; (b) the synthesis is ACADEMIC-mono-dominant, so its de-risk verdict is more confident than the evidence licenses; (c) the highest-value finding (finding 5) is the least supported, and the briefing's recommendation structure ("commit only if pilot cost does not erase Delta") is sound but the pilot cost is never even order-of-magnitude estimated.

Top fixes, in priority order:
1. **Re-scope finding 2 from "pre-empts the mechanism" to "pre-empts the budget-allocation heuristic, but not the provable-CI estimand."** Verify in full text whether AETC-OPT / the diversity decomposition transfer to CI-width objectives. This single correction is what determines whether the verdict is "junior, de-risk" or "narrow but real," and the current briefing tilts to the former on under-verified grounds.
2. **Ingest 2505.09828 and 2301.03962 into the KnowledgeHub and cite them in the card**, so the central novelty-narrowing claim is vault-grounded rather than dependent on externally-supplied citations the rest of the document cannot check.
3. **Re-word H2's falsifier** from the vacuous "identical copies" to the meaningful "DR-variants-of-one-engine bank vs different-physics-engine bank at matched count and matched compute," and **promote H5 (crossover) + the pilot-cost charge to the load-bearing falsifiable test.**
4. **Add the metrologist's estimand/coverage-under-shift audit** as an explicit risk and elevate Conformal-PPI (2510.16166) accordingly; treat the policy-agnostic-bank frontier as a correctness prerequisite, not just an economic upside.
5. **Put at least one quantitative bound on the pilot cost** (how many paired real trials to estimate the residual-correlation structure to a useful tolerance), since the entire commit/no-commit decision is defined as "does pilot cost erase Delta" yet that cost is never numerically bracketed.

---

### D (Cluster review)

Topic: the coherence + central shared-bottleneck thesis of Cluster D — "Deployment-Time Adaptation: Closing the Residual Online" — whether splitting deploy-time adaptation by *which model is on hand* (privileged extrinsics / analytical model / learned world model) carves the problem at the right joint, and whether the shared bet (the residual δ(t) is time-varying and observable only at deploy-time, so each engine closes it) is sound. Cluster framing (Overview row): "The residual δ(t) that survives A/B/C is time-varying and observable only at deploy-time"; intro: "Close the residual δ(t) at deploy-time … The three directions split by which model is on hand: privileged extrinsics, an analytical model, or only a learned one." Shared primitive: [[2603.04029|Self-Adapting RL]] as the residual-trigger.

#### Prompt 1 — Five Perspectives

**1. PRACTITIONER (builds/deploys policies daily).**
- *Core position*: The cluster's split-by-model-on-hand is operationally honest — on a real platform you genuinely have either privileged extrinsics (D1), an analytical core (D2), or only a learned WM (D3), and that determines your adaptation engine. But the bet underweights that all three online loops share one deployment killer: an online weight/gradient update running on hardware is a *safety and stability* liability, and the cluster offloads that to Cluster E rather than owning it.
- *Strongest evidence*: D2's own numbers prove the practical envelope is narrow — [[2508.21065|Learning on the Fly]] gets 81% hover-error reduction vs L1-MPC and 3-step / 4.5 s correction *only* in the aerial analytical-core regime; its own KH note concedes "contact/friction transients hard to differentiate stably." [[2602.20057|AdaWorldPolicy]] runs at *4 Hz* on real robots — that is the practical ceiling for a billion-param DiT LoRA loop, and 4 Hz is slow for contact-rich correction. [[2603.04029|Self-Adapting RL]] needs *8 minutes* of real interaction (F1Tenth) to adapt — minutes, not seconds.
- *Only this perspective says*: The three directions are not really co-deployable the way "synergy" implies — D2's 4.5 s aerial loop and D3's 4 Hz manipulation LoRA loop live on different robots with different failure modes; the cluster reads like three separate papers wearing one jacket, and a practitioner would commit to exactly one (D2 aerial or D3 manipulation), never the trio.

**2. ACADEMIC (knows the literature).**
- *Core position*: The cluster's grouping joint is defensible and matches how the field actually partitions test-time adaptation, but each direction's *contrarian wedge* is thinner than the framing admits — in all three, the card itself documents that the general claim is already taken and only an inverted/augmented sub-claim survives.
- *Strongest evidence*: The card is unusually candid: D1 concedes infer-then-condition inside-range is "settled" ([[2107.04034|RMA]], [[2409.16578|FLaRe]] 50%→80.7%) and bets only the *inverse* of [[2412.04323|GRAM]]'s give-up-OOD conclusion (GRAM: 0.92 ID / 0.79 near-OOD / 0.58 far-OOD, robust-fallback). D2 concedes [[2507.10914|M-GAPS]] "settles online-gradient-beats-RL for an analytical controller" and bets only the learned-residual-vs-analytical delta. D3 concedes [[2605.08215|T3VF]] makes prediction-error-as-reward "consensus" and that [[2007.04309|Self-Supervised Deploy Adapt]] broke "needs reward" in *2021* — leaving only the force-term-on-real-robot wedge.
- *Only this perspective says*: The shared "δ(t) observable only at deploy-time" first principle is half-true and the literature contradicts the strong reading — much of δ(t) *is* recoverable offline (Cluster B real-to-sim, [[2510.11689|Phys2Real]] parameter estimation 57% vs 23%), so the cluster's claim to own a residual "that survives A/B/C" is a *definitional* boundary, not an empirically measured one; nobody has shown how much residual actually survives B.

**3. SKEPTIC (thinks it's wrong or already solved).**
- *Core position*: Two of the three front-line bets are at serious risk of being already-answered-in-the-wrong-direction. D1's wedge ("continued inference beats robust-fallback past the range") runs straight into a wall of evidence that inference *does* degrade OOD and the field is converging on detect-then-robustify, not keep-inferring.
- *Strongest evidence*: [[2412.04323|GRAM]] already built D1's exact architecture (proprioception-history latent context) and concluded the *opposite* of D1's bet, on real hardware (9 kg payload, far-OOD). Independently, [2507.06111] (Safe Domain Randomization via Uncertainty-Aware OOD Detection, Mila/Meta/McGill) operationalizes the GRAM stance: detect OOD via uncertainty, then *adapt/switch* — the field's momentum is detect-then-handle, not extrapolate-the-latent. D3's wedge is threatened from a different angle: [2602.12218] (The Observer Effect in World Models: Invasive Adaptation Corrupts Latent Physics) shows invasive online fine-tuning under *narrow OOD data* selectively *erases* dynamic features (CKA <0.2, OOD MAPE spiking 50–150%, ρ→0.05) — i.e. the very LoRA/weight-update loop D3 and D2 rely on can corrupt the model's physics exactly when you push it OOD.
- *Only this perspective says*: The card never measures whether the inference estimate actually extrapolates — D1's H2 ("envelope exists") and H3 ("inference beats fallback") are *both unrun*, and the only cited support ([[2508.20294|DALI]] +96.4% extrapolation, [[2602.04412|HoRD]] 86% in unseen Genesis sim) is sim-only single-point evidence; the whitespace exists because the experiment is hard, and the hardness is the reason GRAM gave up.

**4. ECONOMIST (follows money/incentives).**
- *Core position*: This cluster is cheap-to-publish, hard-to-deploy bait — deploy-time adaptation papers are favored because they promise to dodge the two most expensive line-items in robot learning (real-robot data and per-environment re-training), which is exactly why the area is crowded and why each wedge is already half-taken.
- *Strongest evidence*: Every anchor sells a cost-avoidance story: [[2107.04034|RMA]] "zero real fine-tune," [[2409.16578|FLaRe]] "6 h new behavior," [[2508.21065|Learning on the Fly]] "4.5 s," [[2602.20057|AdaWorldPolicy]] "no environment reward," all benchmarked on cheap shared substrates ([[2306.03310|LIBERO]]-10 0.96, [[2510.13626|LIBERO-Plus]], [[2510.17950|RoboChallenge]]). The benchmark-saturation tell is real: LIBERO-10 at 0.96 is near-ceiling, which is *why* the field migrated to LIBERO-Plus (drop >95%→<30%) — the bet is partly chasing the next un-saturated benchmark, not a deployment need.
- *Only this perspective says*: The contrarian framing ("the field assumes X, we bet the inverse") is itself an incentive artifact — it manufactures novelty against a single named competitor (GRAM, M-GAPS, T3VF) rather than a deployment metric, which is grant/citation-optimal but means the win condition is "beat one paper's conclusion" not "ship a robot," and that's a fragile bet if the named competitor's conclusion is actually correct.

**5. HISTORIAN (has seen prior waves).**
- *Core position*: This is the latest turn of the adaptive-control / system-identification wheel that has spun since the 1980s (MRAC, L1 adaptive control) through Dreamer-lineage model-based RL — the cluster's three engines map cleanly onto three historical camps (indirect adaptive control = D1 extrinsics, online sysID/gradient = D2, model-based-RL = D3), and each camp historically converged on the same lesson the SKEPTIC raises: adaptation gains are real in-distribution and brittle at the edge.
- *Strongest evidence*: D2's own lineage in the card is explicit and old — [[2202.09834|Differentiable-Physics Online MPC+SysID]] (2022), [[2210.12320|GAPS]]/[[2209.10021|DiffTune]], with [[2507.10914|M-GAPS]] as the current capstone; D3 traces to [[2007.04309|Self-Supervised Deploy Adapt]] (2021). The pixel-vs-latent world-model cycle recurs in D3 (image-foresight [[2605.08215|T3VF]] vs force-augmented [[2602.20057|AdaWorldPolicy]]) — the field keeps rediscovering that a single modality's prediction error is blind to another's, which is precisely D3's force-term wedge.
- *Only this perspective says*: The robust-vs-adaptive swing (D1's whole bet) is a pendulum that has swung both ways before — DR/robustness won when adaptation proved fragile, adaptation won when DR proved conservative — so D1 isn't discovering a frontier, it's betting on the next half-swing; the durable contribution is never "adaptation beats robustness" but the *envelope map* (when each wins), which is exactly D1's H2 that the card leaves unrun.

#### Prompt 2 — Contradiction Map

1. **Direct clashes.**
   - *SKEPTIC vs ACADEMIC/cluster on D1*: SKEPTIC says GRAM (0.58 far-OOD via robust-fallback) plus [2507.06111] show the field has *settled the opposite* of D1's bet — inference degrades OOD, detect-then-robustify wins. The cluster (and ACADEMIC, weakly) says GRAM only *concedes* unreliability and never ran the graded SR-vs-distance head-to-head, so the inverse is still open. The specific claim in tension: does the real-SR gap *grow* with distance past the range (D1/H3) or does robust-fallback overtake (GRAM)?
   - *PRACTITIONER vs cluster-synergy*: PRACTITIONER says D1/D2/D3 are not co-deployable (different robots, 4.5 s aerial vs 4 Hz manipulation vs 8-min F1Tenth); the cluster's "synergy" row implies one residual-closing program. Claim in tension: is D a *theme* or three *separate* papers.
   - *SKEPTIC vs D2/D3 mechanism (via Observer Effect)*: [2602.12218] says invasive online updates under narrow OOD data erase latent physics; D2/D3 assume the online LoRA/BPTT update *improves* under OOD. Claim in tension: does the online update correct or corrupt when pushed past the range.

2. **Strongest vs weakest perspective.**
   - *Strongest*: SKEPTIC — it is the only one citing a real paper ([2507.06111]) and a real mechanism ([2602.12218]) that threaten the bets rather than restating the card, and it correctly identifies that the two front-line hypotheses (D1/H3, and D2/H1) are unrun.
   - *Weakest*: ECONOMIST — its incentive read is true but non-falsifiable and doesn't change the technical verdict; "contrarian framing is grant-optimal" is a meta-critique, not evidence the bet is wrong.

3. **The one question that resolves the biggest contradiction.**
   - Run D1's H3 directly: on one matched task, sweep deployment dynamics from inside the DR range to far outside, plot real-robot SR for {continued latent-extrinsics inference} vs {GRAM-style robust-fallback}. *Does the SR gap grow, vanish, or invert with distance past the range?* This single experiment resolves the SKEPTIC-vs-cluster clash, validates or kills D1, and produces the HISTORIAN's envelope map as a byproduct.

4. **What every perspective agrees on (likely true).**
   - The split-by-model-on-hand axis (privileged extrinsics / analytical / learned WM) is a *coherent and honest* taxonomy of deploy-time adaptation — no perspective disputes the carve itself. And all agree the *in-distribution* settled results are real ([[2107.04034|RMA]] zero-fine-tune, [[2409.16578|FLaRe]] 50%→80.7%, [[2508.21065|Learning on the Fly]] 4.5 s, [[2602.20057|AdaWorldPolicy]] 0.96 LIBERO-10) — the contention is entirely about the OOD/past-the-range wedge.

5. **Blind spot (addressed by none).**
   - *How much residual actually survives Cluster B?* The cluster's existence is justified by "the residual that survives A/B/C," but no perspective and no card row *measures* the post-B residual. If a grounded real-to-sim pipeline (Cluster B) removes 90% of δ(t) offline, the deploy-time loop is optimizing the last 10% under the worst possible conditions (on-hardware, safety-critical, 4 Hz) — the cost/benefit of online adaptation vs better offline grounding is never quantified. Relatedly, none addresses *observability*: D1 assumes proprioception exposes the extrinsics (the card's own Risk row flags vision-dominant manipulation may not), and that under-determination boundary is unmeasured.

#### Prompt 3 — Synthesis Briefing

**CEO summary.** Cluster D carves deploy-time adaptation at a real joint — by which model the robot has on hand — and the taxonomy is clean, honest, and well-evidenced *inside* the randomization range, where the settled results (RMA zero-fine-tune, FLaRe 50%→80.7%, Learning-on-the-Fly 4.5 s, AdaWorldPolicy 0.96 LIBERO-10) are genuine. The risk is that the cluster's *value* lives entirely in three OOD wedges that are each (a) inverted against a single named competitor whose published conclusion points the other way (GRAM for D1, M-GAPS for D2, T3VF for D3), and (b) supported only by sim-only or single-point extrapolation evidence with the two true front-line experiments (D1/H3 and D2/H1) unrun. This is committable as a single sharp paper — most likely D1's "does latent inference extrapolate past the range, and where does the envelope close" — but not as a three-direction program, and the central liability is that the field's momentum ([2507.06111] detect-then-robustify) and a real corruption mechanism ([2602.12218] invasive adaptation erases latent physics under narrow OOD) both push against the keep-adapting-OOD premise.

**5 key findings, ranked by reliability.**
1. *In-range adaptation is settled; the contribution is the OOD wedge only.* (Supported: ACADEMIC, HISTORIAN, card's own concessions; challenged by none.) Highest reliability — the card states it explicitly for all three directions.
2. *The two front-line bets (D1/H3 inference-beats-fallback, D2/H1 residual-beats-analytical) are unrun, so the whitespace is real but unproven.* (Supported: SKEPTIC, ACADEMIC; challenged by: ECONOMIST who notes that "beat one paper's conclusion" is a fragile win condition.) High reliability.
3. *The split-by-model-on-hand taxonomy is sound, but the three directions are not co-deployable as a synergistic program.* (Supported: PRACTITIONER, HISTORIAN; challenged by: cluster's synergy row.) Medium-high.
4. *Field momentum favors detect-then-robustify over keep-inferring-OOD, directly threatening D1.* ([2507.06111], [[2412.04323|GRAM]] far-OOD 0.58. Supported: SKEPTIC, HISTORIAN's pendulum; challenged by: cluster's "GRAM only concedes, never measured the sweep.") Medium — GRAM's conclusion is real but the graded sweep genuinely was not run.
5. *Online weight updates may corrupt, not just fail, under OOD (latent-physics erasure).* ([2602.12218] CKA<0.2, OOD ρ→0.05 under narrow-data fine-tune. Supported: SKEPTIC; challenged by: D2/D3 which assume the update helps, and note it's an *evaluation-probing* paper not a control paper.) Lower-medium — suggestive, not yet shown on a control loop.

**Hidden cross-perspective connection.** The HISTORIAN's "the durable contribution is the envelope map, not who-wins" and the SKEPTIC's "H2/H3 are unrun" and the blind-spot's "post-B residual is unmeasured" are the *same* missing object: a quantitative map of *how far past the randomization range each engine holds*, and *how much residual is even left to close there*. Every critique converges on the absence of one measurement — the SR-vs-distance-past-range curve — which is simultaneously D1's H2/H3, the resolution to the biggest contradiction, and the only output of this cluster that survives whichever way the robust-vs-adaptive pendulum swings.

**Actionable insight for the researcher (COMMIT / DE-RISK / DROP).** DE-RISK, then commit to D1 narrowly. Do *not* commit to the three-direction cluster as one program (PRACTITIONER: not co-deployable; ECONOMIST: fragile triple-wedge). Instead, de-risk by running the single resolving experiment *first*, before writing the framing: on one matched legged task with a graded dynamics sweep ([[2508.20294|DALI]]'s Interpolate/Extrapolate/Mixed as the sim substrate, then a real Unitree distance-past-range sweep), put {continued latent-extrinsics inference, RMA-style} head-to-head against {GRAM-style robust-fallback}, and plot real-SR gap vs distance past the DR range. Three outcomes, all publishable: gap grows → D1's contrarian bet wins (strong paper); gap vanishes at a finite distance → you've produced the *envelope map* (the HISTORIAN's durable contribution, the safer paper); fallback overtakes → GRAM is right, pivot to "where the handoff should happen" (still a contribution). Gate any online weight-update variant on [2602.12218]'s corruption finding — measure CKA/feature-erasure during the OOD update, not just SR. Drop the "synergy across D1/D2/D3" claim entirely.

**Frontier question that would change everything.** *Is there a measurable distance past the randomization range where on-robot inference provably extrapolates the true dynamics — and does that distance exceed how much residual a grounded real-to-sim pipeline (Cluster B) leaves uncorrected?* If yes, deploy-time adaptation is a necessary frontier and D1's envelope is the map of it; if the post-B residual is smaller than the inference envelope, the entire cluster collapses into "polish what B couldn't reach," and the right bet moves upstream to grounding.

---

#### Prompt 4 — Peer Review (independent)

*Reviewer stance: skeptical Stanford robotics faculty. The cluster text (Sim2Real.md L810–1033) was read in full; scores are grounded in what the cards actually claim and concede, not the CEO summary's gloss.*

---

##### 1. Confidence scores (1–10, reliability)

**Finding 1 — In-range adaptation is settled; the contribution is the OOD wedge only. → 9/10.**
This is the most defensible claim in the briefing and the cluster's own cards say it plainly. D1 explicitly demotes the manipulation in-range result to "a baseline to beat, not an open target" (L827) and labels In-Hand RMA / COCOI as pre-answering H1. D2's thesis concedes M-GAPS "settles it for an analytical controller" (L905). D3 concedes "prediction error stands in for reward is settled, not novel" (L971). The settled in-range targets (RMA zero-fine-tune, FLaRe 50→80.7%, Learning-on-the-Fly 4.5 s, AdaWorldPolicy 0.96 LIBERO-10) are real, attributed, and not over-stated. One point off only because "settled" is doing slightly heavy lifting: FLaRe and AdaWorldPolicy are single-paper, single-lab results, not community-replicated, so "settled" is "demonstrated by the anchor," not "settled" in the strong sense.

**Finding 2 — The two front-line bets (D1/H3, D2/H1) are unrun, so the whitespace is real but unproven. → 8/10.**
Verifiable directly from the text: D1/H3 is tagged "(front line)" (L867) and its test is a head-to-head that has not been run; D2/H1 is "(front line)" (L928) and the suite "benchmarks robust-adaptive control methods, so the learned-residual-vs-analytical comparison itself is the unrun bet it hosts" (L930) — i.e. the paper admits the comparison does not yet exist. The ECONOMIST counter ("beat one paper's conclusion is a fragile win condition") is well-placed and the briefing surfaces it honestly. I dock two points because the briefing under-counts: there are *three* unrun front-line bets, not two. D3/H3 (force-prediction-error beats T3VF's image-foresight head, L1011) is equally unrun and equally a single-competitor inversion. The CEO summary's "two true front-line experiments unrun" silently drops D3, which slightly inflates the cluster's apparent readiness.

**Finding 3 — Taxonomy is sound but the three directions are not co-deployable as a synergistic program. → 7/10.**
The split-by-model-on-hand axis (privileged extrinsics / analytical model / learned model only, L812) is genuinely clean and non-overlapping, and the actionable insight's "drop the synergy claim" is the right call. The reason this is not higher: the briefing asserts non-co-deployability as a finding but the cluster text never actually presents a synergy row I can locate in D's body — the "challenged by cluster's synergy row" pointer is to cross-cluster handoffs (D1→E1, D2→E2, D3→E1) in the Risks blocks, which are safety hand-offs, not a synergy claim. So the finding is correct but is rebutting a weaker target than implied; the real point is simpler — these are three separate papers because they share no experiment, no platform, and no metric.

**Finding 4 — Field momentum favors detect-then-robustify over keep-inferring-OOD, threatening D1. → 5/10.**
This is the briefing's most consequential strategic claim and its least substantiated *inside the document under review*. The two load-bearing counter-papers, 2507.06111 (detect-then-robustify) and 2602.12218 (latent-physics erasure), do **not appear anywhere in the D1/D2/D3 Related-papers tables**. GRAM (2412.04323) is in the table and does concede OOD unreliability — but the briefing's own rebuttal is correct and damaging to the claim: GRAM "only concedes, never measured the sweep" (it falls back to a robust feature as a design choice, it did not run the graded distance-past-range experiment that would show inference *fails*). So "field momentum" rests on (a) one in-table concession that is not a measurement, plus (b) two out-of-table papers the reviewer cannot check against the cluster's own evidence. "Momentum" is a narrative, not a counted trend. 5/10: directionally plausible, weakly evidenced, and the strongest single threat to the whole cluster is precisely the one least anchored in the document.

**Finding 5 — Online weight updates may corrupt, not just fail, under OOD via latent-physics erasure (2602.12218). → 4/10.**
The briefing itself flags this as "lower-medium" and the discount is right. 2602.12218 is not in any D table, and the briefing correctly notes it is "an evaluation-probing paper not a control paper" — i.e. it characterizes what fine-tuning does to features, it does not demonstrate that D2/D3's specific loops corrupt. The CKA<0.2 / rho→0.05 numbers are cited without the reviewer being able to verify the regime they were measured in (narrow-data fine-tune of *what* policy, on *what* shift). Critically, D1's bet is about *inference* (no weight update — RMA infers a latent, it does not fine-tune), so the corruption finding bites D2/D3's weight-update variants and AdaWorldPolicy's LoRA, but is largely orthogonal to D1, which is the direction the briefing actually recommends committing to. The corruption risk is real and worth gating on, but as stated it is an imported caution, not a measured threat to the recommended bet. 4/10.

**Mean = 6.6. Lowest = 4.**

---

##### 2. Weakest link

Finding 5 (latent-physics erasure as a *corruption* mechanism), at 4/10, with Finding 4 close behind. Both depend on out-of-cluster papers (2602.12218, 2507.06111) whose numbers and regimes cannot be checked against the cluster's own evidence base, and both are applied to the cluster somewhat indiscriminately. To verify Finding 5 specifically: pull 2602.12218 and confirm (a) the CKA<0.2 / OOD-rho→0.05 measurements were taken under an *online deploy-time* fine-tune (not offline narrow re-training), (b) on a policy class comparable to AdaWorldPolicy's LoRA or D2's BPTT loop (not a from-scratch supervised net), and (c) whether the erasure persists under the slow-update / experience-filter gates D already proposes (EWAM's filter L994, Self-Adapting RL's residual trigger). Until that is confirmed, "may corrupt not just fail" is a hypothesis the cluster should *test*, which is in fact exactly what the actionable insight already says ("gate on CKA/feature-erasure, measure it") — so the honest framing is that Finding 5 is a proposed experiment, not a finding.

---

##### 3. Bias check

Over-dominated by the **SKEPTIC**, with the **HISTORIAN** second. Four of five findings (2, 3, 4, 5) are framed as "what is unproven / what the field is moving away from / what might corrupt," and the synthesis's "hidden connection" and "frontier question" both resolve to a single SKEPTIC/HISTORIAN object: the SR-vs-distance-past-range envelope map. The PRACTITIONER appears once (co-deployability) and the ECONOMIST once (fragile win condition), each as a one-line counter rather than a structuring lens. The ACADEMIST/ACADEMIC voice is folded into "settled in-range" and never gets to argue the positive case — that a clean envelope map is a genuine scientific contribution independent of who wins. The net effect is a briefing that is *correctly* cautious but tilts toward "don't over-commit" without equally pressure-testing the upside: if D1's inference genuinely extrapolates past the range (which LDG, LocoFormer's real-hardware morphology OOD, and Latent-Variable MBRL's "extrapolates physical parameters beyond training" all weakly support, L841/L844/L853), that is a strong, fundable result and the SKEPTIC-heavy framing under-weights it.

---

##### 4. Missing perspective

A **deployment-economics / opportunity-cost** angle that the briefing's own frontier question gestures at but does not staff: **the Cluster B (grounded real-to-sim) substitution test**. The frontier question asks whether the post-B residual is smaller than the inference envelope — if so "the cluster collapses into polish what B couldn't reach." This is the single perspective that could *invert the recommendation*, and it is left as a rhetorical closer rather than a finding. A sixth reviewer focused on it would ask: what is the measured residual *after* a state-of-the-art real-to-sim grounding pass on the exact legged task D1 proposes? If grounding already closes 90% of the sim-to-real gap on that platform, then D1's entire OOD envelope is operating on the last 10%, and the head-to-head-vs-GRAM experiment is fought over a sliver that may not matter for any real deployment — making D2's *speed* story (correct now, not minutes) the more defensible deploy-time contribution and demoting D1 from "commit" to "nice envelope map." The briefing commits to D1 without first sizing the residual D1 is meant to close, which is the one number that determines whether deploy-time adaptation is a frontier or a mop-up.

---

##### 5. Overall grade and top fixes

**Grade: B+.**

This is an unusually honest briefing — it surfaces its own falsifiers, demotes its settled results, and refuses to oversell the synergy. That candor is why it clears B. It does not reach A− because (a) the two claims that would most change the strategic conclusion (Findings 4 and 5) rest on papers absent from the cluster's own evidence tables, so they read as imported narrative rather than grounded analysis; (b) the "two front-line bets" framing miscounts (three unrun single-competitor inversions, not two), which understates the fragility the ECONOMIST correctly flags; and (c) the commit-to-D1 recommendation is made without sizing the post-B residual — the very quantity the briefing's own frontier question identifies as decisive.

**Top fixes, in priority order:**
1. **Size the residual before committing.** Run, or at minimum cite a number for, the post-Cluster-B grounding residual on the target legged task. If it is small, the whole "commit to D1" call should flip toward Cluster B. This is the cheapest experiment with the highest decision-value and it is currently missing.
2. **Pull the two threat papers into the D-cluster tables.** 2507.06111 and 2602.12218 are currently asserted, not shown. Either add them as rows (with their measured regimes) so the reader can weigh them against GRAM, or downgrade Findings 4 and 5 from "field momentum / corruption mechanism" to "two adjacent cautions to gate against."
3. **Correct the count: three front-line bets, not two.** D3/H3 (force vs image-foresight) is the same fragile single-competitor inversion as D1/H3 and D2/H1. Naming all three sharpens the ECONOMIST's point and strengthens, not weakens, the "one sharp paper, not a program" recommendation.
4. **Decouple the corruption gate from D1.** D1 is inference-only (no weight update), so the 2602.12218 gate belongs on D2's BPTT loop and D3's LoRA, not on the recommended D1 commit. State this so the reader does not over-apply the caution to the bet you are actually making.
5. **Let the envelope map stand as the positive thesis.** The strongest durable output — the SR-vs-distance-past-range curve — survives whichever way the robust-vs-adaptive pendulum swings. Lead with it as the contribution, not as the fallback when the contrarian bet fails.

---

### D1 (Direction review)

Topic: the FP bet that outside the train-time domain-randomization range, a proprioception-only latent-extrinsics estimator **beats a GRAM-style robust-fallback head-to-head**, with the real-SR gap *growing* with distance past the range (and vanishing inside it) over a measurable envelope, reproducing RMA's zero-real-fine-tune adaptation and FLaRe's 50%->80.7% as the in-range baseline.

#### Prompt 1 — Five Perspectives

**1. PRACTITIONER (builds/deploys policies daily).**
- *Core position*: The architecture this bet rides on already ships: RMA's 10 Hz adaptation-module / 100 Hz base-policy split is a deployable, proprioception-only loop with zero real fine-tune, and it survived sand/mud/12 kg. The open question is not "can we infer extrinsics online" but "how far past the range can we trust it before the robot face-plants" — and that envelope is hardware-, sensor-, and gait-specific, so a *single* sweep number does not transfer between robots.
- *Strongest evidence*: [[2107.04034|RMA]] (zero real fine-tune across terrains + 12 kg payload), [[2507.07825|LoadAdapt]] (zero-shot Go2 across 4 kg/6 kg lead balls near an Oracle, beating robustness baselines), [[2509.23745|LocoFormer]] (real Unitree zero-shot to locked limbs / wheel failure / walks on knees). These are all *deployed*, not sim-only.
- *Only-this-perspective*: The bet's falsifier ("the SR gap grows with distance past the range") needs a *real-robot graded distance-past-range sweep* — and every cited real-hardware row (RMA, LoadAdapt, LocoFormer) reports point conditions, not a continuous distance axis. The graded sweep only exists in sim ([[2508.20294|DALI]], [[2412.18781|Offline RL Action Perturbation Eval]]). Building the real sweep IS the contribution; do not let it become a sim paper wearing a robot costume.

**2. ACADEMIC (knows the peer-reviewed literature).**
- *Core position*: The literature already establishes that *outcome-centric* latents extrapolate where *parameter-centric* ones break ([[2606.02280|LDG]] on disabled-actuator / time-varying shifts; [[1812.03399|Latent-Variable MBRL]] explicitly *extrapolates* physical parameters beyond training). So H4 is largely settled and H2's "the latent extrapolates some distance" has prior support — the *novel* claim is narrowly H3: the head-to-head where continued inference beats a robust-fallback as a *function of distance*, which is the exact inverse of [[2412.04323|GRAM]]'s published conclusion.
- *Strongest evidence*: [[2606.02280|LDG]] (outcome-centric beats parameter-centric on structural failure), [[2602.04412|HoRD]] (86.0% zero-shot in an *unseen physics engine* Genesis — inference holding past the training distribution), [[2412.04323|GRAM]] (0.92 ID / 0.79 near-OOD / 0.58 far-OOD — note adaptation *degrades* monotonically OOD, which is evidence *for* GRAM's give-up thesis).
- *Only-this-perspective*: GRAM's own numbers (0.79 -> 0.58 from near to far OOD) show adaptation quality *decaying* with distance. The bet needs continued-inference to *beat robust-fallback at every distance*, but GRAM's data is consistent with adaptation decaying *toward* the robust floor — so the real empirical question is whether there is a *crossover* (adaptation eventually drops below fallback) or merely a *shrinking margin*. The card asserts a "growing gap"; the published evidence is at best a shrinking-but-positive margin, which is a much weaker and possibly non-novel claim.

**3. SKEPTIC (thinks the bet is wrong or already solved).**
- *Core position*: GRAM already answered the boundary question on a real quadruped and concluded the opposite, with uncertainty-gated handoff that *empirically works* (100% on 9 kg / slippery far-OOD hardware). Betting the inverse is betting that a published, hardware-validated negative result is wrong — and the card's own supporting rows (DALI is sim-only, LDG is MuJoCo-only, HoRD is a single OOD point in a different engine) provide no real-robot evidence of the *crossover-beating-fallback* the bet requires.
- *Strongest evidence the card ignores*: GRAM's robust-fallback *is* a strong baseline precisely because far-OOD is where naive adaptation chases noise (the card's own Risk #2). The bet conveniently assumes the estimator's posterior stays calibrated OOD; but every cited estimator (RMA's distilled extrinsics, LDG's contrastive latent) lacks an explicit *uncertainty* estimate, so they cannot even *know* when they have left the envelope — the one row that has it ([[1812.03399|Latent-Variable MBRL]]) is sim-only continuous control.
- *Only-this-perspective*: The whitespace is real but *narrow and fragile*: it is "the head-to-head graded sweep on real hardware", not "a new method". If GRAM's authors run the same sweep first (they have the rig and the topic), the direction is pre-empted overnight. The contribution is a benchmark/finding, not an algorithm — fund it as such or not at all.

**4. ECONOMIST (follows money/incentives).**
- *Core position*: Proprioception-only, zero-real-fine-tune adaptation is the *cheapest possible* sim-to-real story — no real data budget, no reward engineering, no new sensors — which is exactly why labs (Pathak/Skild, MERL, Unitree ecosystem) keep publishing it. The narrative profits whoever owns the quadruped rig and wants a deployment headline without a data-collection line item.
- *Strongest evidence*: [[2409.16578|FLaRe]] *needs RL fine-tuning + reward* (heavier, costlier) yet only reaches 80.7% — the card explicitly frames proprioception-only inference as the cheaper alternative; [[2107.04034|RMA]] and [[2507.07825|LoadAdapt]] are zero-real-data. The incentive gradient points hard toward proprioception-only.
- *Only-this-perspective*: The benchmark substrate is the bottleneck and the moat. There is no named *real-robot distance-past-range* benchmark; DALI/Offline-RL-Action-Perturbation are sim. Whoever defines that benchmark (the SR-vs-distance protocol) captures the citation annuity — that is worth more than the method. But it also means the result is *only* reproducible by groups with the matching hardware, so the finding risks being a single-lab artifact that nobody can contest or replicate (low external validity, high citation-capture).

**5. HISTORIAN (has seen prior waves).**
- *Core position*: This is the explicit-context vs implicit-history cycle replaying for the fourth time: RMA's explicit extrinsics (2021) -> TERT/history-to-action "skip the latent" -> LocoFormer's long-context "just read more history" -> back to "but the explicit posterior tells you *when to trust it*". The adapt-vs-robustify swing is the old model-based-RL "trust your model OOD vs fall back to MPC" debate (Dreamer lineage) wearing a sim-to-real hat.
- *Strongest evidence*: [[2212.07740|TERT]] (history->action, 100% sand where RMA/TCN scores 0% — the "latent is optional" swing), [[2509.23745|LocoFormer]] (Transformer-XL episode-spanning context — the "scale the history" swing), [[2412.04323|GRAM]] (uncertainty-gated fallback — the "don't trust the model OOD" swing). The pendulum has visibly swung between these in <5 years.
- *Only-this-perspective*: Every prior wave that bet "our learned estimate extrapolates past the training distribution" eventually conceded a *bounded* envelope, not unbounded extrapolation (Dreamer needed real data when dynamics shifted hard; pixel world-models gave way to latent then back). So H2 ("accurate only within a measurable envelope") is the *historically inevitable* finding, and H3's "gap grows" is the historically *contested* one. Bet on H2 as the deliverable; treat H3 as the headline you may not get.

#### Prompt 2 — Contradiction Map

1. **Direct clashes.**
   - *Skeptic vs (Practitioner+Academic) on whether the bet is already answered*: Skeptic says GRAM settled the boundary on real hardware (100% far-OOD via fallback) so the inverse is betting against a validated negative; Practitioner/Academic say GRAM *unified* adapt+robust and never ran the *graded distance sweep* of continued-inference-only vs fallback, so the specific head-to-head is open. The concrete clash: is GRAM's 0.58 far-OOD a *ceiling for adaptation* (Skeptic) or *an artifact of handing off too early* (Academic)?
   - *Academic vs card framing on "gap grows"*: Academic reads GRAM's 0.79->0.58 decay as a *shrinking* adaptation margin (possible crossover below fallback), directly contradicting the card's "real-SR gap *growing* with distance".
   - *Economist vs Historian on durability*: Economist says define-the-benchmark-and-win; Historian says the benchmark will show a *bounded envelope* (H2), which deflates the H3 headline the benchmark was built to sell.

2. **Strongest vs weakest perspective.**
   - *Strongest*: ACADEMIC — it is the only one that engages the actual numbers (GRAM 0.92/0.79/0.58, LDG structural-failure) and isolates exactly which sub-hypothesis is novel (H3) vs pre-supported (H2/H4). Its "shrinking margin vs growing gap" distinction is the load-bearing crux.
   - *Weakest*: ECONOMIST — directionally right about incentives and the benchmark moat, but it cannot adjudicate the scientific claim and its "citation-capture" framing is orthogonal to whether the bet is *true*.

3. **The one question that resolves the biggest contradiction.**
   On a single real robot, sweeping deployment dynamics monotonically past the DR range: does continued proprioception-only inference's success rate stay *above* a matched GRAM-style robust-fallback at every distance (gap grows or stays positive), or does it *cross below* fallback at some distance (GRAM's give-up thesis holds)? This single SR-vs-distance crossover plot resolves Skeptic-vs-Academic and confirms/kills the "growing gap" wording.

4. **What every perspective agrees on (likely true).**
   In-range, infer-then-condition beats robustify, and the latent-extrinsics estimate extrapolates *some bounded distance* past the DR range before degrading (H2). Every perspective accepts a *measurable envelope* exists; none disputes RMA/LoadAdapt/LDG in-range results.

5. **The blind spot (addressed by NONE).**
   *Compute/latency and observability of the gating decision itself.* The whole bet hinges on deciding, online, whether you are still inside the trustworthy envelope — but RMA/LDG/TERT estimators carry *no calibrated uncertainty*, and the one Bayesian row (1812.03399) is sim-only. No perspective costed the *runtime uncertainty estimator* needed to operationalize the handoff on a 100 Hz controller, nor whether proprioception even *observes* the relevant dynamics for vision-dominant manipulation (the card flags it as a Risk but no perspective makes it central).

#### Prompt 3 — Synthesis Briefing

1. **CEO summary.** D1's defensible core is *not* "online latent-extrinsics adaptation" (built, deployed, settled in-range by RMA/FLaRe/LoadAdapt) but a single narrow, contested empirical claim: that continued proprioception-only inference *beats* a GRAM-style robust-fallback as deployment moves *past* the randomization range, with the success-rate gap *growing* with distance. That exact head-to-head graded-distance sweep does not exist on real hardware (the only graded protocols, DALI and Offline-RL-Action-Perturbation, are sim-only), so the whitespace is real but fragile: it is a *benchmark-and-finding* contribution, replicable only by groups with the matching rig, and GRAM's own 0.79->0.58 OOD decay is at least as consistent with a *shrinking margin* (or crossover *below* fallback) as with the card's "growing gap". COMMIT only if you reframe the deliverable around H2 (the envelope map) with H3 (the crossover) as the high-variance headline.

2. **Five key findings, ranked by reliability.**
   1. *In-range, infer-then-condition is settled; the novelty is entirely OOD.* Supported by Practitioner, Academic, Skeptic, Economist (RMA, FLaRe 50%->80.7%, LoadAdapt). Challenged by none. Highest reliability — also means the card must not claim in-range results as contribution.
   2. *A bounded extrapolation envelope (H2) exists and is the likely true finding.* Supported by Academic, Historian, Practitioner (LDG, 1812.03399 extrapolates params beyond training, HoRD 86% in unseen engine). Challenged mildly by Skeptic (envelope may be too small to matter). High reliability.
   3. *Outcome-centric beats parameter-centric on unmodeled shifts (H4) is largely pre-established, not novel.* Supported by Academic (LDG structural-failure result, DALI extrapolation). Challenged by no one but *deflates* H4 as a contribution. Medium-high.
   4. *The "gap grows with distance" wording (H3) is contested by GRAM's own data.* Supported by the card's thesis; challenged by Academic (0.79->0.58 = shrinking margin) and Skeptic (GRAM concluded the inverse on hardware). Medium — this is the bet, and it is the least-supported claim.
   5. *The contribution is a benchmark/finding, not a method, and is pre-emption-fragile.* Supported by Skeptic, Economist, Historian. Challenged by Practitioner only insofar as the real sweep is genuinely hard to build. Medium — strategic, not empirical.

3. **Hidden cross-perspective connection.** Economist's "whoever defines the SR-vs-distance benchmark captures the citation annuity" and Historian's "every prior wave conceded a bounded envelope" are the same fact from two sides: the *benchmark* (the distance axis) is durable and ownable, but the *headline it was built to sell* (unbounded "gap grows") is historically the part that gets walked back to "bounded envelope". The asset is the protocol; the liability is the thesis sentence.

4. **Actionable insight for the researcher: DE-RISK, then conditionally COMMIT.** Do not commit to H3's "growing gap" as the thesis. Reframe the deliverable as: *"the real-robot SR-vs-distance envelope map for proprioception-only adaptation, with the GRAM-fallback crossover located."* Concrete next experiment: on one quadruped (Go2/A1), train one shared base policy; deploy two heads — continued-inference (RMA/LDG-style) and a matched GRAM-style uncertainty-gated robust-fallback — and sweep a *single* dynamics axis (e.g. payload or friction) monotonically from inside the DR range to far past it, using DALI's Interpolate/Extrapolate/Mixed regime definitions as the sim pre-registration and then the *physical* sweep (graded lead-ball payload a la LoadAdapt) as the real instrument. Report SR-vs-distance for both heads and locate the crossover. If continued-inference stays above fallback at every tested distance -> H3 confirmed, headline earned. If it crosses below -> you still publish the *envelope map* (H2) and the crossover location as the finding, which is GRAM-complementary and still novel. Pre-register the crossover-vs-shrinking-margin distinction so the result is publishable either way. DROP the manipulation H1 sub-claim as a primary axis: In-Hand RMA / COCOI / Joint-Sensor-In-Hand already close the in-range contact residual without reward, so it is a baseline-to-beat with weaker payoff than the locomotion crossover.

5. **Frontier question.** Can the estimator *know when it has left the envelope* — i.e. can a calibrated, runtime-cheap (100 Hz-compatible) uncertainty signal on the latent-extrinsics posterior predict the H3 crossover point in advance, turning the adapt-vs-robustify handoff from a fixed Epinet threshold (GRAM) into a *self-locating* boundary? If yes, the whole adapt-vs-robust dichotomy dissolves into one uncertainty-gated controller and the "which side wins OOD" question becomes "where does the robot place the gate", which would change the framing of the entire D-cluster.

---

#### Prompt 4 — Peer Review (independent)

Reviewer stance: skeptical Stanford robotics professor. I read D1 (Sim2Real.md L814-887) and spot-checked the load-bearing numbers against the KH notes for GRAM (2412.04323), DALI (2508.20294), Offline-RL Action Perturbation (2412.18781), LoadAdapt (2507.07825), and HoRD (2602.04412). The briefing is well-reasoned and mostly honest, but it under-reports one hardware fact that cuts against its own headline, and its star counter-argument is itself under-determined by the cited data.

##### 1. Confidence scores (1-10, reliability)

1. **In-range infer-then-condition is settled; novelty is entirely OOD — 9/10.** Verified directly: RMA, FLaRe (50%->80.7%), LoadAdapt (near-Oracle, real Go2) all close the in-range case and the card already concedes it. This is the most robust claim in the briefing; the only reason it is not 10 is that "settled" is asserted, not shown to be unbeatable in-range.

2. **A bounded extrapolation envelope (H2) exists and is the likely true finding — 7/10.** The supporting evidence is real but indirect. 1812.03399 extrapolates *parameters* beyond training in sim HalfCheetah; HoRD hits 86% in an unseen *physics engine* (a single cross-sim point, not a graded distance); DALI's Extrapolate regime is sim-only. None of these is a real-robot graded envelope, so "exists" is an inference, not a measurement. The direction of the bet is sound; the magnitude is unconstrained.

3. **Outcome-centric beats parameter-centric (H4) is largely pre-established, not novel — 7/10.** LDG's structural-failure argument and DALI's extrapolation gains support it, and the briefing is right to deflate H4 as a contribution. Slight reservation: "largely pre-established" is doing a lot of work — DALI is sim-only and LDG's real-hardware outcome-centric-vs-parameter head-to-head on *disabled-actuator* shifts is not actually shown, so H4 is deflated correctly but the deflation itself rests on sim evidence.

4. **"Gap grows with distance" (H3) is contested by GRAM's own data — 4/10.** This is the briefing's headline finding and it is the one I most distrust *as stated*. The briefing says GRAM's 0.79->0.58 is "at least as consistent with a shrinking margin." But the GRAM note gives **no baseline OOD curve** to subtract, so neither "growing gap" (card) nor "shrinking margin" (briefing) is computable from the cited number — both are interpretations of an absolute return, not a margin. Worse, the briefing omits GRAM L41: on **real hardware**, GRAM held **100% SR in far-OOD (9 kg payload, slippery) where baselines largely failed**. That hardware fact is the strongest single piece of evidence against H3, and the briefing does not cite it. The finding's *conclusion* (don't bet the thesis on H3) is correct and I endorse it, but its *justification* (the 0.79->0.58 margin reading) is itself a soft claim, and it leaves the actual smoking gun on the table.

5. **Contribution is a benchmark/finding not a method, and is pre-emption-fragile — 6/10.** Plausible and strategically sharp. The "citation annuity" / "bounded envelope gets walked back" synthesis is a genuine insight. But "pre-emption-fragile" is an unfalsifiable strategic worry without a named competitor closing in; GRAM itself already partly occupies the hardware boundary, which is evidence the whitespace is narrower than the card implies, not that someone is about to scoop a protocol nobody has built.

Mean = 6.6, min = 4.

##### 2. Weakest link

Finding 4 (H3 "growing gap"). The specific information needed to verify it: a **baseline (DR or contextual-RL) OOD return curve over the same near/far axis as GRAM's 0.79/0.58**, so a *margin* (GRAM-minus-baseline) can actually be computed — and, separately, **GRAM's far-OOD hardware SR for the continued-inference head vs the robust-fallback head**, which is the head-to-head the card claims does not exist. Until one of those exists, neither "growing gap" nor "shrinking margin" is supported by data; both are priors dressed as readings. The single fact that most moves this: GRAM's note already reports **100% hardware SR in far-OOD with the fallback engaged** (L41) — that should be in the briefing and it pushes against H3, so the briefing is currently citing the half of GRAM that helps it.

##### 3. Bias check

**Skeptic over-dominated, with the Economist a close second.** Every finding is framed as a reason the thesis is fragile, and the actionable insight is "de-risk then maybe commit" — a Skeptic's posture. That is largely the right call here, but it produced one blind spot: the briefing's skepticism is aimed entirely at the *card's* over-reach (the "growing gap") and not at *its own* counter-claim (the "shrinking margin" reading), which is equally under-determined. A balanced synthesis would have flagged that GRAM's number cannot adjudicate either direction, rather than implicitly siding with the shrinking-margin reading. The Practitioner is present (RMA/FLaRe/LoadAdapt numbers are concrete) but is used only to support "in-range is settled," not to interrogate whether the proposed Go2 two-head rig can actually be built and instrumented at the precision the finding requires.

##### 4. Missing perspective (a 6th angle that would change the conclusions)

**The Metrologist / measurement-validity perspective.** All five perspectives debate *which side wins OOD*; none asks whether "SR-vs-distance" is even a well-posed measurement. The proposed instrument — a physical lead-ball payload sweep on one Go2 — confounds at least three things the card treats as one axis: (a) payload mass, (b) CoM shift, and (c) the contact/friction change from the ball rig itself. GRAM and LoadAdapt both already show payload is the *easy* axis where fallback and adaptation both succeed near 100% on hardware, so a payload sweep may never reach the regime where the two heads separate — the crossover could sit outside the dynamic range of the chosen instrument. This perspective changes the conclusion: the binding constraint is not "which head wins" but "can any single real-hardware axis be swept far enough, cleanly enough, to *locate* a crossover at all." If not, the whole deliverable (H2 envelope map) is unbuildable as specified, and the frontier question (uncertainty signal predicting the crossover) is premature. This would move the recommendation from "DE-RISK then conditionally COMMIT" to "first prove the instrument has a measurable crossover regime on a pilot axis before committing the rig."

##### 5. Overall grade and top fixes

**Grade: B+.** This is a strong, intellectually honest triage that correctly identifies the real defensible core (the protocol/envelope), correctly demotes the in-range and H4 claims, and correctly refuses to bet the thesis on H3. It loses the A tier on three things: (1) it omits GRAM's far-OOD *hardware* 100% SR, the single most decisive datum against its own headline; (2) it lets its own counter-reading ("0.79->0.58 = shrinking margin") stand as if it were derivable from the data when it is not — symmetric over-reach to the card's; (3) it never stress-tests whether the proposed real instrument can reach the crossover regime.

Top fixes, in order:
1. **Cite GRAM L41 (100% far-OOD hardware SR, fallback engaged) explicitly** and state that it weakens H3 *more* than the sim 0.79/0.58 decay does. The honest framing is: GRAM already showed the robust-fallback succeeds on the easy real axis (payload), so H3 must be demonstrated on a *harder* axis (friction/actuator) where fallback is conservative-but-suboptimal, not on payload.
2. **Replace "shrinking margin vs growing gap" with "no margin is computable from GRAM's reported absolute returns."** Pre-register the baseline OOD curve as a required deliverable so the result is interpretable either way — this is consistent with the briefing's own "publish either way" instinct but fixes the soft claim that currently justifies it.
3. **Add a pilot feasibility gate before the rig commit:** demonstrate on one axis (in sim, via DALI's Extrapolate regime) that the two heads measurably separate, and that the separation falls inside the dynamic range a physical lead-ball sweep can reach, before building the real two-head Go2 experiment. Otherwise the envelope map may be unbuildable on the chosen instrument.

Endorsed without change: dropping manipulation H1 as a primary axis (In-Hand RMA / COCOI / Joint-Sensor-In-Hand close the in-range residual with no reward — verified in KH — so it is a baseline-to-beat, not whitespace), and treating the protocol as the durable asset and the thesis sentence as the liability.

---

### D2 (Direction review)

Topic: the bet that *BPTT through a learned-residual hybrid differentiable model adapting a learned neural policy* corrects an OOD disturbance in ≤3 steps / 4.5 s with 81% hover-error reduction (vs L1-MPC; 55% vs DATT), recovering SR on a disturbance the residual captures but M-GAPS's analytical-only model cannot represent, at matched wall-clock.

#### Prompt 1 — Five Perspectives

**1. PRACTITIONER (builds/deploys policies daily).**
- *Core position*: The 81% / 3-step / 4.5-s numbers in [[2508.21065|Learning on the Fly]] are *simulation* numbers; the KH note is explicit ("achieved superior performance **in simulation**, reducing hovering error by 81%"), with real-world quadrotor results stated only qualitatively ("confirmed the framework's ability to rapidly adapt"). A practitioner reads that as: the headline metric the bet quotes is not the hardware metric.
- *Strongest supporting evidence*: 4.5 s of in-flight wall-clock "policy training" while the platform is airborne is a real safety window. The card itself concedes this (Risk 3, H5: "a bad gradient step destabilizes the platform"), and the closest analytical competitor [[2507.10914|M-GAPS]] converges in 8–12 s on a *real* quadrotor with no learned residual to blow up — the cheaper, already-flying option.
- *Only-this-perspective insight*: BPTT through a learned residual on an onboard compute budget is the unmodeled cost. M-GAPS deliberately uses O(1)-per-step analytic gradients (SymForce) precisely because embedded compute is the constraint; "exact gradient through a learned MLP residual at flight-control rates" is the thing that will not fit, and nobody has shown the residual loop running onboard at control rate on hardware.

**2. ACADEMIC (knows the peer-reviewed literature).**
- *Core position*: The bet's "first principle" (if the model is differentiable, adapting is gradient descent) is correct but is the *premise of the entire FoPG/BPTT/differentiable-sim lineage*, not a new claim — the card admits the speed assumption "fell to the GAPS/DiffTune/Differentiable-Physics lineage by ~2022." The genuinely new claim narrows to one word: the residual must *capture* a disturbance the analytical core cannot *represent*, then BPTT adapts a *neural* policy.
- *Strongest supporting evidence*: The literature already separates these regimes. [[2505.15589|Reflexive World Models]] does exact gradient adaptation but off a *learned* WM reference (0.6003 vs 0.3600 reward, 1.8 h vs 16.5 h) — the D2/D3 hinge; [[2606.09640|Physics-Aware Sparse EL]] *captures* payload-release on an analytical Euler-Lagrange core via a learned force-residual across 5 platforms but adapts the *model*, not a policy, by Bayesian update. So both halves of the bet exist separately; the unclaimed cell is their conjunction.
- *Only-this-perspective insight*: H1's falsifier is the whole paper. If on a disturbance off the nominal model M-GAPS's analytical gradient matches the learned-residual loop, "the residual buys nothing" — and the academic's read of [[2205.06908|Neural-Fly]] (CIT, real strong-wind agile flight via a learned wind-invariant representation + composite adaptive control) is that *learned-feature online adaptation in wind on real hardware is a 2022 result*, so the residual must be shown to beat a tuned analytical/feature baseline, not just an untuned one.

**3. SKEPTIC (thinks the bet is wrong or already solved).**
- *Core position*: The anchor [[2508.21065|Learning on the Fly]] **already is** the bet, verbatim — "low-fidelity analytical core + learned residual MLP," "BPTT," "policy adaptation," "81%, 3 steps / 4.5 s." The card's own bet sentence quotes that paper's own numbers as the prediction. There is no whitespace; there is a paper, and the "direction" is a request to re-run that paper's ablations.
- *Strongest supporting evidence*: Every H1–H5 row lands back on [[2508.21065|Learning on the Fly]] as the method-under-test; the "new" experiments (H2 residual-size sweep, H3 contact erosion, H4 vs world-model, H5 bounded update) are characterization studies *of the anchor*, not a new mechanism. The card concedes the speed question is "settled" (M-GAPS) and the capture question has a sibling that already demonstrates it ([[2606.09640|Physics-Aware Sparse EL]] captures payload-release).
- *Only-this-perspective insight*: The convenient omission is that "captures vs tolerates" may be a distinction without a deployable difference — a learned residual that *captures* an unmodeled aero effect is also a learned residual that *overfits transient noise* (the card's own Risk 2), and a *tolerating* analytical controller is provably stable. On real hardware the "capturing" advantage can be net-negative.

**4. ECONOMIST (follows money/incentives).**
- *Core position*: This is a single-lab agenda. The anchor, [[2410.03076|Residual Policy via Differentiable Sim]], and [[2602.10111|Learning Agile Quadrotor Flight in the Real World]] are all RPG/UZH-Scaramuzza; the differentiable-sim-for-drones narrative is the asset of one group that owns the hardware, the sim (RotorPy-adjacent), and the publication cadence. Committing here is competing on the incumbent's home turf with the incumbent's own metric.
- *Strongest supporting evidence*: The proposed benchmark substrate [[2510.03471|Quadcopter Control Eval Suite]] (RotorPy, position-RMSE 0.030–0.074 m, L1-MPC/Geometric/INDI baselines) is a *standardized aerial-control* suite — cheap, simulated, saturating. The reward gradient points at "+x% on a quad benchmark," exactly the benchmark-chasing the card elsewhere warns about, and the real-robot cost (the expensive, differentiating part) is structurally underweighted by the incentive.
- *Only-this-perspective insight*: The money is in *generalist* policies (VLA / cross-embodiment), not in a per-platform quadrotor adaptation loop. Whoever funds robot learning funds transfer that amortizes across fleets; a 4.5-s single-drone wind-correction loop has no fleet-scaling story, so it wins a workshop, not a roadmap.

**5. HISTORIAN (has seen prior waves).**
- *Core position*: This is the analytical-vs-learned-residual swing replayed. Differentiable-physics online adaptation (Differentiable-Physics Online MPC+SysID, RA-L'22; GAPS/DiffTune) was the "model-based gradient is fast" wave; the learned-residual-hybrid is the "but learned components capture what physics misses" counter-swing — and the field has run this loop before with Neural-Fly (2022) and with pixel-vs-latent world models.
- *Strongest supporting evidence*: Every prior cycle resolved the same way — the learned component wins *in-distribution and in sim*, the analytical/structured component wins *on stability and OOD guarantees*, and the durable systems went *hybrid with the structure load-bearing* (exactly what [[2606.09640|Physics-Aware Sparse EL]] does: keep inertia/Coriolis fixed, adapt only the force residual). [[2603.04029|Self-Adapting RL]]'s minutes-long DreamerV3 fine-tune is this decade's Dreamer-lineage tax the bet is trying to dodge.
- *Only-this-perspective insight*: The historically winning move is not "learned residual beats analytical," it is "where do you put the structure so the learned part is small enough to adapt in 3 steps and bounded enough not to destabilize." The bet that survives is a *scoping* result (the analytical-core boundary, H3), not a *superiority* result (H1).

#### Prompt 2 — Contradiction Map

1. **Direct clashes.**
   - *Skeptic vs the card (whitespace)*: Skeptic says the anchor [[2508.21065|Learning on the Fly]] **is** the bet (its own 81% / 3-step numbers are quoted as the prediction); the card frames D2 as open whitespace against M-GAPS. Specific claim in tension: is D2 a *new method* or a *characterization of one existing paper*?
   - *Practitioner vs Academic on the headline metric*: Practitioner says 81% / 3-step / 4.5 s are *sim* numbers (KH note says "in simulation"); the bet sentence presents them as the deliverable. Academic adds that the real comparison ([[2205.06908|Neural-Fly]], real wind, learned features) is omitted.
   - *Skeptic vs Historian on "capture"*: Skeptic says capturing = overfitting transient noise (net-negative on hardware); Historian says capture is fine *if scoped and structurally bounded* — they agree capture is risky, disagree on whether scoping rescues it.

2. **Strongest vs weakest.**
   - *Strongest*: SKEPTIC — its claim is checkable against the card's own text: the bet sentence literally quotes the anchor's metrics, and all five hypotheses land back on the anchor. alphaxiv corroborates ([[2410.03076]] is training-time RPL, not the threat; the only paper doing this *exact* online loop is the anchor itself).
   - *Weakest*: ECONOMIST — directionally true (single-lab, benchmark-saturation risk) but least falsifiable and least specific to the technical bet; it argues against the *area*, not the *claim*.

3. **Resolving question.** On a disturbance demonstrably *off* the nominal analytical model (e.g. rotor-fault or off-center payload from [[2510.03471|Quadcopter Control Eval Suite]]), does the learned-residual BPTT loop recover *measurably more* real-hardware SR than (a) M-GAPS's analytical-only gradient AND (b) [[2205.06908|Neural-Fly]]'s learned-feature adaptive control, at matched wall-clock? This single experiment resolves the whitespace clash (#1a), the metric clash (#1b), and H1's falsifier at once.

4. **Universal agreement (likely true).** Every perspective agrees the analytical-vs-learned-residual *split is real and the regime boundary is the substantive finding* — i.e. learned residuals help where the analytical core is wrong (aerial aero, payload), the analytical route suffices where it is accurate, and the durable contribution is *characterizing that boundary* (H3) rather than a blanket superiority claim (H1).

5. **Blind spot (none addressed).** Nobody addressed **online identifiability / observability**: a 3-step single-trajectory adaptation must *infer* the residual from a few seconds of one trajectory, and whether that trajectory is persistently exciting enough to identify the disturbance (vs aliasing sensor noise into the residual) is an estimation-theory question the card frames purely as an optimization-speed question. Fast convergence to the *wrong* residual is the unexamined failure mode.

#### Prompt 3 — Synthesis Briefing

1. **CEO summary.** D2's underlying mechanism is real and the closest competitor analysis is honest, but the bet has a structural problem: its single anchor, [[2508.21065|Learning on the Fly]], *already instantiates the exact mechanism the bet proposes*, and the card's own falsifiable prediction is that paper's own simulation metrics. The genuinely open contribution is therefore not "do learned-residual BPTT TTA" (done) but the narrow, defensible scoping result H1/H3 frame: *demonstrate on real hardware that the learned residual captures a specific off-nominal disturbance that the strongest analytical/learned-feature baselines (M-GAPS, Neural-Fly) cannot, and map where that advantage erodes.* As stated, the headline is a re-run of an incumbent lab's paper; reframed as a hardware head-to-head on an off-nominal disturbance with the right baselines, it is a thin but real wedge.

2. **Five key findings (ranked by reliability).**
   - **F1 (highest).** The bet's headline numbers (81% / 3-step / 4.5 s) are the anchor's *simulation* results, not demonstrated hardware metrics. *Support*: Practitioner, Academic (KH note 2508.21065 says "in simulation"). *Challenge*: none — the card itself sources them to the anchor.
   - **F2.** The anchor *is* the bet; H1–H5 are characterizations of one existing method, not a new mechanism. *Support*: Skeptic, Economist. *Challenge*: the card (frames it as whitespace vs M-GAPS) — but the framing relies on M-GAPS being the only competitor, which understates the anchor's coverage.
   - **F3.** Both halves of the bet exist separately ([[2505.15589|Reflexive World Models]] = exact gradient off learned reference; [[2606.09640|Physics-Aware Sparse EL]] = learned residual captures payload-release on analytical core); only their conjunction-into-a-policy-loop is unclaimed. *Support*: Academic, Historian. *Challenge*: Skeptic (says the conjunction is the anchor anyway).
   - **F4.** The durable result is a *scoping/boundary* claim (H3: where the analytical-core advantage erodes), not a *superiority* claim (H1). *Support*: Historian, Academic. *Challenge*: the card leads with H1 as "front line."
   - **F5 (lowest, still actionable).** The right baselines are missing: M-GAPS (cited) is analytical-only, but [[2205.06908|Neural-Fly]] (learned-feature online wind adaptation, real hardware, 2022) is the omitted strong comparator that could already eat H1. *Support*: Academic, Economist. *Challenge*: not in the card, so unverified against full Neural-Fly results — flagged, not proven.

3. **Hidden cross-perspective connection.** The Practitioner's onboard-compute objection and the Historian's "put the structure so the learned part is small" prescription are the *same constraint* viewed from two ends: the learned residual must be small enough to (a) backprop through at flight-control rate on embedded compute AND (b) adapt in 3 steps without destabilizing — and [[2606.09640|Physics-Aware Sparse EL]]'s sparse-active-subspace residual is the existing answer to both. The compute limit and the stability limit are one design variable: residual capacity.

4. **Actionable insight — DE-RISK (do not COMMIT as headline; do not DROP).** The mechanism is sound but the bet as written re-derives an incumbent paper. De-risk by re-centering on the one experiment nobody has run: **a real-quadrotor head-to-head on an off-nominal disturbance** (rotor-fault or off-center payload from [[2510.03471|Quadcopter Control Eval Suite]]) comparing {learned-residual BPTT (anchor)} vs {M-GAPS analytical gradient} vs {Neural-Fly learned-feature adaptive control} vs {Self-Adapting RL sampled fine-tune}, reporting *hardware* SR, steps-to-correct, AND onboard wall-clock at control rate. Concrete next experiment: reproduce the anchor's loop, then run H1's falsifier on hardware with Neural-Fly added as a baseline; if the learned residual does not beat *both* analytical and learned-feature baselines on an off-nominal disturbance at matched compute, the bet collapses to a scoping note (H3) — which is still publishable but is a workshop result, not a flagship.

5. **Frontier question.** Is "capture vs tolerate" decidable *online from a single short trajectory* — i.e. can the system *identify* that a disturbance is off-nominal (and size the residual accordingly) fast enough and observably enough to act, before it has overfit transient noise? If yes, the learned residual earns its place and the bet generalizes beyond aerial to contact-rich regimes; if no, the analytical/structured route wins on stability and the entire learned-residual TTA line is scoped to in-distribution sim demos.

---

#### Prompt 4 — Peer Review (independent)

*Reviewer stance: skeptical robotics professor. The synthesis is unusually self-critical (it talks itself down from COMMIT to DE-RISK), which is good practice but also a tell: a briefing that pre-concedes its own weakness can smuggle an under-verified claim through as a "flagged" finding. I checked each finding against the KH notes and the card text, not against the synthesis's own confidence labels.*

##### 1. CONFIDENCE SCORES (1–10 reliability)

- **F1 — 81%/3-step/4.5s are the anchor's SIM numbers, not hardware: 8/10.** Verified directly: KH note 2508.21065 Results bullet 1 reads "achieved superior performance **in simulation**, reducing hovering error by 81% compared to L1-MPC and 55%." The card's "Key targets" row quotes exactly these as the deliverable.
