Docked two points because the finding over-reaches in one direction the synthesis repeats: bullet 3 of the *same* note says "Real-world experiments on quadrotors with added mass and wind confirmed the framework's ability to rapidly adapt," and bullet 2 (3 steps / 4.5 s) is NOT sim-tagged in the note. So the precise truth is "the 81%/55% percentages are sim; hardware adaptation was demonstrated qualitatively." The CEO summary's call to "demonstrate on REAL hardware" (as if hardware is unrun) is slightly stronger than the evidence: the anchor flew on real quadrotors; what it lacks is a *quantified hardware SR head-to-head on an off-nominal disturbance*. That narrower claim is airtight; the broader one is not.

- **F2 — the anchor IS the bet; H1–H5 characterize one paper: 8/10.** Strongly grounded against the card's own text. The bet sentence (line 906) quotes the anchor's 81%/55%/3-step/4.5s as its *prediction*; every hypothesis row (931, 936, 941, 946, 951) lands method-under-test back on Learning-on-the-Fly. The anchor's KH Method section is the bet verbatim ("low-fidelity analytical core + learned residual MLP," "BPTT," "policy adaptation"). This is the most load-bearing and best-supported finding in the briefing. One point off because "is the bet *verbatim*" slightly overstates: the anchor does the mechanism, but the card's H1 *contribution* (a controlled head-to-head isolating capture-vs-tolerate against the right baselines) is genuinely not in the anchor, which compares only to L1-MPC/DATT, not to M-GAPS or a learned-feature method. So it is fairer to say "the anchor owns the mechanism; the bet's residual contribution is a comparison study," which is exactly F4.

- **F3 — both halves exist separately, only the conjunction is open: 7/10.** Both KH notes check out: 2505.15589 (Reflexive WM) does exact gradient adaptation against a *learned* WM reference by adjusting actions not params; 2606.09640 (Physics-Aware Sparse EL) captures payload-release on an analytical EL core via a learned force-residual, Bayesian-updated, model-not-policy, across 5 platforms. The "decomposition into two existing halves" is real and well-cited. Docked because the Skeptic's challenge (recorded in the finding itself) is not actually resolved: if the conjunction-into-a-policy-loop is *exactly* what the anchor already does, then "only the conjunction is unclaimed" and "the anchor is the bet" cannot both be fully true. The briefing leaves this internal tension standing. The honest version is that the anchor does conjunction-for-aerial-hover, and the open cell is conjunction-with-a-controlled-capture-vs-tolerate-baseline, which is again F4 wearing a different hat.

- **F4 — the durable result is a scoping claim (H3), not superiority (H1): 7/10.** This is the synthesis's best judgement and I largely agree as taste: boundary/where-it-erodes results age better than blanket-superiority claims, and the Historian's "the learned part wins in-distribution, structure wins on OOD stability" is the correct prior. But it is an *opinion about publishability*, not a verified fact, so it cannot score as high as F1/F2. It is also slightly self-fulfilling: the briefing first reframes the contribution as scoping, then concludes the durable contribution is scoping. A skeptic would note H3's own test (line 940: "compare steps-to-correct on aerial vs contact-rich tasks") is under-specified, there is no named contact-rich benchmark or platform, so the "durable" result currently has no substrate.

- **F5 — the right baselines are missing; Neural-Fly omitted: 4/10.** This is the weakest finding and the briefing correctly self-labels it "lowest, still actionable / unverified." Neural-Fly (2205.06908) is NOT in the KnowledgeHub: it appears nowhere in the vault except the analyst's own Prompt-1/2 parts. So (a) the claim that Neural-Fly "could already eat H1" is unverifiable from any vault note: nobody has read its actual numbers, regime, or whether its composite-adaptive-control wind result even covers the *off-nominal rotor-fault / payload-release* disturbance H1 targets; and (b) you cannot fairly fault the *card* for omitting a paper that is not in the vault. The finding is a reasonable *literature hypothesis* a reviewer would raise, but presenting it as a "missing baseline" is over-stated: it is a baseline-to-go-find. Neural-Fly is wind-adaptation on a known model class; whether it is even the right comparator for a residual that captures *off-nominal* effects is itself the open question, not a settled gap.

##### 2. WEAKEST LINK

**F5 (Neural-Fly is the omitted comparator that could already eat H1).** The specific information needed to verify: (1) ingest 2205.06908 into the KH and read its actual disturbance regime, does its wind-invariant representation + composite adaptive control generalize to *off-nominal* disturbances (rotor-fault, off-center payload, unmodeled aero) or only to wind it was designed for? (2) its real-hardware SR / tracking-error numbers under those specific disturbances at matched compute; and (3) whether its learned feature is "learned-residual-equivalent" or a fundamentally weaker class (a fixed wind-basis vs an MLP residual). Until 2205.06908 is in the vault and read, F5 is an assertion, and the briefing's headline experiment design (which budgets a Neural-Fly arm) is resting on an unread paper.

##### 3. BIAS CHECK

**The SKEPTIC over-dominated.** The whole briefing is organized around the Skeptic's thesis ("the anchor IS the bet"): F2 is Skeptic, F4/F3 are Skeptic-compatible scoping retreats, and the CEO summary, actionable insight, and frontier question all flow from "this re-derives an incumbent paper." The contradiction map even crowned the Skeptic "strongest" and the Economist "weakest," then the synthesis weighted findings accordingly. The cost: the Skeptic's own *self-refutation* was never priced in. If the anchor already does BPTT-through-learned-residual-hybrid-policy on real quadrotors, then F1's "but it's only sim" and F2's "it's already done" are in tension, the synthesis quotes the anchor as simultaneously *too-done* (F2: nothing left) and *not-done-enough* (F1: sim only). A balanced synthesis would have resolved that the anchor is done-for-hover-in-sim-plus-qualitative-hardware and open-for-controlled-off-nominal-hardware-comparison, which is a *narrower, more defensible* and more positive read than the Skeptic's flat "re-run." The Economist's structural point (single-lab RPG/UZH home-turf) was sound and under-used. The Practitioner's onboard-compute objection (BPTT through an MLP residual at flight-control rate) is arguably the single most decision-relevant finding and got demoted into a "hidden connection" footnote rather than a ranked finding.

##### 4. MISSING PERSPECTIVE

**The Control Theorist / Estimation-Theory perspective.** The briefing's own contradiction map flagged the blind spot (online identifiability / persistent excitation) and then dropped it: it never made it into the five findings. A controls reviewer would say the deciding question is not "is the gradient exact" but "is a 3-step single-trajectory window persistently exciting enough to *identify* the residual rather than alias sensor noise into it", and, separately, *can you certify stability of a policy whose dynamics model is being updated in the loop mid-flight?* M-GAPS and Neural-Fly come with adaptive-control stability arguments (Lyapunov / regret bounds); a learned-residual BPTT loop adapting a neural policy has none stated. This would change the conclusion: the durable contribution might not be H3's empirical scoping at all, but a *stability/identifiability certificate* for when the capture loop is safe, which is a stronger and more novel result than a benchmark head-to-head, and it reframes Neural-Fly/M-GAPS not as "baselines to beat on SR" but as "the certified routes the learned loop must match on guarantees, not just numbers." The frontier question gestures at this ("before it has overfit transient noise") but treats it as an empirical timing question, not a formal observability one.

##### 5. OVERALL GRADE

**B+.**

The briefing is honest, well-sourced on its core claim (F1/F2 are checkable against KH notes and the card text and they check out), and arrives at a defensible, appropriately humble recommendation (DE-RISK, not COMMIT, not DROP). That is genuinely good research hygiene. It loses the A because: (a) it leans on one unverified, not-in-vault paper (Neural-Fly) as a load-bearing element of both a ranked finding *and* the headline experiment design; (b) it never resolves its own internal F1-vs-F2 tension (sim-only vs already-done), letting the Skeptic frame both as faults; and (c) it relegates the two most decision-relevant issues, onboard-compute feasibility of BPTT-through-an-MLP-at-control-rate, and online identifiability/stability certification, to a footnote and a dropped blind spot rather than ranking them.

Top things to fix, in order:
1. **Ingest and read Neural-Fly (2205.06908) before citing it as a missing baseline.** Either confirm it covers the off-nominal regime (then it is a real F5) or find that it is wind-only (then F5 weakens and the headline experiment's Neural-Fly arm needs a different learned-feature comparator). Do not design a flagship experiment around an unread paper.
2. **Resolve the F1/F2 contradiction explicitly.** State the precise open cell: the anchor demonstrated hover adaptation in sim (81%/55%) plus *qualitative* hardware on wind/added-mass; what is unrun is a *quantified hardware SR head-to-head on an off-nominal disturbance against the right baselines.* That single sentence converts "re-run of an incumbent paper" into a crisp, novel wedge and stops the briefing from talking itself down too far.
3. **Promote the Practitioner compute objection and add the controls/estimation lens.** Make "does BPTT-through-a-learned-residual run onboard at control rate" a first-class falsifier, and add a stability/persistent-excitation criterion, the bet's most likely real failure is not "M-GAPS ties on SR" but "the loop can't run onboard fast enough and/or can't be certified stable mid-flight." H5 (bounded update) gestures at this but needs the formal version.

---

### D3 (Direction review)

Topic: the FP "bet" verbatim — *An AdaWorldPolicy-style unified WM+force+action loop drives **4 Hz real-robot** online adaptation to unseen dynamics with no real reward, holding **0.96 LIBERO-10** under OOD where a static policy degrades, **and** its **force-prediction-error term beats a T3VF-style image-foresight-only head on contact-rich OOD** (the regime image foresight is blind to).* Falsifiable: if image-foresight prediction error matches the force-augmented loop on contact OOD (or a static policy holds 0.96 under the same OOD), the force term and the real-robot unified backbone buy nothing over T3VF's sim result.

Novelty stress-test result up front: the alphaxiv `discover_papers` MCP **worked** this run (returned ranked results, `get_paper_content` confirmed two contested papers). The mechanism layer of the bet (unified WM+force+action, online LoRA from WM prediction error, 4 Hz real, no reward) is *already published as the card's own anchor* [[2602.20057|AdaWorldPolicy]]. The surviving novelty is only the **isolated H3 wedge**: that the *force*-prediction-**error** term specifically (vs image-foresight only) is what carries contact-OOD recovery, demonstrated with a controlled ablation and a calibration gate (H5). That wedge is now *flanked* by two June-2026 papers the card does not cite: [[2606.08555]] **FAWAM** (force-tracking-error → closed-loop residual correction, real FR3, recovers contact perturbations) and **TacForeSight** (2606.11184, force-conditioned tactile foresight, recovers in-process contact perturbations, real xArm7). Neither does online *weight* updates from force prediction error, so the exact wedge is intact — but the *claimed effect* (force-prediction-residual recovers contact OOD that vision misses) is independently demonstrated, collapsing H3's surprise value.

#### Prompt 1 — Five Perspectives

**1. PRACTITIONER (builds/deploys policies daily).**
- *Core position*: The bet's hard part is not the signal, it's that **online weight updates on a multi-billion-param DiT at 4 Hz on real hardware** is operationally terrifying. AdaWorldPolicy's own headline is 4 Hz LoRA, but a deploy engineer reads "4 Hz" as "the control loop now depends on a backprop step that can diverge mid-contact," and a poisoned gradient under contact OOD is a *force spike into the workpiece*, not a softly-wrong number.
- *Strongest supporting evidence*: FAWAM (2606.08555) is the practitioner's tell — it gets **85.0% vs 48.75%** vision-only on real contact tasks using a **residual corrector at 10 Hz** trained *offline on 20 human-intervention episodes*, with a binary gate (τ=0.8) so corrections fire sparsely. It deliberately avoids online weight updates. TacForeSight (2606.11184) likewise keeps the policy **frozen** at deploy and gets **86.7%** under perturbation. The field's best contact-OOD recovery results come from *not* updating weights online.
- *The one thing only this perspective says*: The force-prediction-error term may be real, but **the correction mechanism (online LoRA) is the liability, not the asset** — H3 conflates "force-error is the right signal" with "online weight-update is the right actuator," and FAWAM shows you can have the former with a safer residual actuator. De-risk by testing the force signal inside a *residual/gated* corrector first, on hardware, before betting the paper on online LoRA.

**2. ACADEMIC (knows the peer-reviewed literature).**
- *Core position*: The card is honest that the mechanism is consensus ([[2007.04309]] broke "needs reward" in 2021; [[2605.08215|T3VF]] made prediction-error-as-reward consensus in sim), so the paper *must* live or die on H3. But the literature already contains the H3 effect under different names, so the contribution is an **ablation-isolation claim**, not a mechanism claim — a weaker publication posture.
- *Strongest supporting evidence*: [[2602.20057|AdaWorldPolicy]]'s own ablation already "confirms the critical role of ... the force predictor." FAWAM's ablation isolates **+17.5%** from joint action-force *prediction* over force-as-observation, and **0% vs 85%** Swiping-P when cross-attending current↔future tactile is removed — i.e. the field has *already run* the "is the force-prediction term load-bearing for contact OOD" ablation and answered yes. TacForeSight shows wrist-wrench conditioning beats RGB/state conditioning for tactile forecasting (MSE 0.017 vs 0.027).
- *The one thing only this perspective says*: H3's falsifier ("image-foresight matches force-augmented on contact OOD") will almost certainly resolve in force's favor — but **that's the predicted, unsurprising outcome**, so winning H3 is not a contribution; the *only* defensible novelty is the conjunction **force-prediction-error-driven online weight update + conformal calibration gate (H5)**, which no cited or discovered paper does. Reframe the paper around H5, not H3.

**3. SKEPTIC (thinks it's already solved / wrong).**
- *Core position*: The whitespace is an artifact of *mechanism slicing*. The card carves "online LoRA from WM prediction error" away from "offline residual from force-tracking error" and calls the gap novel, but from a reviewer's altitude both are "use predicted-vs-sensed force discrepancy to fix contact behavior at deploy time," and that is solved by FAWAM **on a real robot, this month**.
- *Strongest supporting evidence*: AdaWorldPolicy (the anchor) *already* reports 4 Hz real online adaptation with the force predictor and OOD recovery — the card concedes this in row 1 ("prediction-error-supervised proof — whether it matches a true-reward loop is open"). FAWAM (85.0% real, perturbation-robust) and TacForeSight (86.7% perturbation) close the "force foresight recovers contact OOD" claim. DREAM-Chunk ([[2606.18589]]) recovers **10%→65%** can-insertion with *no weight update at all*. EWAM ([[2606.12690]]) already instantiates the experience-filter/gate. The card's own table is a graveyard of papers that each own a piece.
- *The one thing only this perspective says*: The single falsifiable thing left — that **online weight updates beat frozen-policy chunk-selection / residual-correction on contact OOD** — is the experiment the card *never proposes*. H1–H5 all compare prediction-error supervision to weaker baselines; none pits *online-LoRA* against *frozen-policy DREAM-Chunk/FAWAM*. That missing baseline is exactly where the bet most likely dies.

**4. ECONOMIST (follows money/incentives).**
- *Core position*: "Real-robot 4 Hz online adaptation" is a narrative that sells because it lets an academic lab claim deployment relevance without a fleet — a single arm + force sensor produces a publishable real-robot contact result. The incentive gradient pushes toward *yet another force-aware VLA on 4–5 contact tasks*, which is precisely the saturating sub-field (FAWAM, TacForeSight, OmniVTA, ContactWorld, TaF-VLA all dated within weeks).
- *Strongest supporting evidence*: The discover_papers sweep returned **8+ force/tactile-WM contact-manipulation papers from a 6-week window** (2603–2606), several on the identical FR3/xArm + ATI/UFactory + 4-task template. The benchmark substrate the card leans on — [[2510.13626|LIBERO-Plus]], [[2510.17950|RoboChallenge]] contact split (8% SR / 27% progress) — is itself new and unsaturated, which is good, but the *method* template is crowded.
- *The one thing only this perspective says*: The cheap-to-run, hard-to-differentiate part of the bet (force-aware VLA, contact tasks) is where everyone is rushing; the **expensive, defensible moat is H5's conformal calibration gate against world-model hallucination** — it requires the E3 conformal machinery the rest of the field is *not* building, so it is where a citation-durable contribution lives. Spend the budget there, not on a sixth contact-task demo.

**5. HISTORIAN (has seen prior waves).**
- *Core position*: This is the **online system-identification / adaptive-control loop reborn in latent-VLA clothing** — "use predicted-vs-observed discrepancy to update the controller online" is MRAC/RLS-with-forgetting from the 1980s, then Dreamer-lineage residual triggers ([[2603.04029]] uses a [[2301.04104|DreamerV3]] residual), now LoRA-on-a-DiT. Each wave rediscovered that the *gating/stability* problem, not the *signal*, is the real contribution.
- *Strongest supporting evidence*: The card's own lineage table is the history compressed: 2021 self-supervised deploy-adapt ([[2007.04309]]) → DreamerV3 residual trigger ([[2603.04029]]) → sim image-foresight TTT ([[2605.08215|T3VF]]) → real unified WM+force LoRA ([[2602.20057|AdaWorldPolicy]]). [[2505.15589|Reflexive World Models]] is the explicit control-theoretic sibling (corrects *actions* via squared-error vs latent reference, 0.60 vs 0.36 reward under perturbation) — i.e. the adaptive-control reading is already in the field.
- *The one thing only this perspective says*: Every prior wave of online-adaptation-from-prediction-error eventually lost to *frozen robust policies + good gating* because online updates are unstable near the regimes you most need them (contact). The historically-validated bet is **not "make the update smarter" but "make the gate provably safe"** — H5 + the E1 safe-update handoff are the parts that survive the cycle; H3 is the part the cycle eats.

#### Prompt 2 — Contradiction Map

1. **Direct clashes.**
   - *Practitioner ⟂ the card's H3 framing*: card says the force term's value is realized via **online LoRA** at 4 Hz; Practitioner (backed by FAWAM 85.0%, TacForeSight 86.7%, both *no online weight update*) says the force signal's value is best realized via a **gated residual on a frozen policy**, and online LoRA is a net liability. Specific claim in conflict: *"online weight-update is the right actuator for the force-error signal."*
   - *Skeptic ⟂ Academic on where novelty sits*: Skeptic says even H5+H3-conjunction is "discrepancy→deploy-time fix," already FAWAM-shaped; Academic says H5 (conformal-gated online weight update from force prediction error) is genuinely uncited and defensible. Specific claim in conflict: *"is calibration-gated online weight update meaningfully distinct from a learned gated residual?"*
   - *Economist ⟂ card's benchmark choice*: card invests H3 on RoboChallenge contact split + LIBERO-Plus; Economist says the *method* is in a saturating template and only H5's gate is moat. Conflict: *where to spend the experiment budget — a sixth contact demo vs the gate.*

2. **Strongest vs weakest.**
   - *Strongest*: **Skeptic**, because its core claim is grounded in the card's *own* anchor (AdaWorldPolicy already does the 4 Hz real loop) plus two externally-discovered real-robot papers (FAWAM, TacForeSight) that independently demonstrate the H3 *effect*. It names the one missing experiment (online-LoRA vs frozen DREAM-Chunk/FAWAM) the card avoids.
   - *Weakest*: **Historian**, not because it's wrong but because its contribution is interpretive — it correctly reframes the bet as adaptive-control redux but offers no new falsifiable test the card doesn't already gesture at (H5/E1). Useful as a prior, not as a deciding datum.

3. **The one question that resolves the biggest contradiction (Practitioner ⟂ card on the actuator):**
   **"On the same real contact-OOD task, does force-prediction-error-driven *online LoRA* recover more success (and stay safer) than a *frozen* policy corrected by a FAWAM-style gated residual or DREAM-Chunk-style chunk selection fed the *same* force signal?"** If the frozen-policy variants match online LoRA, the entire "real-robot unified backbone + online weight update" half of the bet collapses to "force foresight is a good input," which is already published.

4. **What every perspective agrees on (likely true).**
   *Force/wrench prediction error carries contact-OOD-recovery information that an image-foresight-only signal is blind to.* Practitioner, Academic, Skeptic, Economist, Historian all concede this — it is the one robustly-supported claim (AdaWorldPolicy ablation, FAWAM +17.5%, TacForeSight wrist-wrench-best). The corollary they also share: **this very agreement is why H3 is unsurprising and cannot be the headline.**

5. **The blind spot (no perspective fully addressed).**
   **Force-sensor / wrench-prediction transfer across embodiments and sensors.** Every cited and discovered result is single-arm, single-F/T-sensor (FR3+ATI, xArm7+UFactory, the AdaWorldPolicy rig). Whether a *force*-prediction-error head — and its calibration gate — survives a change of gripper, force-sensor mounting, or payload (the real sim-to-real *force* gap) is untouched by the card and by all five lenses. A force-error signal that is itself uncalibrated across hardware would poison the gradient in exactly the contact regime H3 targets.

#### Prompt 3 — Synthesis Briefing

**(1) CEO summary.** D3's bet is *mechanism-pre-empted but wedge-intact*: the unified WM+force+action loop with online LoRA from world-model prediction error at 4 Hz on real robots is not a future contribution — it is [[2602.20057|AdaWorldPolicy]], the card's own anchor, already published with 0.96 LIBERO-10 and OOD recovery. The only unclaimed territory is a *conjunction*: force-prediction-**error**-driven **online weight updates**, isolated against image-foresight-only (H3) **and** conformal-calibration-gated against world-model hallucination (H5). That conjunction is genuinely uncited, but it is squeezed from both sides — H3's *effect* (force foresight recovers contact OOD that vision misses) is now independently shown on real robots by FAWAM and TacForeSight using *frozen-policy* correction, so the bet's distinctiveness rests almost entirely on proving that *online weight-update* beats *frozen-policy correction* given the same force signal, an experiment the card never proposes. Recommendation: **DE-RISK, do not commit as written** — re-center the paper on H5's calibrated gate and run the missing online-vs-frozen baseline before writing.

**(2) Five key findings, ranked by reliability.**
1. *Force/wrench prediction error recovers contact OOD that image-foresight misses.* **Most reliable.** Supported by all 5 perspectives + AdaWorldPolicy ablation, FAWAM (+17.5% from joint action-force prediction; 85.0% vs 48.75%), TacForeSight (86.7% perturbation, wrist-wrench best). Challenged by none — which is exactly why it cannot be the headline (Academic, Skeptic).
2. *The mechanism (online prediction-error supervision, reward-free) is consensus, not novel.* **Highly reliable.** Card concedes it; [[2007.04309]] (2021), [[2605.08215|T3VF]], [[2602.20057|AdaWorldPolicy]]. Supported by Academic, Skeptic, Historian. Uncontested.
3. *The H3 effect is already demonstrated on real robots via frozen-policy correction (FAWAM, TacForeSight), so winning H3 is the predicted, low-surprise outcome.* **Reliable.** Supported by Skeptic, Academic, Practitioner; grounded in get_paper_content reads of 2606.08555 and 2606.11184. Mild challenge: those use residual/foresight, not online weight updates, so the *exact* wedge survives (Academic concedes this narrowness).
4. *The defensible novelty is the conjunction force-error → online weight update + conformal hallucination gate (H5), not H3.* **Moderately reliable.** Supported by Academic, Economist, Historian (all independently route value to the gate). Challenged by Skeptic (is a calibrated gate meaningfully distinct from FAWAM's learned τ-gate / EWAM's experience filter?). Resolution requires the H5 ablation actually distinguishing conformal calibration from a heuristic gate.
5. *Online weight updates on a real contact loop are an operational liability that may underperform frozen-policy correction.* **Plausible, untested.** Supported by Practitioner, Historian; the entire field's best contact-OOD numbers come from frozen policies (FAWAM, TacForeSight, DREAM-Chunk). Challenged by the card's premise (AdaWorldPolicy's 4 Hz LoRA works) — but AdaWorldPolicy never benchmarks LoRA *against* a frozen-residual variant, so the comparison is open.

**(3) Hidden cross-perspective connection.** Practitioner ("online LoRA is the liability"), Economist ("the gate is the moat"), and Historian ("prior waves lost to gating, not signal") independently converge on the same structural claim from money, hardware, and history: **the contribution is not the adaptation signal but the safety/stability of the update**. Three unrelated lenses pointing at H5+E1 rather than H3 is the strongest meta-signal in the review — it says the card has its emphasis inverted (H3 marked "front line," H5 marked a risk-mitigation).

**(4) Actionable insight for the researcher (DE-RISK).** Do **not** commit to the bet as framed (H3 front-line). Re-center on the gated-online-update conjunction and run **one decisive experiment first**: on a single real contact-OOD task, hold the *force-prediction-error signal fixed* and compare three actuators — (a) AdaWorldPolicy-style **online LoRA**, (b) FAWAM-style **gated residual on a frozen policy**, (c) DREAM-Chunk-style **chunk selection, no update** — each with and without a **conformal calibration gate** on the world model's prediction error. Two outcomes both de-risk: if online LoRA wins, you have justified the surviving half of the bet against the strongest real baselines; if it ties, you pivot the paper to "the calibrated *gate* is what matters, independent of actuator," which is the moat all three of Economist/Historian/Practitioner point to and which no cited or discovered paper occupies. Cite FAWAM (2606.08555) and TacForeSight (2606.11184) explicitly as the frozen-policy contact-OOD baselines — leaving them out invites a desk-reject on novelty.

**(5) Frontier question that would change everything.** *Does the world-model prediction-error gradient ever point in a genuinely different, task-improving direction than a (computable) task reward — or is "consistency vs reward optimize different things" false, making the whole reward-free premise a convenience rather than a necessity?* H1 names this but treats it as a sub-test; it is actually the load-bearing assumption of the entire D-cluster. If prediction-error supervision merely approximates the reward loop (no objective gap), then on any platform where a sparse reward *is* computable the reward loop dominates, and the reward-free story shrinks to "useful only where reward is truly unavailable" — a much narrower frontier than the card claims.

---

#### Prompt 4 — Peer Review (independent)

*Reviewer stance: skeptical Stanford robotics faculty. Grounded against the D3 card (Sim2Real.md L959–1033), the sibling analyst part (016-D3-1-analyst.md), the TacForeSight KH note (2606.11184), and an independent `get_paper_content` read of FAWAM (2606.08555, which is **not** in the vault). The brief is well-built; my job is the over-reach, and there are three specific ones.*

---

##### 1. Confidence scores (1–10 reliability, per finding)

1. **Force/wrench prediction error recovers contact OOD that image-foresight misses — 8/10.**
   Verified independently: FAWAM's +17.5% from joint action-force *prediction* (56.25%→73.75%) and 85.00% vs 48.75% vision-only are real (confirmed against the paper), AdaWorldPolicy's force-predictor ablation is in-card, and TacForeSight's wrist-wrench-best (MSE 0.017) is in its KH note. Docked two points because the briefing conflates two non-identical claims under one finding: "force *conditioning* helps contact tasks" (what TacForeSight shows) vs "force-prediction-*error* recovers contact OOD that image-*foresight* misses" (the actual H3 claim). Only FAWAM speaks to the second, and even it tests vs vision-only *policies*, not vs a matched image-foresight head. Strong, but slightly over-stated by borrowing TacForeSight's authority for a claim TacForeSight doesn't make.

2. **The mechanism (reward-free online prediction-error supervision) is consensus, not novel — 9/10.**
   The card concedes it; the lineage (2007.04309 2021 → T3VF 2605.08215 → AdaWorldPolicy 2602.20057) is in-card and uncontested across all five lenses. The most reliable finding. Minor reservation: "consensus" is asserted from three papers plus a survey, not a true field census, so 9 not 10.

3. **The H3 effect is already shown on real robots via frozen-policy correction, so winning H3 is low-surprise — 6/10.**
   Half-verified. FAWAM genuinely is a residual corrector on a *frozen* base policy (10 Hz, τ=0.8 binary gate, 20 human-intervention episodes) — the "frozen-policy correction" label is exactly right *for FAWAM*. But TacForeSight is **not** a deploy-time corrector at all: it is a force-conditioned *anticipatory imitation policy* that forecasts tactile latents as action priors. Calling it "frozen-policy correction" alongside FAWAM is a category error the briefing inherited from the analyst and never flagged. So the "real robots, this month" claim rests on **one** paper (FAWAM), not two. The finding survives, but its evidentiary base is half as wide as advertised.

4. **The defensible novelty is the conjunction force-error → online weight update + conformal gate (H5), not H3 — 6/10.**
   The conjunction is plausibly uncited (I could not find a paper occupying it, and the card's own table has each piece owned separately: AdaWorldPolicy = LoRA, T3VF = update-filter, EWAM = experience-filter, DREAM-Chunk = chunk-select). But the Skeptic's challenge is not resolved: is a *conformal* gate provably distinct from FAWAM's learned τ-gate or EWAM's experience filter, or is "conformal" decoration on the same idea? The briefing routes value to the gate without demonstrating the gate is a *different object*. Until the H5 ablation distinguishes conformal calibration from a heuristic threshold, this is a hypothesis about where novelty lives, not a finding.

5. **Online weight updates on a real contact loop are an operational liability that may underperform frozen correction — 4/10.**
   The weakest link. It is inferred from a *selection artifact*: the 6-week window's best contact-OOD numbers happen to come from frozen policies (FAWAM, TacForeSight, DREAM-Chunk). That is "no one published an online-update winner recently," not "online updates lose." The briefing itself concedes AdaWorldPolicy's 4 Hz LoRA works and that the head-to-head was never run. "Plausible, untested" is the honest label the briefing uses — but it then leans on this untested claim to justify inverting the card's entire emphasis (the "hidden connection" and the recommendation both ride on it). Load-bearing weight placed on a 4/10 claim.

**Mean = 6.6. Min = 4.**

---

##### 2. Weakest link

Finding 5 (online updates underperform frozen correction). The whole DE-RISK recommendation pivots on it, yet it is supported only by the *absence* of recent online-update winners — a publication-timing artifact, not a controlled comparison. **What would verify it:** the exact experiment the briefing itself proposes — same real contact-OOD task, force-prediction-error signal held fixed, AdaWorldPolicy-style online LoRA vs FAWAM-style gated residual on a frozen policy vs DREAM-Chunk-style chunk selection — reporting both recovered SR *and* a safety metric (force-spike magnitude / constraint violations during the adaptation window). Without that, "online LoRA is the liability" is a Practitioner prior, not a result. The briefing should label its own recommendation as *predicated on an unrun experiment* rather than as an established weakness of the card.

---

##### 3. Bias check

**Skeptic-dominant, with a Practitioner tilt in the recommendation.** The synthesis presents four lenses (Practitioner, Economist, Historian, Academic-partial) "independently converging" on route-to-the-gate as its strongest meta-signal. But three of those four share a *single prior* — "gating/stability beats signal" — so they are not four independent measurements; they are one belief wearing three hats (money, hardware, history). The genuinely independent structural contribution comes from the Academic alone (the conjunction is uncited). The Practitioner's "online LoRA on a billion-param DiT is terrifying" framing then drives the headline recommendation despite Finding 5 being the least-verified. The Economist's "saturating template" claim is also asserted from the same `discover_papers` sweep that produced the threats, so it double-counts. A balanced synthesis would foreground that the de-risk case is *one prior plus one genuine novelty-gap*, not a four-way convergence.

---

##### 4. Missing perspective

**The Metrologist (measurement-validity / sensor-calibration auditor).** Every finding assumes the *force-prediction-error signal is itself a calibrated quantity*. It is not addressed whether wrench prediction error is comparable across gripper, F/T-sensor mounting, payload, and tool — the real sim-to-real *force* gap. The analyst names this as the blind spot (every result is single-arm, single-F/T-sensor: FR3+ATI, xArm7+UFactory) but the briefing demotes it to a one-line risk. This 6th lens *changes the conclusion*: if the wrench-prediction error is not calibrated across hardware, then it is ill-posed for **both** H3 (the correction direction is in uncalibrated units) **and** H5 (you cannot conformally calibrate a gate on a signal whose distribution shifts with the rig). That undercuts the briefing's central move — "retreat from H3 to the H5 gate" — because the gate inherits the same uncalibrated-force problem it was supposed to escape. The decisive de-risk question is therefore not only "online vs frozen actuator" but "does the force-prediction-error signal survive a sensor/payload swap at all" — a cheaper, more fundamental experiment the briefing never proposes.

---

##### 5. Overall grade + top fixes

**Grade: B+.**

This is a strong, well-grounded review: the central diagnosis (mechanism pre-empted by the card's own anchor; surviving wedge is narrow; cite FAWAM or invite desk-reject) is correct and verifiable, and the de-risk experiment is the right one to run. It earns the plus for grounding the headline numbers in actual paper content rather than memory, and for correctly catching that FAWAM is uncited (indeed absent from the vault) and TacForeSight is in KH but uncited in the card. It is held below A- by three specific over-reaches:

- **Top fix:** Stop treating TacForeSight as a second "frozen-policy correction" witness for H3. It is an anticipatory force-conditioned IL policy, not a deploy-time corrector — it supports "force conditioning helps contact" but not "force-prediction-*error* beats image-*foresight* under online adaptation." Findings 1 and 3 should be re-scoped so FAWAM carries the H3-effect claim alone (still sufficient for the recommendation, but honestly narrower).
- **Second:** Label Finding 5 and the resulting emphasis-inversion as *predicated on an unrun experiment / publication-timing artifact*, not as an established liability of online updates. The recommendation is sound; its stated justification over-claims.
- **Third:** Add the calibration/metrology test (does wrench-prediction error survive a sensor or payload swap) as a peer to the online-vs-frozen test, since it conditions whether *either* H3 or the H5 gate is well-posed. Also resolve, in H5, whether "conformal" gate is provably distinct from FAWAM's learned τ-gate / EWAM's experience filter — otherwise the moat the briefing routes all value to may be the same object the field already built.

*Net: the recommendation (DE-RISK, re-center on the gate, run the matched-signal baseline, cite FAWAM/TacForeSight) stands. The confidence the briefing attaches to the supporting findings should come down a notch, and the gate it retreats to needs its own novelty- and calibration-validity check before it can carry the paper.*

---

### E (Cluster review)

Topic: the coherence + central shared-bottleneck thesis of Cluster E — "Risk-Bounded Sim-to-Real Deployment: Safety Under the Irreducible Gap" — whose framing is that *C measures the residual; an un-handled residual is a safety failure, not just a performance one*, partitioned into three "distinct safety surfaces over Cluster D's online updates": E1 bounds the **update** (safe continual adaptation), E2 bounds the **action** (reachability shield), E3 **flags** the failure neither can rule out (conformal detection), "each working without failure labels or a hand-built dense reward." This review asks whether that grouping carves at the right joint and whether the shared bet is sound — not whether each direction is individually novel.

#### Prompt 1 — Five Perspectives

**1. PRACTITIONER (builds/deploys policies daily).**
Core position: The three-surface partition is operationally real — on hardware you genuinely have three different intervention points (don't update unsafely, don't *act* unsafely, notice when you've failed), and the cluster's insistence that these "compose over Cluster D's online updates" is the right deployment question because in practice you stack all three. But the cluster under-prices the integration tax: every surface adds latency, a calibration set, and a failure mode of its own, and nobody ships all three at once.
Strongest supporting evidence: RAIL runs at 0.42 s/plan on a real Franka (2409.19190) and PACS at 0.20 ms safety-step (2511.06385) — execution shields are deployable. SCDA's 20%→60% real grasp at zero violations (2503.10949) and FOSP's 40-gradient-step real-Franka safe adaptation (2407.04942) show update-bounding works on hardware. ATACOM's 12%→71% on a real KUKA over 3000 episodes (2404.09080) is the existence proof that throughput-safe exploration ships.
The one thing only this perspective says: the cluster's "compose" bet is tested almost entirely against *single-surface* baselines, but the real deployment question is the *budget* — a conformal calibration set of successes (E3), a learned cost model (E1), and an occupancy/geometry model (E2) all need maintaining under the *same* drifting deployment distribution, and if you recalibrate one you may invalidate another. No card row measures the joint maintenance cost.

**2. ACADEMIC (knows the peer-reviewed literature).**
Core position: The cluster's "settled vs open" line is mostly honest and well-cited — success-only conformal detection (FIPER 2510.09459, Sentinel CoRL'24 2410.04640) and hard-vs-soft execution shields (PACS +68% over CBF, 2511.06385) genuinely are settled, and the cluster correctly retreats to *composition* as the front line. But the literature already contains the formal machinery for E3's hardest sub-claim, and the cluster cites it as a topThreat (Robust Conformal CBF/CLF 2606.15366), which means E3's H3 is closer to "characterize-the-known" than "open whitespace."
Strongest supporting evidence: 2606.15366 proves coverage/safety survive *through* iterative policy updates via Adversarially-Robust CP and a closed-loop-sensitivity distribution-shift budget (100% empirical coverage on inverted pendulum, 99.8% score-coverage/100% safety on a maze) — exactly the bound-preservation-through-the-loop E3-H3 needs. The newly-surfaced ACoFi (2604.18482, Apr 2026) applies adaptive conformal inference *at deployment time* to a learned HJ filter with an asymptotic miscoverage bound, but explicitly only a *soft* guarantee and on a *fixed* DreamerV3/PID nominal policy in sim.
The one thing only this perspective says: the cluster's three surfaces are not actually independent in the literature — they're converging on one object, the *conformal-calibrated reachability/CMDP layer*. 2606.15366 (E3) and ACoFi (E2/E3) and SPiDR's CMDP (E1) all use the same conformal/robust-CP toolkit, so the "three distinct surfaces" framing overstates their separateness at the method level even if it holds at the *what-you-bound* level.

**3. SKEPTIC (thinks the bet is wrong or already solved).**
Core position: The cluster's load-bearing novelty is the single word "composition" — wrap an arbitrary Cluster-D engine (E1), shield a *changing* policy (E2), preserve the FPR *through* the act (E3) — and at least two of those three are either already done in adjacent form or are control-theoretic facts dressed as detection results. The claimed whitespace is thin and the cluster admits it (every direction's thesis concedes the headline is "won" or "settled").
Strongest supporting evidence the card half-concedes: E3-H3's own falsifier says it — *if 2606.15366's distribution-shift budget already closes the loop without E3's success-only detector, the preservation is a control-theoretic fact, not a detection one.* It does. For E2, ACoFi (2604.18482) already adapts a conformal switching threshold under OOD dynamics shift; the only thing it does NOT do is shield a *changing* policy — but neither does any cited E2 row, so the entire E2 novelty rests on one untested clause (H1: adapting-policy). For E1, SLowRL (2603.17092, safe LoRA RL sim-to-real on locomotion) and 2605.18842 (safe continual RL with adaptive safety constraints) already do on-hardware safe online adaptation; "modular wrapper over an arbitrary engine" is an engineering claim, not a scientific one.
The one thing only this perspective says: the cluster's three "open" sub-bets (E1 wrap-arbitrary-engine, E2 shield-changing-policy, E3 preserve-FPR-through-act) all reduce to the *same* underlying hazard — the policy's own action shifts the distribution the safety guarantee was calibrated on. That is *one* problem (closed-loop distribution shift under self-induced policy change), not three. 2606.15366 already names and solves it formally. The cluster has split one bet into three cards.

**4. ECONOMIST (follows money/incentives).**
Core position: "Safety under the irreducible gap" is the narrative that converts a saturated benchmark race (LIBERO/RoboTwin SR-chasing) into a fundable, un-saturated axis — zero-violation/0%-collision are binary headline metrics that always have a publishable delta. The incentive is to *frame* three safety surfaces as composable because composition yields N×3 ablation cells and a survey-shaped contribution, regardless of whether the deployment community needs three layers.
Strongest supporting evidence: the cluster is benchmark-rich precisely where benchmarks newly exist — Safety-Gymnasium (2310.12567), RoboEval Safety/Stability, VLA-Arena CBDDL, RoboFAC closed-loop SR 47.5%→61.25% (2505.12224) — each a fresh leaderboard with safety-cost endpoints. The 2606.15366 / ACoFi / SLowRL cluster all landing in 2026 shows a fast-moving, well-funded subfield (ETH/UPenn, WUSTL, CMU/Berkeley/NVIDIA) where being scooped on a single sub-claim is the dominant risk.
The one thing only this perspective says: the binary-metric framing (zero violations / 0% collisions) is a citation magnet but a deployment trap — industry cares about the *SR cost* of safety and the *maintenance cost* of three calibration sets, which the academic incentive structure systematically under-reports. Whoever owns the *safety-cost-vs-SR frontier* (the H2 rows) rather than the binary-zero headline captures the durable, less-scoopable contribution.

**5. HISTORIAN (has seen prior waves).**
Core position: This is the safe-RL/shielding wave (Hamilton-Jacobi reachability → CBF-QP → learned latent filters → conformal-calibrated filters) meeting the sim-to-real adaptation wave (DR → RMA → online TTA) at exactly the seam where every prior safety wave stalled: the guarantee holds for a *fixed* plant/policy and quietly breaks the moment the thing it guards starts moving. The cluster's three "open" clauses are the field re-encountering the same wall.
Strongest supporting evidence: the lineage is explicit in the cards — RAIL (2409.19190) and refineCBF (2602.23478) share the Herbert HJ-reachability root; ACoFi (2604.18482) cites that same Bansal/Herbert/Bajcsy lineage and the latent-filter chain (2505.00779, 2509.14758, 2511.07899). Recovery RL (2010.15920, pre-deep-WM recovery zones) and Sim-to-Lab-to-Real (2201.08355, PAC-Bayes staged transfer) are the prior-wave instances the cluster correctly dates as "diagnosis, not composition."
The one thing only this perspective says: every prior shielding wave eventually collapsed the "detector + shield + adaptation" trichotomy into a *single* learned safety value function that does all three (the latent-HJ-filter trajectory is already doing this — 2509.19555, 2604.18482, 2606.15366). The cluster's three-surface partition is historically a *transient* — useful now for carving the problem, but the field's gravity is toward unification, so betting on "composition of three surfaces" risks being on the wrong side of the consolidation the historian has seen before (pixel→latent world models, modular→end-to-end).

#### Prompt 2 — Contradiction Map

**(1) Where perspectives directly clash.**
- *Is the three-surface partition the right joint?* The PRACTITIONER and (operationally) the ACADEMIC say yes at the *what-you-bound* level (three real intervention points), while the SKEPTIC and HISTORIAN say no at the *mechanism* level — all three reduce to one hazard (self-induced closed-loop distribution shift) and the field is converging on a single conformal-calibrated safety value function (2606.15366, 2604.18482). Specific clash: ACADEMIC "three distinct surfaces over Cluster D" vs SKEPTIC "one bet split into three cards."
- *Is the whitespace real?* ECONOMIST/ACADEMIC say the *composition* gap is genuine and un-saturated; SKEPTIC says E3-H3 is already a control-theoretic fact (2606.15366) and E2-H1 is the only untouched clause, so two-thirds of the claimed opening is pre-empted or trivial.

**(2) Strongest vs weakest perspective.**
Strongest: the SKEPTIC — it is the most evidence-bound, lands on the card's own conceded falsifiers (E3-H3's "control-theoretic fact" escape hatch, E2's reliance on a single untested adapting-policy clause), and is corroborated by a real paper surfaced outside the card (ACoFi 2604.18482). Weakest: the ECONOMIST — directionally right about incentives but its claims (binary-metric trap, maintenance cost) are not falsifiable from the card and don't change the technical verdict; it adds color, not a decision.

**(3) The one question that resolves the biggest contradiction.**
Does shielding/adapting a *changing* (online-adapting) policy pose a hazard that is *not already covered* by 2606.15366's closed-loop-sensitivity distribution-shift budget? If the policy's online update is just another source of the same closed-loop trajectory-distribution shift that 2606.15366 already bounds, the three-surface composition collapses into one solved problem and the cluster is pre-empted. If online *policy-parameter* change (not just action-induced state change) breaks the sensitivity bound's assumptions, E2-H1/E1-H1 are real, distinct whitespace.

**(4) What every perspective agrees on (likely true).**
The single durable, un-saturated contribution is the **safety-cost-vs-SR frontier** (the H2 rows across E1/E2 + RoboEval/VLA-Arena), NOT any binary zero-violation/0%-collision headline — the headlines are settled/won (PRACTITIONER deployability, ACADEMIC "settled," SKEPTIC "won," ECONOMIST "citation trap," HISTORIAN "prior-wave result"). Bounding the *cost* of safety, not asserting its existence, is where all five converge.

**(5) The blind spot (topic none addressed).**
None of the five addressed *who validates the safety guarantee itself under the gap* — the calibration set and the cost/occupancy model are all estimated from the **same** sim-or-early-deploy distribution that the irreducible gap corrupts. The cluster bounds the residual but never bounds the *error in its own safety model* induced by the very gap it exists to handle (the meta-gap: a wrong occupancy model or mis-calibrated success set silently voids every guarantee). Cluster C measures the residual but no E direction measures the residual *in the safety layer's own inputs*.

#### Prompt 3 — Synthesis Briefing

**(1) CEO summary.**
Cluster E carves sim-to-real safety at a defensible joint — three things you can bound on hardware (the update, the action, the failure-flag) — and its "settled vs open" honesty is unusually disciplined: it concedes the headlines (success-only conformal detection, hard-vs-soft shielding, zero-violation continual adaptation) are won and retreats to *composition* as the front line. The problem is that the composition bet, stress-tested, is narrower than three cards imply: E3's bound-through-the-loop is a control-theoretic result a cited topThreat (2606.15366) already proves, a newly-surfaced paper (ACoFi 2604.18482) covers much of E2/E3's conformal-filter-under-shift ground for a *fixed* policy, and the genuinely-open core of all three directions collapses to one hazard — a policy that changes its own action distribution and thereby the distribution its safety guarantee was calibrated on. The cluster is a COMMIT candidate, but the bet should be re-scoped to that single hazard and to the safety-cost-vs-SR frontier, not pitched as three independent composable surfaces.

**(2) Five key findings, ranked by reliability.**
1. *The settled headlines are genuinely settled* — success-only conformal detection (FIPER 2510.09459, Sentinel 2410.04640) and hard-vs-soft execution shields (PACS +68% over CBF, 2511.06385; RAIL 0% vs 5–35%, 2409.19190). Supported by ACADEMIC, SKEPTIC, PRACTITIONER; challenged by none. (Highest reliability — card and alphaxiv agree.)
2. *The real open core of E1/E2/E3 is ONE hazard, not three: self-induced closed-loop distribution shift.* Supported by SKEPTIC, HISTORIAN, ACADEMIC; challenged by PRACTITIONER (insists the three intervention points stay operationally distinct even if the hazard is shared). (High — grounded in 2606.15366 + the cards' own falsifiers.)
3. *E3-H3 (preserve FPR through the act) is largely pre-empted by 2606.15366* — the card itself flags this as the falsifier ("a control-theoretic fact, not a detection one"). Supported by SKEPTIC, ACADEMIC; challenged by no one with counter-evidence. (High.)
4. *E2-H1 (shield a CHANGING policy) is the cluster's one genuinely-untouched clause* — ACoFi (2604.18482) and every cited E2 row shield a *fixed* policy. Supported by SKEPTIC (as the *only* surviving novelty), HISTORIAN (as the seam every wave stalls at); challenged by PRACTITIONER's latency-budget skepticism. (Medium-high — absence-of-prior-art, harder to prove.)
5. *The durable contribution is the safety-cost-vs-SR frontier, not the binary headlines.* Supported by all five (unanimous in Prompt-2 Q4); challenged by none, but it is the finding most vulnerable to the ECONOMIST's point that incentives push *away* from it. (Medium — normative, not empirical.)

**(3) Hidden cross-perspective connection.**
The HISTORIAN's "the field collapses the trichotomy into one learned safety value function" and the SKEPTIC's "three cards are one hazard" and the ACADEMIC's "the three surfaces share the conformal/robust-CP toolkit" are the *same observation* from three angles: detection, shielding, and safe adaptation are converging onto a single conformal-calibrated reachability/CMDP object (2606.15366, 2604.18482, 2509.19555). The cluster's three-surface partition is a *snapshot of a converging field*, which means its coherence is real *today* but its grouping logic has a half-life.

**(4) Actionable insight for the researcher: COMMIT, but DE-RISK by re-scoping to E2-H1.**
Commit to the cluster as a publishable bet, but drop the "three independent composable surfaces" pitch and concentrate the novelty on the *one* clause no prior art touches and that the convergence-toward-unification does NOT yet cover: **does a reachability/conformal safety guarantee survive a policy that is changing its own parameters online (not just its state) during deployment?** Concrete next experiment: take RAIL's reachability shield (2409.19190) or ACoFi's adaptive-conformal filter (2604.18482) and run it over an AdaWorldPolicy/Learning-on-the-Fly online-adapting policy (2602.20057/2508.21065) on a real arm, with 2606.15366's closed-loop-sensitivity budget as the *baseline that assumes only action-induced shift* — the falsifiable claim is that *parameter-update-induced* shift breaks 2606.15366's sensitivity bound where action-induced shift does not. Report the collision/violation rate AND the SR cost (the frontier from Q4) on RoboEval's Safety/Stability metrics. If the bound holds, the cluster is pre-empted and should be DROPPED to a single characterization paper; if it breaks, E2-H1 is the whole contribution and E1/E3 become its supporting ablations.

**(5) Frontier question that would change everything.**
Can the safety layer bound the error in *its own* inputs (occupancy model, success-calibration set, cost function) under the irreducible gap — i.e., a guarantee that degrades *gracefully and measurably* as the safety model itself goes OOD, rather than silently voiding? Every E direction assumes its safety model is correct; the gap that breaks the policy also corrupts the model that is supposed to guard it. Whoever bounds the meta-gap owns the next wave.
