#### Prompt 4 — Peer Review (independent)

*Reviewer stance: skeptical Stanford robotics professor. Grounded in Sim2Real.md lines 1034-1274 (Cluster E: E1 zero-violation continual adaptation, E2 reachability-filtered execution, E3 conformal runtime detection), the upstream five-perspective analyst part (017-E-1-analyst.md), the in-vault KH note for 2606.15366, and web verification of 2606.15366 + ACoFi 2604.18482.*

The briefing is strong, honest, and unusually well-disciplined about conceding settled ground. But its central actionable insight rests on one factual claim about a paper that, when checked against that paper's own abstract, does not hold. That single crack propagates into the COMMIT/DROP recommendation. Details below.

---

#### 1. Confidence scores (1-10, reliability)

**Finding 1 — the settled headlines are genuinely settled (success-only conformal detection; hard-vs-soft shields). Score: 9/10.**
Best-supported claim in the briefing. FIPER (2510.09459), Sentinel (2410.04640), PACS/Path-Consistent (2511.06385 +68% over CBF), RAIL (2409.19190 0% vs 5-35%) are all in-KH, on-axis, and the cards themselves label H1/H4 confirmatory. Three perspectives support, none challenge. Docked one point only because "settled" is asserted from the cards' own framing rather than an independent saturation check, but the underlying results are real and correctly cited.

**Finding 2 — the open core of E1/E2/E3 is ONE hazard (self-induced closed-loop distribution shift), not three. Score: 7/10.**
This is the briefing's best original insight and it is genuinely illuminating: E1-H1 (wrap arbitrary engine), E2-H1 (shield changing policy), E3-H3 (preserve FPR through act) do share the same mechanism. Grounded in 2606.15366 and each card's own falsifier. Docked because it is a reframing, not a discovery the cards missed: E3-H3's falsifier already says the bound-through-the-loop "is a control-theoretic fact, not a detection one," and the PRACTITIONER's counter (three operationally distinct intervention points) is not actually rebutted, only outvoted. "One hazard at the mechanism level" and "three surfaces at the what-you-bound level" are both true; the briefing privileges the former without proving the latter is the wrong frame for a *deployment* paper.

**Finding 3 — E3-H3 is largely pre-empted by 2606.15366. Score: 8/10.**
Well-supported and the card concedes it in its own falsifier. The KH note confirms 2606.15366 proves coverage/safety survive *through* iterative policy updates via ARCP + a closed-loop-sensitivity budget. "Largely pre-empted" is fair: the success-only-detector framing of E3-H3 is not what 2606.15366 proves, but the bound-preservation half is. Docked because "largely" is doing real work, the detection-side contribution (does a *success-only conformal detector* wired to an act preserve FPR) is not literally what 2606.15366's control-theoretic theorem covers, so "pre-empted" overstates slightly.

**Finding 4 — E2-H1 (shield a CHANGING policy) is the one genuinely-untouched clause. Score: 6/10.**
This is the load-bearing finding for the entire COMMIT recommendation, and it is the most fragile. Two problems. (a) It rests on an absence-of-prior-art argument over ACoFi (2604.18482), a paper that is NOT in the KnowledgeHub, NOT cited in the Sim2Real card, and surfaced only by the upstream analyst, so it has had no independent vault verification. I verified via web that ACoFi is real (Huriot/Tabbara/Sibai, WUSTL, Apr 2026) and does shield a *fixed* DreamerV3/PID policy, which supports the clause. (b) But absence-of-prior-art is the weakest evidence class and the briefing leans its whole de-risk on it. Score held at medium because the web check corroborated the specific "ACoFi shields a fixed policy" claim, but a single corroborated negative is not a proof of whitespace.

**Finding 5 — the durable contribution is the safety-cost-vs-SR frontier, not the binary headlines. Score: 6/10.**
Unanimous across perspectives, which is itself a yellow flag (unanimity often signals an untested premise, not a verified one). It is a *normative/strategic* claim ("durable," "less-scoopable"), not a falsifiable technical one, the ECONOMIST itself was flagged upstream as the weakest perspective and its incentive argument is non-falsifiable from the card. The frontier rows (H2) are real and well-instrumented (RoboEval, VLA-Arena, Safety-Gymnasium), so the claim is defensible, but "durable contribution" is a bet on the field's future taste, not a finding.

**Mean = 7.2. Min = 6.**

---

#### 2. Weakest link

**The weakest link is the actionable insight's central falsifiable claim, which depends on Finding 4 + a mischaracterization of 2606.15366.** The briefing proposes: run a shield over an online-adapting policy "with 2606.15366's closed-loop-sensitivity budget as the baseline that assumes ONLY action-induced shift, falsifiable claim: parameter-update-induced shift breaks 2606.15366's sensitivity bound where action-induced shift does not."

This is contradicted by 2606.15366 itself. I checked both the in-vault KH note and the paper's abstract on arxiv. The KH note's Method states the distribution-shift budget is "quantified through closed-loop trajectory sensitivity analysis, which bounds the potential increase in model error due to a policy update," and the framework "iteratively updates robust conformal CLF/CBF policies." The arxiv abstract is explicit: the budget "allows us to control how much the model error can increase **across policy updates**," and the framework "iteratively updates the robust conformal CLF/CBF policy while maintaining stability/safety guarantees across episodes." In other words, 2606.15366's budget is built precisely for *policy-parameter changes between episodes*, not merely action-induced state shift under a fixed policy. The briefing has inverted the topThreat: it casts 2606.15366 as the action-only baseline that parameter-update shift will break, when 2606.15366's whole contribution is bounding parameter-update-induced shift.

**Info needed to verify / repair:** the precise scope of 2606.15366's sensitivity analysis, specifically whether its "policy update" is a discrete episodic re-synthesis (a known, bounded-magnitude jump in parameters) versus E2-H1's continuous online LoRA/test-time adaptation at 4 Hz (2602.20057) or 3-step correction (2508.21065). The genuine open question is NOT "parameter vs action shift" (2606.15366 covers both) but "does the sensitivity budget remain *computable and finite* when the policy update is online, high-frequency, and not separated into clean episodes with re-calibration windows." That is a real and defensible whitespace, but the briefing did not frame it that way, and the as-written falsifiable claim would likely fail on first contact with the paper.

---

#### 3. Bias check — which perspective over-dominated

**The SKEPTIC over-dominated, and the briefing inherits the analyst's own admission of it.** 017-E-1-analyst.md explicitly names the SKEPTIC as the strongest perspective and the ECONOMIST as the weakest. The synthesis then adopts the SKEPTIC's core move wholesale: "three cards are one hazard," "two-thirds of the opening is pre-empted or trivial," re-scope to the single surviving clause. Every one of the five findings is a SKEPTIC-compatible deflation; the PRACTITIONER's substantive counter (three operationally distinct intervention points, integration tax, joint maintenance cost of three calibration sets under one drifting distribution) appears only as a parenthetical "challenged by" and never as a finding in its own right. The HISTORIAN and ACADEMIC are absorbed as corroboration of the SKEPTIC ("the same observation from three angles"), which the briefing presents as a *strength* (hidden cross-perspective connection) but which is actually three perspectives collapsing into one, the opposite of independent triangulation. Net: the briefing is a high-quality SKEPTIC brief wearing a five-perspective costume. A genuinely balanced synthesis would have given the PRACTITIONER's "nobody ships three layers, and recalibrating one invalidates another" the same weight as the SKEPTIC's "it's all one hazard," because those two claims point at *different* papers (composition-tax vs composition-redundancy) and only one can be the headline.

---

#### 4. Missing perspective (a 6th angle that changes conclusions)

**A SAFETY-ASSURANCE / certification engineer (the "who signs off on this on real hardware" angle).** The five perspectives all argue about *novelty and incentives*; none asks the question that decides whether any of this matters in deployment: **what is the assurance argument when the safety model itself is OOD?** The briefing's own frontier question gestures at this (the meta-gap), and the analyst flagged it as the shared blind spot, but it is parked as a closing flourish rather than driven into the conclusions. An assurance reviewer would invert the whole verdict: the durable contribution is not the safety-cost-vs-SR frontier (Finding 5) and not E2-H1 (Finding 4), it is *graceful, measurable degradation of the guarantee as the occupancy model / success-calibration set / cost function goes OOD*. Every E direction assumes its safety model is correct, and the same residual gap that breaks the policy corrupts the model meant to guard it. This angle changes the COMMIT recommendation: if the meta-gap is the real frontier, then E2-H1 (shield a changing policy with a still-trusted shield) is the *less* interesting bet, and the cluster should re-scope toward "bound the error in the safety layer's own inputs," which is also far less likely to be pre-empted by the 2606.15366 / ACoFi convergence the briefing fears. The briefing saw this door and walked past it.

---

#### 5. Overall grade + top fixes

**Grade: B+.**

This is a well-organized, intellectually honest briefing that correctly identifies the settled-vs-open boundary, finds a real unifying insight (one hazard under three surfaces), and grounds most claims in in-vault, on-axis papers. It loses the A band for three reasons: (1) its single most consequential claim mischaracterizes the topThreat it cites, in a direction that happens to preserve the recommendation, (2) it presents a SKEPTIC-dominant synthesis as balanced triangulation, and (3) its strongest novelty (E2-H1) rests on a not-in-vault paper and an absence-of-prior-art argument it labels "medium-high" but treats as load-bearing.

**Top fixes, in priority order:**
1. **Fix the 2606.15366 framing before committing.** As written, the falsifiable claim ("parameter-update shift breaks its bound, action shift does not") will likely fail because 2606.15366's budget is built for policy-parameter updates. Re-pose the open question as: does the closed-loop-sensitivity budget stay *computable and finite* under continuous, high-frequency online adaptation that lacks clean episodic re-calibration windows? That is the defensible version and it survives contact with the paper.
2. **Promote ACoFi (2604.18482) into the KnowledgeHub and the E2 card** before resting E2-H1's novelty on it. A clause whose whitespace depends on a paper that is neither in the vault nor in the card cannot be the "whole contribution" with confidence. Verify directly whether ACoFi's adaptive-conformal threshold degrades gracefully or silently under a *changing* policy, that is the actual test of the clause.
3. **Give the PRACTITIONER's composition-tax a finding of its own.** "Nobody ships three layers; recalibrating one calibration set invalidates another under the same drifting distribution" is a falsifiable, deployment-relevant claim the SKEPTIC-dominant synthesis suppressed. It may be a stronger durable contribution than the safety-cost-vs-SR frontier.
4. **Elevate the meta-gap (frontier question) from flourish to thesis candidate.** It is the one direction the 2606.15366 / ACoFi unification trend does *not* cover, which is exactly the property the briefing wants from its re-scoped bet, and it is currently buried.

---

### E1 (Direction review)

Topic: the bet that SCDA's PCRPO+EWC, **wrapping a Cluster-D online engine** ([[2107.04034|RMA]]/[[2602.20057|AdaWorldPolicy]]), holds the **20%→60% gain at zero violations** *and* keeps both zero forgetting and zero violations across **N successive domains** on real hardware. Whitespace = the *composition* claim (constraint wraps an arbitrary online engine + holds across successive domains), not "is unconstrained adaptation unsafe" (already a measured result).

#### Prompt 1 — Five Perspectives

**1. PRACTITIONER (builds/deploys policies daily).**
- *Core position*: The wrapper is the right product framing, but the bet quietly assumes the online engine and the safety layer share a state/observation interface and a cost signal that exists on hardware. In practice the cost function you can *measure* at deploy-time (joint-limit, tip-over, force) is not the cost SCDA penalizes in expectation, and that mismatch, not "does it compose", is what bites.
- *Strongest evidence*: SCDA ([[2503.10949]]) hit 20%→60% at zero cost on a *single* grasp loop; the real-hardware cost there was hand-instrumented. [[2603.17092|SLowRL]] (Unitree Go2, jump/trot) shows the practitioner's actual recipe: freeze the base policy, fine-tune a rank-1 LoRA, and bolt on a *recovery policy + safety filter* — 0.0 falls on trot, 2.0 on jump, 60 min wall-clock — i.e. people already wrap an online-LoRA engine with a shield and it works without PCRPO+EWC.
- *Only-this-perspective*: H5 (cost-model misspecification) is not a side-risk, it is the main event — your zero-violation number is only as honest as your cost sensor, and a model-free reachability backstop (E2) is doing the real safety work that the learned PCRPO cost gets credit for.

**2. ACADEMIC (knows the peer-reviewed literature).**
- *Core position*: The card's lineage is accurate and current — [[2604.19737|NSCMDP]]/[[2502.15922|Safe EWC]] genuinely settle "unconstrained continual adaptation is unsafe" and expose the safety×forgetting tension, so moving the front line to *composition* is the correct read of the evidence. But the literature already contains every *component* of the proposed wrapper, so the contribution is integrative, not mechanistic.
- *Strongest evidence*: [[2506.11033|Adaptive Shielding]] already demonstrates train-time SRO ∘ execution-time conformal shield *composing* under hidden friction/mass shift (Car-Goal, +1.1s overhead); [[2601.21845|Constrained Meta-RL]] gives provable test-time safe adaptation to *novel* domains with a regret bound; [[2509.18648|SPiDR]] gives transfer-time safety on a real race car + Go1. The pieces of H1/H4/H3 are individually published; what is unpublished is their *conjunction on hardware across successive domains*.
- *Only-this-perspective*: H4 (transfer ∘ adaptation compose) is at risk of being a non-result — Adaptive Shielding already shows train∘execution composition, so unless E1 shows the windows are *non-overlapping* and additive on hardware, reviewers will say "composition was known."

**3. SKEPTIC (thinks it's wrong or already solved).**
- *Core position*: The defensible novelty has shrunk to a single cell: zero-forgetting × zero-violation across *successive* domains on *hardware*. H1 (wrap an online engine, hold the gain) is essentially already demonstrated, and H2/H4/H5 are either sim-confirmed elsewhere or definitional.
- *Strongest evidence*: [[2603.17092|SLowRL]] is the smoking gun — it wraps an online-LoRA hardware engine (and AdaWorldPolicy *is* a 4 Hz online-LoRA engine) with a safety mechanism and reports near-zero failures *while preserving the adaptation gain* on a real quadruped. That is H1, demonstrated, modulo the EWC/forgetting axis. The card even concedes NSCMDP is "the topThreat" but is MuJoCo, not a hardware wrapper.
- *Only-this-perspective*: the card's "arbitrary Cluster-D engine" is doing rhetorical work — RMA and AdaWorldPolicy are both *latent-conditioned* engines with compatible interfaces; "arbitrary" is untested and probably false (a vision-only world-model engine and a proprioceptive extrinsics engine do not share the cost interface), so the generality claim is softer than stated.

**4. ECONOMIST (follows money/incentives).**
- *Core position*: Safe-continual-RL is a benchmark-poor, hardware-expensive niche, which is exactly why a *hardware composition* paper is differentiated but also why it is slow and risky to produce. The incentive gradient pushes everyone toward sim suites (Safety-Gymnasium, NSCMDP's Damaged-HalfCheetah, highway-env), leaving the real-robot successive-domain cell genuinely empty — but empty because it is unrewarding to fill, not because it is unknown.
- *Strongest evidence*: [[2310.12567|Safety-Gymnasium]] (16-algorithm SafePO, PPO-Lag 98% cost cut), [[2604.19737|NSCMDP]] (8-algorithm MuJoCo protocol), [[2605.18842|LILAC+]] (highway-env merge-v0, 750 CPU-hours) — the entire competitive set is sim, because sim is where citations/leaderboards accrue. The one hardware datapoint that moved (SLowRL, SCDA, [[2407.04942|FOSP]] real Franka) each used *one* robot, *one* loop.
- *Only-this-perspective*: the cheapest publishable unit here is *not* the full wrapper — it is a sim NSCMDP-style benchmark that *adds the wrapper-composition protocol* (engine × constraint × N-domains), which would get cited by the sim crowd while the hardware result is still being collected; de-risk the bet by shipping the protocol first.

**5. HISTORIAN (has seen prior waves).**
- *Core position*: This is the safe-RL replay of the modular-vs-monolithic sim2real swing. RMA-era rapid adaptation, then DR-robustness, then "bake safety into the policy" (CPO/PCRPO), and now the pendulum swings back to *modular* shields wrapping a learner — the same arc as shielding (Alshiekh 2018) re-emerging as adaptive conformal shields a decade later.
- *Strongest evidence*: [[2010.15920|Recovery RL]] (decoupled task+recovery policy, 2020) → [[2409.19190|RAIL]] reachability shield → [[2506.11033|Adaptive Shielding]] conformal shield → [[2603.17092|SLowRL]] recovery-policy filter (2026): the field keeps re-deriving "separate the safety controller from the task learner." SCDA's single cost-constrained policy is the *monolithic* counter-swing; history says the modular wrapper usually wins on hardware because the recovery policy is debuggable.
- *Only-this-perspective*: betting on EWC/Fisher as the continual mechanism is betting on the *losing* side of a prior wave — EWC was already shown brittle in the original continual-learning cycle, and NSCMDP shows online-EWC *violates*; the durable contribution is the composition *protocol*, not the EWC choice.

#### Prompt 2 — Contradiction Map

1. **Direct clashes.**
   - *Skeptic vs Academic-on-H1*: Skeptic says SLowRL already demonstrates H1 (wrap an online-LoRA hardware engine, hold the gain at near-zero violations); Academic says SLowRL uses a *recovery-policy shield* (E2-flavored) on a *single task*, not PCRPO's expected-cost constraint across *successive* domains, so H1-as-stated (PCRPO+EWC over AdaWorldPolicy/RMA across N domains) is unfilled.
   - *Practitioner vs the card on cost*: Practitioner says the zero-violation claim rests on a hand-instrumented cost sensor (H5 is the real risk); the card treats the learned PCRPO cost as the safety guarantor and the E2 shield as a mere backstop.
   - *Historian vs the card on mechanism*: Historian says EWC is the losing-wave choice and NSCMDP already shows online-EWC violates; the card anchors the whole bet on SCDA's Fisher-protected EWC.

2. **Strongest vs weakest.** Strongest = **Skeptic**, because it is anchored to a concrete 2026 hardware paper (SLowRL) that directly instantiates the wrapper, forcing the novelty claim to retreat to a single defensible cell. Weakest = **Economist** on *truth* (its market story does not bear on whether the bet is correct), though it gives the best *de-risking tactic*.

3. **Resolving question.** Does the zero-violation guarantee survive *across a chain of N successive domains* on real hardware while preserving zero forgetting — i.e. run SLowRL's exact wrapper-on-Go2 setup but chain ≥3 domain shifts with an EWC/Fisher term and report per-domain violations + normalized forgetting? That single experiment separates "SLowRL already did it" (single-domain) from the surviving whitespace (successive-domain forgetting×safety frontier, H3).

4. **Universal agreement (likely true).** Every perspective agrees that (a) unconstrained continual adaptation on hardware is unsafe and this is now *measured* (NSCMDP/Safe EWC/SafeAdapt), and (b) a *modular* safety layer wrapping a separate online adaptation engine is a sound and increasingly standard architecture. The "is the diagnosis settled" half of the card is unanimously accepted.

5. **Blind spot (no perspective addressed).** None addressed **how zero violations is even certified on hardware in the rare-event regime** — "zero violations over K episodes" with small K is statistically indistinguishable from a low-but-nonzero rate, so the headline metric needs a confidence-bounded violation-*rate* estimate (a CP-style upper bound), not a raw zero count. The whole bet is stated in terms of an un-bounded "zero," which is not a guarantee.

#### Prompt 3 — Synthesis Briefing

**CEO summary.** E1's diagnosis is rock-solid and current: the field has *measured* that unconstrained continual adaptation goes unsafe (NSCMDP, Safe EWC, SafeAdapt), so the card correctly refuses to re-litigate that and moves to *composition*. But the composition claim has been partially overtaken: [[2603.17092|SLowRL]] (Mar 2026, real Go2) already wraps an online-LoRA adaptation engine with a safety mechanism and holds the adaptation gain at near-zero failures, which is H1 in all but the EWC/successive-domain detail, and [[2506.11033|Adaptive Shielding]] already shows train-time∘execution-time safety composing under dynamics shift (H4) in sim. What genuinely survives is one cell: **zero-forgetting × zero-violation across N *successive* domains on real hardware, with a confidence-bounded violation rate** — narrow, real, but expensive and benchmark-poor. This is a DE-RISK, not a clean COMMIT: the publishable unit is the successive-domain composition *protocol*, not the wrapper per se.

**5 key findings, ranked by reliability.**
1. *Unconstrained continual adaptation on hardware is unsafe is settled* (NSCMDP 2604.19737, Safe EWC 2502.15922, SafeAdapt 2604.09452). Supported by ALL; challenged by none. Highest reliability — the card's own framing.
2. *The core "wrap an online adaptation engine, hold the gain at near-zero violations on hardware" (H1) is essentially demonstrated* by SLowRL (2603.17092) for the LoRA case (and AdaWorldPolicy *is* online-LoRA). Supported by Skeptic, Historian, Practitioner; challenged by Academic (different shield type, single task). High reliability against the *broad* H1, lower against H1-as-precisely-stated.
3. *Train-time ∘ execution-time safety composition (H4) is already shown* in sim (Adaptive Shielding 2506.11033; Constrained Meta-RL 2601.21845 for novel-domain test-time safety). Supported by Academic, Historian; challenged by Practitioner (sim-only, interfaces differ on hardware). Medium-high.
4. *The surviving whitespace is the successive-domain × zero-forgetting × zero-violation cell on hardware* (H3). Supported by Skeptic, Economist, Academic; challenged by none directly, but Historian warns the EWC mechanism may be the wrong tool to fill it. Medium reliability as *whitespace*, low as *EWC will fill it*.
5. *The zero-violation metric is not certifiable as stated* — needs a confidence-bounded violation-rate, not a raw zero count. Supported by the blind-spot analysis; unaddressed by all five perspectives, so untested but high face-validity. Medium.

**Hidden cross-perspective connection.** Practitioner's H5 (your guarantee is only as good as your cost sensor) and the blind spot (zero-violations is statistically un-bounded at small K) are the *same* failure: the card's safety claim rests on two unstated measurement assumptions — that the deploy-time cost signal is faithful, and that "zero over K episodes" certifies a rate. Both Skeptic and Historian independently route around the learned cost by preferring a *model-free recovery/reachability shield* (SLowRL's filter, Recovery RL, RAIL) — i.e. the field's convergent answer to "don't trust the learned cost alone" is exactly E1's own H5 backstop, promoted from footnote to load-bearing.

**Actionable insight for the researcher (DE-RISK, do not COMMIT as written).** The H1 wrapper is too close to SLowRL to be the headline. Re-anchor the contribution on the *successive-domain* cell and the *certification* gap. Concretely: (1) **Next experiment** — reproduce SLowRL's Go2 LoRA-wrapper-on-hardware exactly, then *chain ≥3 domain shifts* (payload → terrain → actuator wear), add SCDA's EWC/Fisher term, and report per-domain {violation-rate with a CP upper bound, normalized forgetting} vs an online-EWC baseline (which NSCMDP predicts will violate). The result is the H3 frontier curve, which no cited paper owns. (2) **Cheap parallel unit (Economist's tactic)** — ship a sim NSCMDP-style benchmark that adds the *engine × constraint × N-domain* composition protocol so the contribution lands even if hardware slips. (3) **Drop the "arbitrary engine" claim** to "latent-conditioned engines (RMA, AdaWorldPolicy) with a shared cost interface" — defensible; "arbitrary" is not. Falsifier stays as the card states it: if chaining domains forces a forgetting/safety trade-off or the CP-bounded rate is non-zero, the composition does not hold and SCDA is a single-domain result.

**Frontier question.** Can a single online adaptation engine carry a *provable, confidence-bounded* violation-rate guarantee that is preserved *through* the engine's own distribution-shifting updates across an unbounded sequence of domains — i.e. does the safety certificate survive the very adaptation it is meant to make safe, or does each new domain reset the guarantee? (This is the closed-loop analogue of the S2R-E3 H3 problem: CP bounds invalidated when the deployed policy's actions shift the trajectory distribution away from calibration.)

---

#### Prompt 4 — Peer Review (independent)

*Reviewer stance: skeptical Stanford robotics professor. Target: E1 — Zero-Violation Continual Adaptation (`Mechanism/Sim2Real.md` L1038–1115). Verification performed: SLowRL `2603.17092` confirmed real via arXiv (Daneshmand et al., "Safe Low-Rank Adaptation RL for Locomotion", real Go2, rank-1 LoRA + separate safety filter, 46.5% faster fine-tune, near-zero violations); Adaptive Shielding `2506.11033` and NSCMDP `2604.19737` claims cross-checked against their KH notes; KH presence checked for all 11 load-bearing IDs.*

---

##### 1. Confidence scores (1–10, reliability)

| # | Finding | Score | One-line justification |
|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | Unconstrained continual adaptation on HW is unsafe — **settled** (NSCMDP/Safe EWC/SafeAdapt) | **9** | Three independent papers, all in KH; NSCMDP note confirms the safety×forgetting tension empirically. The card's strongest, least-contestable claim. Docked 1 only because "settled" is a sociological verdict on a 4-month-old literature, not a theorem. |
| 2 | H1 ("wrap an online engine, hold the gain at near-zero violations on HW") **essentially demonstrated** by SLowRL | **6** | SLowRL is **real and on-point** (verified: real Go2, online LoRA, separate safety filter, gain preserved). But it is a *recovery-policy/filter* shield (E2-flavored), single-task, **not** PCRPO's expected-cost continual constraint across successive domains. "Essentially demonstrated" is true for *broad* H1, an over-reach for *H1-as-stated*. The briefing flags this itself, which is why it's a 6 not a 4. |
| 3 | H4 (train-time ∘ execution-time safety) **already shown in sim** (Adaptive Shielding; Constrained Meta-RL) | **8** | Adaptive Shielding's KH note literally states proactive-training and reactive-execution safety are "complementary" under friction/mass shift — that *is* the composition result. The only gap is sim-only + interface portability, which the finding states. Well-grounded. |
| 4 | Surviving whitespace = successive-domain × zero-forgetting × zero-violation on HW (H3) | **6** | The whitespace is real (no cited paper owns the per-domain forgetting×safety curve on hardware). But it is whitespace-*by-expense*, not whitespace-by-ignorance (Economist's own point), and the "EWC will fill it" sub-claim is contradicted by the Historian and by NSCMDP showing online-EWC violates. Medium as stated; the briefing correctly splits the score. |
| 5 | Zero-violation metric not certifiable — needs CP-bounded rate, not a raw zero | **7** | Methodologically correct and important; "zero over K episodes" at small K is statistically indistinguishable from a low nonzero rate. High face-validity, but it is an analyst-introduced critique with no perspective stress-testing it and no power analysis sizing K — so it is asserted, not demonstrated. |

**Mean = 7.2, min = 6.**

---

##### 2. Weakest link

Tie at 6 between findings **2 and 4**; the more *consequential* weak link is **Finding 2**, because it single-handedly justifies the headline downgrade (COMMIT → DE-RISK).

**The specific verification gap:** the entire "H1 is already demonstrated" claim rests on **SLowRL `2603.17092`, which is NOT in KnowledgeHub and NOT cited in the E1 card** — it appears only in the upstream analyst review part. So the card under review is *unaware of its own closest competitor*, and the peer reviewer cannot confirm the load-bearing specifics from vault content. What is needed to settle it: (a) read SLowRL's body to confirm the exact fall counts (the analyst's "0.0 trot / 2.0 jump" are NOT in the public abstract, which only says "near-zero"); (b) confirm whether SLowRL's safety filter is reset-free *across distribution-shifting domains* or only within one task — if single-task only, then H1-as-stated (PCRPO+EWC over an arbitrary engine across N domains) is materially less covered than "essentially demonstrated" implies, and the downgrade is too aggressive. Until SLowRL is ingested and read in full, Finding 2 is a citation the card cannot see and the reviewer cannot audit.

---

##### 3. Bias check — dominant perspective

**Skeptic over-dominated.** The Contradiction Map explicitly crowns Skeptic "strongest" (line 39 of the analyst part) and the entire CEO summary + actionable insight is the Skeptic's "novelty has shrunk to one cell" thesis, operationalized. SLowRL — the Skeptic's smoking gun — drives findings 2, 4, the hidden connection, and experiment (1). The synthesis is *Skeptic-with-footnotes-from-the-others*: Practitioner survives only as H5, Historian only as the anti-EWC caveat, Economist only as the cheap-parallel tactic, Academic only as the "integrative not mechanistic" hedge. This is a defensible bias (skepticism is the job here) but it tilts the briefing toward *demolition*: the framing assumes the bet has been overtaken and reasons backward. A more balanced read would weight the **Academic's** counter — that SLowRL's shield type and single-task scope leave H1-as-stated genuinely open — as heavily as the Skeptic's "already done," rather than relegating it to a parenthetical "challenged by Academic."

---

##### 4. Missing perspective (a 6th that would change conclusions)

**The Theorist / Control-Theoretic safety reviewer.** Every cited "guarantee" here is *empirical* ("zero over K episodes", "near-zero violations"). None of the five perspectives asks the question a CoRL/L4DC safety reviewer asks first: **does the wrapper preserve a formal invariant?** SCDA's PCRPO bounds *expected* cost; that is not a per-episode safety guarantee, and chaining N domains with EWC says nothing about whether a control-invariant set (CBF/HJ-reachability) is maintained across the shifts. This perspective changes the conclusions two ways: (1) it reframes Finding 5 from "report a CP upper bound" to "the right object is a *certificate that survives the adaptation*, which expected-cost EWC structurally cannot provide" — sharpening the frontier question into a falsifiable formal claim; (2) it would pull `2601.21845` (Constrained Meta-RL, *provable* test-time safety with a regret bound + matching lower bound) and Sim-to-Lab-to-Real `2201.08355` (PAC-Bayes generalization certificate) from supporting-row status to the **center** of the contribution — suggesting the publishable unit may be a *certified* successive-domain bound, not just an empirical frontier curve. The briefing's own frontier question ("does the certificate survive the adaptation?") is exactly this perspective's question, raised but then not staffed by any of the five lenses.

---

##### 5. Overall grade + top fixes

**Grade: B+.**

A genuinely strong, current, well-sourced briefing: the diagnosis is correct, the SLowRL catch is a real and valuable de-risk, and the COMMIT→DE-RISK call is the right instinct. It loses the A-range on three things: a load-bearing citation the card itself doesn't carry, a Skeptic-tilted synthesis that under-weights the "still open as stated" reading, and a safety claim (zero-violation) stated in a form no safety reviewer would accept, with no formal-certificate perspective to repair it.

**Top fixes, in priority order:**

1. **Ingest SLowRL `2603.17092` into KH and cite it in the E1 card.** It is the single closest prior work and the card is currently blind to it. Until the card itself names SLowRL and pins down whether its filter is single-task or cross-domain, the downgrade rests on a paper the direction doc cannot see. (This is the most important fix.)
2. **Sharpen the metric, not just flag it.** Replace "zero violations" everywhere in the card (thesis, targets, H1/H3 falsifiers) with a CP-bounded violation-*rate* and state the episode count K and the power needed to distinguish it from a target rate. Right now the headline guarantee is statistically un-bounded.
3. **Resolve the H1 over-claim explicitly in the card.** State that SLowRL covers *broad H1* (filter-shield, single task) and that the *defended* H1 is narrower (PCRPO+EWC, expected-cost, across N domains) — don't let the briefing's nuance live only in the review while the card still says "arbitrary Cluster-D engine."
4. **Demote "arbitrary engine" to "latent-conditioned engines with a shared cost interface"** (already recommended; it is untested and probably false across a vision-WM vs proprioceptive-extrinsics interface).
5. **Recruit the formal-certificate angle.** Decide whether the contribution is an *empirical* H3 frontier curve or a *certified* successive-domain bound; `2601.21845` and `2201.08355` make the latter plausible and far more defensible at a top venue.

### E2 (Direction review)

Topic: a hard reachability shield holds the 0%-collision guarantee over a *changing* (online-adapting) policy mid-adaptation, at bounded SR cost, *plus* an uncertainty-aware occupancy model closes residual-gap hazards a fixed-geometry filter misses (verbatim bet: "A reachability shield holds the 0%-collision guarantee over a *changing* policy mid-adaptation ... at a bounded SR cost during the adaptation window ... *and* an uncertainty-aware occupancy model closes the residual-gap hazards a fixed-geometry filter misses").

#### Prompt 1 — Five Perspectives

**1. PRACTITIONER (builds/deploys policies daily).**
- *Core position*: The static-policy half is real and already shippable: RAIL (2409.19190) runs 0.42 s/plan with 0% collisions on a real Franka, and Path-Consistent Safety Filter / PACS (2511.06385) runs a safety step in 0.20 ms while preserving SR (79% vs 80% unprotected, real HRI). The bet's novel half — shielding a policy that adapts mid-deployment — collides with deploy-time reality: online adaptation (Learning-on-the-Fly 2508.21065 needs differentiable-sim BPTT; AdaWorldPolicy 2602.20057 runs no-reward updates) is itself slow and jittery, and the shield must re-verify occupancy every time the action distribution shifts.
- *Strongest evidence*: RAIL 0.42 s/plan, PACS 0.20 ms safety step, 14% faster execution from chunk-feasible trajectories. Learning-on-the-Fly only shows 3 adaptation steps = 4.5 s wall-clock on a *quadrotor*, not a manipulator — the adaptation engines the bet rides on were never demonstrated on the E2 hardware class.
- *Only-this-perspective*: The real cost is not collisions, it is the *interaction* between two slow loops — the per-step occupancy re-check budget vs. the per-step policy-update budget. Nobody has measured whether a shield can keep up with a policy whose Jacobian is changing under it; that scheduling question, not the safety theorem, is what will kill the demo.

**2. ACADEMIC (knows the peer-reviewed literature).**
- *Core position*: The hard-vs-soft argument is settled (the card concedes this: PACS +68% SR over soft CBF, RAIL 0% vs 5–35%). The genuinely open theoretical object is whether a reachability *guarantee* even type-checks when the shielded policy is non-stationary — but the literature already has a direct answer the card under-weights.
- *Strongest evidence*: Safe Deep Policy Adaptation / SafeDPA (2310.08602, CMU) explicitly claims to be the *first* framework jointly solving policy adaptation + safe RL with a theoretical safety guarantee — a CBF-QP with a robust margin ε over an RMA-style adapting policy, sim-to-real fine-tuned, 100% safety on real hardware (RC car / pendulum / Safety-Gym). UNISafe (2505.00779) already supplies the uncertainty-aware-occupancy half (epistemic-uncertainty HJ reachability, Jenga 0.08 vs 0.82 failure). refineCBF (2602.23478, Herbert lab — same HJ lineage as RAIL) re-converges a *safe value function* online in <2 s with SAFEADAPT-REFINECBF's *monotonic* safety guarantee.
- *Only-this-perspective*: The card frames "changing policy" and "uncertain geometry" as two open cells, but the literature couples them via the robust-margin ε: SafeDPA's safety theorem already holds *because* the margin absorbs the adaptation-induced model error. The novel contribution must be that *continuous-time reachability* (not CBF-QP) needs a different, weaker margin — that is a sharper, narrower claim than the card makes.

**3. SKEPTIC (thinks it is wrong or already solved).**
- *Core position*: The "shield a changing policy" whitespace is mostly an artifact of how the card slices the field. From the shield's viewpoint the policy is a black box emitting action chunks; whether those chunks come from a frozen net or an adapting one is irrelevant to a *per-chunk* reachability check — PACS already re-verifies each chunk, so a changing policy is just a new chunk it re-checks. The "guarantee assumes a fixed policy" framing is a strawman: RAIL/PACS never assumed policy stationarity, only that the *robot dynamics* (forward occupancy) are known.
- *Strongest evidence*: PACS's whole point is OOD-action robustness via path-consistent braking — it handles actions "unseen in training", which is precisely what an adapting policy produces. SafeDPA (2310.08602) shields an adapting policy to 100% safety today. The card's own H4 is marked "confirmatory — PACS won it"; the live H1 differs from H4 only by swapping a static for an adapting policy, which the per-chunk filter does not distinguish.
- *Conveniently ignored*: The card never explains *why* a per-chunk reachability check would fail under adaptation if the dynamics model is unchanged. If occupancy is verified each chunk, the falsifier ("collisions appear while the policy adapts") only fires if adaptation corrupts the *dynamics/occupancy model itself* — which is the uncertain-geometry half, not a distinct changing-policy phenomenon. The two halves may be one problem double-counted.

**4. ECONOMIST (follows money/incentives).**
- *Core position*: Safety-filter papers are cheap to publish and hard to falsify (0% collisions is a clean headline), which is why the cell is crowded (RAIL, PACS, UNISafe, AnySafe, refineCBF, SafeDPA, Ensemble Safety Filters, OSC-CBF all within ~18 months). The marginal publishable unit has shifted from "hard beats soft" (saturated) to "+ adapting policy / + uncertain geometry" — exactly where the card points — which means the bet is chasing a frontier the whole community is already pricing in.
- *Strongest evidence*: The Related table itself lists 13+ safety mechanisms differing by one cell each (constraint-set adaptation = AnySafe, value-fn refinement = refineCBF, conformal switching = Ensemble 2511.07899, manifold projection = 2503.07404). RoboEval (2507.00435) and VLA-Arena CBDDL exist *because* vendors need a safety-cost-vs-SR leaderboard — benchmark supply precedes the bet.
- *Only-this-perspective*: The defensible asset is not the theorem but the *benchmark*: there is no public manipulator collision-vs-SR-under-online-adaptation suite. Whoever defines "safe-success during the adaptation window" as a metric on RoboEval owns the citation surface, regardless of which filter wins. Commit to the measurement protocol, not the algorithm.

**5. HISTORIAN (has seen prior waves).**
- *Core position*: This is the safe-RL / shielding wave repeating the model-based-RL pattern: a clean guarantee (HJ reachability, à la 1990s viability theory → Tomlin → Herbert) meets the messy reality that the guarantee is only as good as the model, and each cycle re-discovers that "online adaptation breaks the offline certificate". The "shield a learning policy" question is the safe-exploration problem from constrained-RL (CPO, Lagrangian, ISSA) in new clothes.
- *Strongest evidence*: SafeDPA (2310.08602) literally positions itself against hierarchical safe-RL (Safety Layer, ISSA) "breaking down with modest dynamics changes" — the same failure mode the bet names. refineCBF revives warm-started HJ-reachability, echoing the pixel-vs-latent and offline-vs-online value-function cycles.
- *Only-this-perspective*: Every prior wave resolved this not by a better shield but by *budgeting the certificate's validity window* — the historian's bet is that the winning framing is "how stale can the occupancy certificate be before the guarantee voids", a quantity refineCBF's <2 s re-convergence already operationalizes. The novelty, if any, is a staleness bound, not a new filter.

#### Prompt 2 — Contradiction Map

1. **Direct clashes.**
   - *Skeptic vs. Academic/card on whether the cell is open*: Skeptic says a per-chunk reachability check is policy-agnostic, so "changing policy" is not a distinct hazard (PACS re-verifies each chunk); Academic + card say a non-stationary policy voids the static certificate and SafeDPA only partially covers it (CBF-QP, not continuous-time, non-manipulator). The specific claim in tension: does adaptation introduce a hazard *beyond* per-chunk OOD actions, or not?
   - *Practitioner vs. Economist on what to build*: Practitioner says the binding constraint is the two-loop scheduling/latency budget (a systems result); Economist says the asset is the benchmark/metric, and the algorithm is fungible. They disagree on where the publishable contribution lives.
   - *Academic vs. card on the two halves*: Academic argues the changing-policy and uncertain-geometry halves collapse into one via the robust margin ε (SafeDPA already couples them); the card treats them as two separable open cells (H1 vs. the second clause).

2. **Strongest vs. weakest.**
   - *Strongest*: ACADEMIC — it is the only perspective that names the specific pre-empting paper (SafeDPA 2310.08602) with its exact claim ("first to jointly tackle adaptation + safe RL with guarantees") and correctly identifies the surviving narrow wedge (continuous-time reachability + manipulator + no-reward online updates).
   - *Weakest*: HISTORIAN — insightful on the staleness-window reframing but evidentially thin; its "every wave resolved this by budgeting the certificate" is an analogy, not a falsifiable claim about E2's specific hardware/policy class.

3. **Resolving question.** *On a real manipulator, does running a per-chunk continuous-time reachability filter over a no-reward online-adapting policy (Learning-on-the-Fly / AdaWorldPolicy) produce any collision that the same filter over the frozen base policy does not — holding the occupancy model fixed?* If no, the Skeptic is right and H1 buys nothing beyond H4; if yes, the residual hazard is real and isolates whether it comes from the policy or the geometry.

4. **Universal agreement (likely true).** The *static-policy* hard-vs-soft result is settled and not itself novel: every perspective treats RAIL's 0% (vs 5–35%) and PACS's +68%-over-CBF as confirmatory floor, not contribution. A publication cannot rest on re-proving hard-beats-soft.

5. **Blind spot (no perspective addressed).** Sensing/state-estimation under adaptation: every perspective debates the *policy* and the *occupancy model* but assumes the robot's *own state and the obstacle geometry are observed accurately* during the adaptation window. In real sim-to-real deployment the residual gap corrupts perception (latency, mis-registered point clouds, moving humans in HRI) — the certificate can void from a perception error that neither the changing-policy nor the uncertain-geometry clause names.

#### Prompt 3 — Synthesis Briefing

**CEO summary.** E2's static-policy thesis (a hard reachability shield beats a soft penalty, 0% collisions, preserved SR) is *won and conceded* — the card is honest that RAIL and PACS settle it. The live bet is narrower: hold the 0%-collision guarantee over a policy that is *adapting* mid-deployment, plus uncertainty-aware occupancy. A literature check shows this is partially pre-empted: SafeDPA (2310.08602) already shields an RMA-style *adapting* policy to a theoretical guarantee with 100% real-hardware safety, and UNISafe (2505.00779) plus refineCBF (2602.23478) own the uncertain-geometry half. What survives is a genuinely narrow, defensible wedge — *continuous-time set-based reachability* (RAIL/PACS lineage, not CBF-QP) over a *no-reward online-gradient* adapting policy (not RMA latent-extrinsics) on the *manipulator collision-vs-SR frontier* (not RC-car/pendulum) — but the card over-states it as open whitespace and under-cites the nearest threat. The contribution most likely to stick is the *benchmark + metric* (safe-success during the adaptation window on RoboEval), not a new filter.

**5 key findings, ranked by reliability.**
1. *The static hard-vs-soft result is settled, not a contribution.* (Supported: all five; Challenged: none.) RAIL 0%/0.42 s, PACS +68%/0.20 ms — the card itself marks H4 "confirmatory."
2. *The changing-policy half is partially pre-empted by SafeDPA (2310.08602).* (Supported: Academic, Skeptic, Historian; Challenged: card, which omits SafeDPA from the Related table.) SafeDPA = adapting policy + CBF safety filter + guarantee + real hardware, 100% safety, +300% over RMA-P.
3. *The two open halves (changing policy, uncertain geometry) may collapse into one via the robust margin / shared occupancy model.* (Supported: Academic, Skeptic; Challenged: Practitioner, who sees them as separately hard.) UNISafe couples uncertainty into the value function; the falsifier only fires if adaptation corrupts occupancy.
4. *The binding engineering constraint is two-loop latency/scheduling, never measured on a manipulator.* (Supported: Practitioner, Historian; Challenged: Skeptic, who thinks per-chunk re-check is free.) Learning-on-the-Fly's 3-step/4.5 s adaptation was shown on a quadrotor, not the E2 arm.
5. *The durable asset is the benchmark/metric, not the algorithm.* (Supported: Economist, Practitioner; Challenged: Academic, who values the narrowed theorem.) No public manipulator safe-success-under-adaptation suite exists; RoboEval + VLA-Arena CBDDL are the substrate to define it on.

**Hidden cross-perspective connection.** The Historian's "certificate-staleness window" and the Practitioner's "two-loop scheduling budget" are the *same quantity* seen from theory vs. systems: how stale the occupancy/value certificate may be before the guarantee voids equals how far the policy/geometry can drift between re-verifications. refineCBF's <2 s re-convergence and SAFEADAPT-REFINECBF's monotonic guarantee already give the theory side a handle; the systems side is unmeasured. Unifying them — a *staleness bound* tying re-verification rate to adaptation rate — is the real novel object hiding under "shield a changing policy."

**Actionable insight for the researcher: DE-RISK (do not commit as framed; do not drop).** The bet as written ("shield an adapting policy") is partially pre-empted by SafeDPA and risks a reviewer rejecting it as RAIL+SafeDPA. De-risk by (a) re-scoping the claim to the surviving wedge — *continuous-time set-based reachability over a no-reward online-gradient adapting policy on the manipulator collision-vs-SR frontier* — and (b) re-centering the contribution on a **staleness bound + benchmark**, not a new filter. Concrete next experiment (also the Prompt-2 resolving question): on a real Franka with RoboEval Safety/Stability metrics, run the *same* PACS/RAIL filter and *same* occupancy model over (i) the frozen base diffusion policy and (ii) a Learning-on-the-Fly / AdaWorldPolicy online-adapting version, and report collisions + safe-success + SR-cost *as a function of the filter's re-verification rate vs. the policy's adaptation rate*. If (ii) shows zero excess collisions at the base re-verification rate, the changing-policy clause is dead (cite SafeDPA, pivot to the staleness bound); if collisions appear, you have isolated the staleness threshold — a publishable systems+theory result SafeDPA does not have.

**Frontier question.** What is the tight relationship between a policy's online adaptation rate and the minimum re-verification frequency a reachability certificate needs to stay valid — i.e., is there a provable staleness bound (a "safe adaptation speed limit") below which the 0%-collision guarantee survives any online policy update, and does it depend on the *policy's* drift or only on the *occupancy model's*? Answering it would convert E2 from a crowded filter-comparison into the one result that tells the field how fast a robot may safely learn on the job.

---

#### Prompt 4 — Peer Review (independent)

*Reviewer stance: skeptical robotics PI. Grounding: read Sim2Real.md E2 (L1116–1191), verified SafeDPA 2310.08602 against the arXiv PDF / RI-CMU page, confirmed vault state for all anchor IDs.*

**Verification done first (so the scores mean something).**
- SafeDPA (2310.08602) is **absent from the vault** (no KH note; not in E2's 19-row Related table) — present only in the sibling analyst part. The briefing's characterization is **accurate**: CBF-based safety filter on top of an RMA-style adaptive RL policy (adaptation module fits the environment encoder from state-action history = textbook RMA), theoretical guarantee, real hardware, +300% safety rate, evaluated on Inverted Pendulum / Safety-Gym / **RC Car — not a manipulator**. The three "surviving wedge" distinctions (continuous-time set-based reachability ≠ CBF-QP; manipulator collision-vs-SR ≠ RC-car/pendulum; no-reward online-gradient ≠ RMA latent-extrinsics) all **hold against the real paper**. The pre-emption is therefore *real but genuinely partial*, exactly as claimed.
- 2505.00779 = **UNISafe** (CMU, Bajcsy) — uncertainty-aware latent HJ reachability + OOD detection. The card already uses it for the uncertain-geometry half (rows L1143, L1152–1153; FP bet L1133–1134). Briefing's use is faithful.
- refineCBF (2602.23478) **is in the card** — full Related row (L1153) *and* a Risks bullet (L1190), explicitly flagged "adapts environment/dynamics, **not a changing policy**… ground vehicles + quadcopters, not a manipulator." So the briefing's blanket "the card under-cites the nearest threat" is **half-wrong**: it under-cites *SafeDPA* (true), but refineCBF is among the best-cited rows in the table.

---

**1. CONFIDENCE SCORES (1–10 reliability).**

1. *Static hard-vs-soft is settled, not a contribution* — **9/10.** Directly verifiable in-card: H4 is labelled "confirmatory — PACS won it" (L1176), RAIL 0%/0.42s and PACS +68%-over-CBF are quoted (L1127–1128). Unanimous across perspectives, no counter-evidence. The only reason it is not 10 is that "settled" elides RAIL's ~10-pp SR cost, which is itself a live tradeoff E2 could still contribute on.
2. *Changing-policy half partially pre-empted by SafeDPA* — **8/10.** SafeDPA's claim independently verified against the PDF; it genuinely shields an adapting policy to a guarantee on real hardware. Docked 2 because "partially" is doing heavy lifting and the briefing states it more softly than the analyst part: SafeDPA's adapting policy is RMA latent-extrinsics + model-free RL, *not* the no-reward online-gradient diffusion/VLA policy class (Learning-on-the-Fly / AdaWorldPolicy) E2 targets — the hazard mechanism (gradient updates shifting the action distribution within a chunk) is arguably distinct from RMA's bounded extrinsics estimate. Pre-emption of the *framing* is strong; pre-emption of the *specific hazard* is unproven.
3. *The two halves collapse into one via the robust margin / shared occupancy* — **5/10.** This is the most speculative finding and the briefing flags it (Practitioner challenges). It is an *argument by analogy to SafeDPA's ε-margin*, not a result. The card deliberately separates H1 (changing policy) from the uncertain-geometry clause, and the Skeptic's own point cuts the other way: if a per-chunk reachability check is policy-agnostic, adaptation may introduce *no* hazard beyond per-chunk OOD — in which case the halves don't "collapse," one of them *vanishes*. The finding asserts collapse where the evidence equally supports vanish; conflating those two is a real flaw.
4. *Binding constraint is two-loop latency/scheduling, never measured on a manipulator* — **6/10.** The "never measured on an E2 arm" sub-claim is well-supported (Learning-on-the-Fly's 3-step/4.5s was a quadrotor result; RAIL reports 0.42s/plan but for a *static* filter). But "the *binding* constraint" is an unestablished engineering bet — it could equally be occupancy-model fidelity (B1) or the SR-cost floor. Asserting which constraint binds before any two-loop experiment exists is over-confident; this is a hypothesis dressed as a finding.
5. *Durable asset is the benchmark/metric, not the algorithm* — **6/10.** "No public manipulator safe-success-under-adaptation suite exists" is plausible and consistent with my vault check, and RoboEval/VLA-Arena-CBDDL are real substrates. But "durable asset" is a *strategic prediction*, not a verified fact, and Academic challenges it for good reason: a benchmark with no accompanying staleness-bound theorem is a leaderboard, not a result — reviewers reward the theorem, the benchmark is the vehicle. The finding inverts what actually carries the paper.

**Mean = 6.8. Min = 5.**

---

**2. WEAKEST LINK.** Finding 3 (halves collapse via the robust margin, 5/10). It rests entirely on an analogy to SafeDPA's ε-margin and never confronts the Skeptic's rival reading (a per-chunk filter re-verifies every chunk, so a *slowly* adapting policy injects no hazard the static filter doesn't already catch). To verify, you need exactly one experiment the briefing itself proposes but mis-frames: run the *fixed* PACS/RAIL filter over an online-adapting Learning-on-the-Fly policy on a real Franka and measure whether excess collisions appear *at the base re-verification rate*. If zero → the changing-policy clause is dead (halves don't collapse, H1 vanishes; cite SafeDPA, pivot to staleness). If nonzero → you have isolated a real adaptation-induced hazard and *can* ask whether the margin absorbs it. Until that single number exists, Finding 3 is a coin-flip dressed as a synthesis.

---

**3. BIAS CHECK — Academic over-dominated.** The synthesis is organized around the Academic's single move: name the pre-empting paper (SafeDPA), declare the wedge narrow-but-defensible, recommend "re-scope + cite the threat." Findings 2 and 3, the CEO summary's spine, and the de-risk action all trace to that one perspective; the analyst part itself rates Academic "strongest." The Skeptic is invoked but its sharpest claim (adaptation may be a non-hazard for a per-chunk filter) is **softened into agreement** with the Academic rather than allowed to contradict it — that contradiction is the most decision-relevant tension in the whole card and it gets buried. The Economist and Historian appear mainly as supporting cast for Findings 4–5. Net: **Academic-dominant, Skeptic under-weighted at the exact point it matters most.**

---

**4. MISSING PERSPECTIVE — the Control-Theory Reviewer (formal-guarantee skeptic).** Every perspective here treats "0%-collision guarantee" as a binary asset to be transported onto a moving policy. None interrogates *what the guarantee is actually conditioned on.* RAIL/PACS-style continuous-time reachability assumes (a) a bounded, known forward-occupancy / dynamics model and (b) the backup planner remains feasible. A no-reward online-gradient policy that shifts the action distribution does **not** by itself void a per-chunk reachability certificate — the certificate is over *occupancy*, not over the policy. So the genuinely hard question the briefing never asks is: *does online adaptation change the dynamics/disturbance bound the HJ value function was computed against, or only the nominal action the filter then overrides?* If only the latter, H1 is provably a non-problem and the entire "staleness bound" frontier collapses — there is nothing to go stale, because the certificate was never a function of the policy. This sixth angle could **invert the conclusion**: the durable object may not be a staleness bound at all, but a precise statement of which adaptation operators preserve the reachability certificate's preconditions (and the answer may be "all of them that don't touch the dynamics model"). The briefing's central novel object ("safe adaptation speed limit") may be answering a question that, on closer formal inspection, doesn't bind.

---

**5. OVERALL GRADE — B+.**

A strong, honest, well-grounded brief: it correctly identifies that the headline is conceded, finds a real un-cited threat (SafeDPA) that the card genuinely missed, and proposes one decisive falsifying experiment. That is real peer-review value and most of it survives scrutiny. It falls short of A-territory for three reasons.

Top fixes, in order:
1. **Resolve the collapse-vs-vanish ambiguity before recommending anything** (Finding 3 / the Skeptic tension). The single Franka experiment the briefing already names settles it; run it *first*, because if H1 vanishes, the whole "staleness bound" frontier is moot and the de-risk advice changes from "re-scope the wedge" to "drop the changing-policy clause entirely and keep the benchmark." The briefing hedges instead of forcing this.
2. **Interrogate the guarantee's preconditions** (the missing Control-Theory perspective). State explicitly whether adaptation touches the dynamics/occupancy model the certificate is computed against. This is the difference between a publishable theorem and a category error.
3. **Calibrate the over-claims in Findings 4–5.** "The *binding* constraint is latency" and "the *durable* asset is the benchmark" are strategic bets stated as findings; mark them as hypotheses, and correct the record that refineCBF is *already well-cited* in the card (only SafeDPA is missing), so the "under-cites the nearest threat" line doesn't overstate the card's sins.

Net: the de-risk verdict (don't commit as framed, don't drop) is correct; the reasoning is ~70% solid and 30% Academic-perspective momentum that a sharper Skeptic / control-theorist read would have arrested.

### E3 (Direction review)

Topic: the First-principles bet of E3 — Conformal Runtime Failure Detection — verbatim: "(i) A detect-then-act loop wiring the conformal flag to RAIL's backup or SCDA's safe-adapt **preserves the FPR guarantee** while cutting user-facing failures (the bound holds *through* the action, not just at the flag). (ii) SAFE's internal-feature detector **does not transfer across architectures**, and pairing it with FAIL-Detect's policy-agnostic density score recovers detection on the architectures the internal features miss."

#### Prompt 1 — Five Perspectives

**1. PRACTITIONER (builds/deploys policies daily).**
- *Core position*: The settled half (success-only conformal detection) is already deployable — FAIL-Detect's ~78% sim / ~72% real and SAFE's <1 ms zero-shot overhead are exactly what a deploy engineer wants. The genuinely useful, un-shipped thing is the *act* half: a flag that triggers a bounded recovery without a fleet-wide false-alarm storm, which is where real-robot cost lives.
- *Strongest evidence*: SAFE <1 ms / FPR guarantee; PATCH (2606.16690) already runs the closest thing to a deployed loop — FPR 0.04 static / 0.07 transient at TPR 0.97, 90% resume accuracy, 55.2 Hz on a real robot — but with *no conformal guarantee*. SafeContract in 2605.28726 adds <13 μs/step and shows enforcement with *no task degradation* (Fisher p=0.92/0.76/0.68 across 3 architectures).
- *Only-this-perspective*: The bound is a paper artifact; what kills a deployment is recalibration drift. FAIL-Detect's own risk (calibration set of successes must match the deploy distribution) means every sim2real shift silently invalidates the FPR guarantee the bet rests on — and nobody re-runs conformal calibration on a live fleet. The "preserve the bound through the action" theorem is moot if the *base* calibration is already stale at deploy.

**2. ACADEMIC (knows the peer-reviewed literature).**
- *Core position*: The literature backs the card's split: detection-from-successes is settled (Sentinel CoRL'24, FIPER NeurIPS'25, SAFE NeurIPS'25), and the open piece is bound-preservation-through-the-action. The formal machinery for H3 *already exists in control theory* (2606.15366 ARCP + closed-loop-sensitivity distribution-shift budget), which both helps and threatens the bet.
- *Strongest evidence*: 2606.15366 proves coverage/safety survive *through* iterative policy updates (100% coverage inverted pendulum; 99.8% score-coverage / 100% safety maze) — the exact distribution-shift H3 isolates, where Fallback-Safe MPC (2309.08603) only asserts the FPR bound *at the monitor*. FIPER (2510.09459) does the success-only bet near-verbatim (0.78 overall acc, AND-combination of RND-OE + ACE killing isolated-anomaly false alarms).
- *Only-this-perspective*: The card's own falsifier is the live academic risk: "if 2606.15366's distribution-shift budget already closes H3 *without* E3's success-only detector, the preservation is a control-theoretic fact, not a detection one." The peer-reviewed answer is that the hard part (bound-through-the-loop) is a *CBF/CLF* result, and stapling a success-only OOD detector onto it may be a system-integration paper, not a new theorem.

**3. SKEPTIC (thinks the bet is wrong or already solved).**
- *Core position*: H4 is the weakest claim and is being overtaken in real time. The card frames "internal features may not transfer across architectures" as untested whitespace, but 2605.28726 (May 2026) already *empirically establishes* architecture-specific failure signatures and prescribes architecture-matched monitors — the premise of H4 is no longer a hypothesis.
- *Strongest evidence*: 2605.28726: reversal-rate is a universal predictor (AUROC 0.93/0.79/0.91 across VQ-BeT/Diffusion/ACT) while jerk follows a discrete→continuous gradient (0.88→0.69→0.41) — i.e. *some* signals transfer and others don't, exactly the H4 finding, delivered black-box with no model access. TDQC (2604.20472) already shows a calibrated success-signal transferring across OpenVLA/UniVLA/π₀/π₀-FAST with a logit-only black-box variant — the policy-agnostic case H4 claims SAFE leaves open.
- *Only-this-perspective*: The whitespace is shrinking from both ends. H4's "negative result + density backstop" is half-pre-empted (2605.28726 proves the negative; TDQC proves a backstop transfers), and H3's positive result may be a corollary of 2606.15366. What's left that is *uniquely E3* is narrow: SAFE's *internal-feature* (hidden-state, not action-space) transfer specifically + a *conformal* (not UQ/CUSUM) loop. That is a real but thin wedge.

**4. ECONOMIST (follows money/incentives).**
- *Core position*: The success-only framing is industry-shaped: TRI authored FAIL-Detect and co-authored SAFE, because labeled-failure data is the expensive thing a robotics company cannot collect at fleet scale — "detect from successes" *is* the cost-minimizing corporate narrative. The act-half is under-published because closing the loop requires owning the *whole* stack (policy + shield + recovery), which only vertically-integrated labs can run.
- *Strongest evidence*: The provenance pattern — FAIL-Detect (TRI/Woven), SAFE (TRI/Vector/UofT), Sirius-Runtime's human-gated intervention (+40% real SR, +16% human-effort efficiency, 2310.17552) — all monetize *reducing human teleop cost*. The EU AI Act Article 9 continuous-monitoring mandate (cited in 2605.28726) by 2027 is a regulatory tailwind making *certified* FPR bounds a compliance asset, not just a metric.
- *Only-this-perspective*: The grant/citation incentive favors the *flag*, not the *act* — flagging is benchmarkable on LIBERO-Anomaly / RoboFAC / BotFails offline, while the act-half needs real-robot closed-loop eval that doesn't fit a leaderboard. That is *why* the whitespace exists, and also why it's risky: a closed-loop bound-preservation result is hard to benchmark, so it converts poorly into citations even if true.

**5. HISTORIAN (has seen prior waves).**
- *Core position*: This is the runtime-monitoring/anomaly-detection wave re-running the AV/aerospace playbook one domain late. Fallback-Safe MPC (2309.08603, 2023) and the System-Level OOD survey (2212.14020, 2022) are the prior generation; the manipulation field is now re-deriving "failure = system-level OOD inside the closed loop" that AVs settled with reachability + conformal monitors years ago.
- *Strongest evidence*: The pre-robotics lineage in the card itself — p-Quantile Anomaly Detector (2207.12380, AUROC 0.946, FPR held to 3.4% at 5% target, AVs) and System-Level Anomaly Detector (2309.13475, 90.3–97.4% recall, shrinks the Backward-Reachable-Tube) — are the *exact* conformal-FPR + system-level-OOD framing now rediscovered for VLAs. The detect-then-act-with-fixed-fallback pattern is old; only the *learned-policy distribution-shift* twist (2606.15366) is genuinely new.
- *Only-this-perspective*: Every prior wave of "close the loop with a monitor" converged on the same lesson the card under-weights — the *fixed fallback* (Fallback-Safe MPC's safe-recovery-set) is what carries the guarantee, and the moment you make the recovery a *learned* adaptive act (SCDA/RAIL), you reopen the distribution-shift problem you claimed to close. The historical pattern is that bound-preservation survives only with a *non-learned* fallback; E3's bet to keep the bound through a *learned* recovery is the part history says is hardest.

#### Prompt 2 — Contradiction Map

**(1) Direct clashes.**
- *Skeptic vs Academic/Card on H4 novelty*: Skeptic says 2605.28726 + TDQC (2604.20472) already establish "architectures fail differently + a black-box signal transfers," pre-empting H4's premise. Academic/card counter: those are *action-space / logit black-box* monitors; H4's specific claim is about SAFE's *internal hidden-state features* transferring (or not) — a different signal class 2605.28726 explicitly lists as *untested* for OpenVLA/π₀ ("unverified for autoregressive and flow-matching").
- *Academic vs Card on H3*: Academic argues 2606.15366's distribution-shift budget may *already* close bound-preservation-through-the-loop as a control-theoretic theorem, making E3's success-only detector contribution incremental. Card concedes this is its own falsifier but bets the *detection* angle (success-only, policy-agnostic) is a distinct contribution.
- *Historian vs Card on the act-half*: Historian says the bound only provably survives a *fixed* fallback; card bets it survives a *learned* SCDA/RAIL recovery. Direct contradiction on whether the central H3 result is even achievable with the proposed act.

**(2) Strongest vs weakest perspective.**
- *Strongest*: ACADEMIC — every claim is anchored to a cited, dated, peer-reviewed result (NeurIPS'25 FIPER/SAFE, 2606.15366's theorem) and it correctly identifies the card's true load-bearing risk (H3 may be a control fact, not a detection one) rather than a strawman.
- *Weakest*: ECONOMIST — directionally right on incentives (TRI provenance, EU AI Act) but its claims are structural/sociological, not falsifiable against the card's experiments; it explains *why* the gap exists without telling the researcher whether the bet *works*.

**(3) The one question that resolves the biggest contradiction.**
Does SAFE's *internal hidden-state* failure detector (not action-space, not logit-black-box) retain its ROC-AUC when the trained probe is transferred zero-shot from one VLA backbone (e.g. OpenVLA) to a structurally different one (e.g. π₀ flow-matching), and does a FAIL-Detect/RC-NF density score recover the lost AUROC on the backbones where it collapses? This single transfer experiment resolves the Skeptic-vs-Academic clash: if internal features transfer, H4 dies and 2605.28726 already owns the space; if they collapse *and* the density backstop recovers them, H4 is the real, un-pre-empted contribution.

**(4) What every perspective agrees on (likely true).**
Success-only conformal detection is settled and reproducible — FIPER/Sentinel/SAFE/FAIL-Detect are not in dispute by any perspective. H1 (confirmatory) will reproduce; the field genuinely has dropped failure-labeled detection. Detection *alone* is no longer a publishable contribution; the value, if any, is in the act-half or cross-architecture transfer.

**(5) The blind spot (addressed by no perspective).**
The *benign-vs-critical* distinction as the real driver of user-facing failure reduction. The card's H3 measures "FPR + user-facing failure rate," but no perspective interrogates whether a *bounded FPR* even correlates with *fewer user-harmful failures* — a detector can hold its FPR bound while flagging only recoverable hesitations and missing the irreversible ones. The card's own rows (p-Quantile 2207.12380, Failure-ID-Filtering 2604.13788's 23% TNR-loss-prevention, PATCH's corridor restriction) hint that *task-relevance/criticality weighting*, not the FPR bound, is what makes a closed loop reduce harm — and the bet never tests that the preserved bound buys *safety* rather than just *statistical validity*.

#### Prompt 3 — Synthesis Briefing

**(1) CEO summary.** E3 correctly concedes that success-only conformal failure detection is settled and bets instead on two structural openings: (H3) preserving the conformal FPR bound *through* a closed detect-then-act loop, and (H4) showing internal-feature detectors fail to transfer across architectures with a policy-agnostic density backstop that recovers them. The bet is real but narrowing: H3's hardest piece (bound-survival under policy-induced distribution shift) is now formally addressed by a control-theoretic result the card itself cites (2606.15366), risking that E3's detection contribution is a corollary; H4's *premise* (architectures fail differently) was empirically established weeks ago by 2605.28726 and TDQC, leaving E3's defensible wedge as the specific, still-untested transfer of SAFE's *internal hidden-state* features (not action-space) paired with a conformal density backstop. This is a de-risk-then-commit, not a clean commit: the surviving whitespace is genuine but thin, and the most valuable un-asked question is whether a preserved FPR bound actually reduces *user-harmful* (irreversible) failures rather than just statistically-valid ones.

**(2) Five key findings, ranked by reliability.**
1. *Success-only conformal detection is settled and will reproduce (H1).* Supported by: all 5 perspectives; FIPER 0.78 acc, FAIL-Detect 78%/72%, SAFE <1 ms. Challenged by: none. Highest reliability — and therefore not itself a contribution.
2. *H4's premise (architectures fail differently) is already empirically established, partially pre-empting the framing.* Supported by: Skeptic, Historian; 2605.28726 (AUROC 0.93/0.79/0.91 reversal-rate; jerk 0.88→0.41 gradient), TDQC across OpenVLA/UniVLA/π₀/π₀-FAST. Challenged by: Academic/Card (those are action-space/logit, not internal hidden-state; OpenVLA/π₀ internal-feature transfer explicitly untested). High reliability that the *premise* is pre-empted; the *specific internal-feature wedge* survives.
3. *H3's bound-preservation-through-the-loop may be a control-theoretic fact rather than a detection contribution.* Supported by: Academic, Historian; 2606.15366 proves coverage/safety survive iterative policy updates (100%/99.8%). Challenged by: Practitioner (the integration + success-only detector is the novel system). Medium-high reliability as a *risk*.
4. *The act-half (closing the loop) is the genuine unshipped value, but is benchmark-hostile.* Supported by: Practitioner, Economist; PATCH 0.96 bACC / 55.2 Hz but no conformal bound, EU AI Act tailwind. Challenged by: Historian (learned recovery reopens the shift the bound assumes closed). Medium reliability.
5. *A preserved FPR bound may not buy user-harm reduction (criticality blind spot).* Supported by: implied by p-Quantile 2207.12380, Failure-ID-Filtering 2604.13788; addressed by no perspective explicitly. Speculative but high-leverage — the untested assumption beneath H3's metric.

**(3) Hidden cross-perspective connection.** The Practitioner's "recalibration drift invalidates the base bound" and the Historian's "learned recovery reopens distribution shift" are the *same failure mode* viewed from two ends: both say the conformal guarantee is only valid on the calibration distribution, and *anything* that moves the deploy distribution — sim2real shift (Practitioner) or the recovery action itself (Historian) — breaks it. This is precisely the gap 2606.15366's closed-loop-sensitivity distribution-shift budget targets, which means the Academic's threat-paper is also the *solution* to both the Practitioner's and Historian's objections — the single citation that simultaneously threatens E3's novelty and rescues its feasibility.

**(4) Actionable insight for the researcher: DE-RISK, then commit to a narrowed bet.** Do not commit to the full two-pronged bet as written. Run one decisive experiment first: take SAFE's internal-hidden-state probe trained on one VLA backbone and measure zero-shot transfer ROC-AUC to π₀ and OpenVLA, with FAIL-Detect/RC-NF density as the backstop arm. Two outcomes: (a) if internal features transfer, drop H4 and pivot the paper entirely onto H3's act-half-with-conformal-bound, explicitly differentiating from 2606.15366 by making the *success-only detector* (not a CBF/CLF) the calibration source the bound survives through; (b) if internal features collapse and the density backstop recovers AUROC, H4 is the un-pre-empted core — publish the internal-vs-black-box transfer distinction that 2605.28726 left open for hidden-state signals. In *both* branches, add the criticality test (blind spot): report not just FPR but user-harmful-failure reduction, separating recoverable from irreversible failures, to prove the preserved bound buys safety not just validity.

**(5) Frontier question that would change everything.** Can a single conformal guarantee be made *invariant to the recovery action it triggers* — i.e. a detect-then-act loop whose FPR bound is certified jointly over the policy *and* its learned recovery, so the act cannot break the bound by construction (not by re-calibration)? If 2606.15366's adversarially-robust, closed-loop-sensitivity machinery can be generalized from CBF/CLF fixed structure to an arbitrary *learned* SCDA/RAIL recovery, the entire detect-then-act field collapses into one certified primitive and E3's H3 becomes the canonical result; if it provably cannot (history's lesson), then runtime detection is permanently a *flag-and-fixed-fallback* tool and the learned-recovery dream is the wrong bet.
