| 4 | The act-half (closing the loop) is the genuine unshipped value but benchmark-hostile | **7/10** | Strong: PATCH (2606.16690, FPR 0.04/0.07 at TPR 0.97, 55.2 Hz, no conformal bound) verified line 1233; System-Level OOD survey (2212.14020) explicitly names the missing closed-loop-OOD benchmark (line 1239). "Benchmark-hostile" is correct and honest. EU AI Act tailwind is plausible but is an Economist assertion, not a robotics-evidence claim. |
| 5 | A preserved FPR bound may not buy user-harm reduction (criticality blind spot) | **8/10** | The most valuable finding and the best-calibrated one: grounded in two in-card rows (p-Quantile 2207.12380 AUROC 0.946 verified line 1229; Failure-ID-Filtering 2604.13788 benign-vs-critical line 1232). Honestly labeled "speculative but high-leverage." It is the genuinely *un-asked* question and would change how the bet is evaluated. Capped at 8 because no perspective operationalized a metric for "user-harmful." |

**Mean = 7.2. Min = 5.**

---

##### 2. Weakest link

**Finding #2** is the weakest link, and the specific fault is provenance, not logic. The claim "H4's premise was empirically established weeks ago by 2605.28726" rests on a paper that **exists in neither the E3 card nor KnowledgeHub** (confirmed: the only vault file containing `2605.28726` is the upstream analyst part `020-E3-1-analyst.md`). Its three load-bearing numbers — reversal-rate AUROC 0.93/0.79/0.91 across VQ-BeT/Diffusion/ACT, and the jerk 0.88→0.69→0.41 discrete→continuous gradient — are quoted with false precision but cannot be checked. The briefing also propagated the analyst's "EU AI Act Article 9 by 2027" tailwind from the same uncited paper.

*Info needed to verify:* (a) ingest 2605.28726 into KnowledgeHub and confirm it actually reports those AUROC/jerk figures on those three architectures; (b) confirm it tests *internal-feature* signals (the briefing's own counter is that it does not — it tests reversal-rate/jerk, which are trajectory-/action-space, so even if real it does **not** pre-empt the surviving internal-hidden-state wedge). Until (a) is done, finding #2 should be downgraded from "high reliability" to "premise plausibly pre-empted by an in-vault paper TDQC; the strong-form pre-emption is unverified." The honest version of the finding is *weaker* than written but points to the *same* surviving wedge, so the actionable insight (Prompt 4 below) does not change.

---

##### 3. Bias check — which perspective over-dominated

**The Skeptic dominated; the Practitioner was the only effective counterweight.** Findings #2 and #3 — the two "E3 is being pre-empted" claims — are both Skeptic/Historian-sourced, and they set the entire CEO-summary frame ("real but narrowing," "de-risk then commit," "thin wedge"). The synthesis is structurally a demolition-then-salvage. The Academic/Card view is present but consistently cast as the *defendant* ("Challenged by Academic/Card...") rather than as an independent source of positive evidence. The Economist appears once (EU AI Act) and the resulting finding #4 leans on it.

This is over-rotation toward novelty-threat. A balanced synthesis would have given equal airtime to the *positive* in-card asset the Skeptic frame buries: E3 is the only card that assembles a 28-row detector taxonomy on a single clean axis (what-the-detector-learns-from × what-it-outputs) and correctly separates the four cited "closest prior" rows (Fallback-Safe MPC asserts-at-monitor, 2606.15366 through-the-loop, PATCH localizes-without-conformal, TDQC transfers-with-failure-labels). That curation *is* a contribution and the briefing never credits it. Net: the dominant perspective is **Skeptic**, and it pushed the grade of the bet down further than the in-vault evidence warrants.

---

##### 4. Missing perspective — a 6th angle that would change conclusions

**The Statistician / conformal-theory referee.** Every finding treats "preserves the FPR bound through the loop" as a binary the engineering can achieve. A conformal-prediction theorist would ask the question that dissolves H3: *exchangeability is destroyed the instant the detector's flag changes the next action.* The detect-then-act loop is a feedback system where the calibration distribution and the test distribution are no longer exchangeable by construction — so the relevant machinery is not "re-calibrate on shift" (the card's risk-mitigation at line 1269) but *adaptive / online conformal* (ACI-style coverage that is guaranteed only asymptotically and only marginally, never the finite-sample per-step FPR the card advertises). This sixth angle changes two conclusions: (1) it makes finding #3 sharper than the briefing — 2606.15366's "distribution-shift budget" may be the *only* honest route, strengthening the corollary worry rather than leaving it a hedge; and (2) it reframes finding #5 — once you accept coverage is only asymptotic/marginal under the loop, "is a preserved bound even meaningful?" stops being a criticality side-question and becomes the central validity question. A conformal referee would also flag that the card never states *which* coverage notion (marginal vs conditional, split vs full, finite-sample vs asymptotic) H3 promises — the single most reviewer-fatal omission, and one no current perspective raised.

---

##### 5. Overall grade and top fixes

**Grade: B+.**

The briefing is intellectually honest about the central tension (it leads with "the bet is narrowing" rather than hiding it), it is metrically faithful wherever it draws on in-card numbers (PATCH, FAIL-Detect, SAFE, p-Quantile all verified against lines 1217-1233), it correctly identifies the surviving wedge (internal-hidden-state transfer + conformal loop), and finding #5 is a genuinely valuable un-asked question. The de-risk-then-commit actionable insight is the right call for a thin-whitespace bet. What holds it below A-: one load-bearing finding rides an un-vaulted, uncheckable paper; the Skeptic frame is not balanced against the card's real curation asset; and the deepest reviewer objection (conformal validity under closed-loop non-exchangeability) is absent.

**Top fixes, in priority order:**
1. **Vault 2605.28726 or stop citing its numbers.** Either ingest it into KnowledgeHub and verify the AUROC 0.93/0.79/0.91 / jerk-gradient figures, or downgrade finding #2 to "premise pre-empted in-vault by TDQC (action/logit only); strong-form pre-emption unverified." Right now a B+ briefing is propagating false-precision numbers from a paper no one in the vault can check.
2. **State the coverage notion H3 promises.** Marginal vs conditional, finite-sample vs asymptotic, split vs adaptive conformal. Without this, "preserves the FPR bound" is unfalsifiable, and the corollary/2606.15366 risk cannot actually be adjudicated.
3. **Credit the card's curation as a positive contribution,** not just survey it — the 28-row single-axis taxonomy that cleanly separates the four "closest prior" failure modes is itself defensible novelty the Skeptic frame erased.
4. **Operationalize finding #5 before it can carry the paper.** Define "user-harmful" concretely (irreversible vs recoverable, with a per-failure cost) and name the dataset that lets you measure harm-reduction, not just FPR — otherwise the highest-leverage finding stays a slogan.
5. **Run the one decisive transfer experiment first** (SAFE internal-probe zero-shot to π₀/OpenVLA + FAIL-Detect/RC-NF backstop) exactly as the actionable insight proposes — it is the cheapest way to collapse the H3-vs-H4 branch and is the right de-risking move.
